268 — Kubernetes-like Container Orchestrator

Project Number: 268

Project Name: Kubernetes-like Container Orchestrator

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

Goal: Build, from scratch in Rust + Tokio, a Kubernetes-like container orchestrator: a declarative versioned REST API of resource objects (orc-api), a from-scratch MVCC key-value store with revisions / gap-free watch / leases / optimistic concurrency (orc-store), an apiserver (orc-apiserver), a level-triggered controller framework + core controllers (orc-controller), a filter/score scheduler (orc-scheduler), a node agent (orc-kubelet) driving an OCI/runc-like container runtime (orc-runtime), and Service networking (orc-net), reachable via the orctl CLI. It is the orchestration core — not a full Kubernetes API clone, CNI/CSI ecosystem, cloud integration, or service mesh; the store is single-node with a Raft HA hook (cross-link #42).

1. Goal

Goal: ship orc — a from-scratch, Kubernetes-modeled container orchestrator in Rust + Tokio: a declarative versioned REST API over a built-from-scratch MVCC store, driven to convergence by level-triggered controllers, a filter/score scheduler, and a per-node agent that runs real OCI containers — the orchestration core, not a cloud product.

1.1 Precise engineering definition

orc is a distributed control plane + node agent that turns declarative desired state (resource objects) into observed actual state (running containers) via idempotent reconcile loops. Six load-bearing subsystems:

# Subsystem Crate / binary One-line contract
1 Declarative REST API orc-api, orc-apiserver Versioned resource objects (Pod, ReplicaSet, Deployment, Service, Node, Namespace, ConfigMap, Secret, Event, EndpointSlice) with labels/selectors, ListOptions, and gap-free WATCH keyed on resourceVersion.
2 MVCC key-value store orc-store etcd-like: monotonic Revision, range/put/delete, Txn compare-and-set, ordered gap-free Watch, Lease TTL keys, compaction. Single durable source of truth.
3 Level-triggered controllers orc-controller Reflector -> DeltaFIFO -> SharedIndexInformer -> WorkQueue -> Reconciler; reconcile from CURRENT spec+cache, never from events. Idempotent, convergent.
4 Filter/score scheduler orc-scheduler Unscheduled Pod -> FilterPlugins (feasible nodes) -> ScorePlugins (0..100 weighted) -> argmax -> exclusive Binding (writes pod.spec.node_name).
5 Node agent orc-kubelet Watch Pods where spec.node_name == self; PodWorker lifecycle SM; sole writer of pod.status; Node registration + Lease heartbeat; liveness/readiness/startup probes.
6 OCI runtime + Service net orc-runtime, orc-net ContainerRuntime (OCI image pull/unpack, runc-like exec via Linux namespaces + cgroups v2 + mounts + pivot_root); ServiceProxy (ClusterIP VIP -> EndpointSlice), NetworkPlugin (pod net), DNS.

Client surface: the orctl CLI + typed client library (apply, get, describe, delete, scale, rollout, logs, exec, watch).

1.2 System boundaries

Three boundaries from the architecture diagram, each load-bearing:

  • Trust boundary — clients + node agents are untrusted to orc-apiserver (authn/authz at the edge); container images + workloads are untrusted to the node (namespace/cgroup isolation).
  • Concurrency boundary — every component is an independent async actor reconciling via watch; orc-store serializes all writes by Revision (the single global logical clock).
  • Persistence boundary — orc-store is the only durable state. Every other component is a stateless, restartable, cache-rebuilding consumer of its watch stream.

In scope: the declarative API + store + controllers + scheduler + node agent + runtime + L4 Service dataplane. Out of scope: everything in §1.3.

1.3 Non-goals (explicit)

Non-goal Boundary / what we DO provide instead
Full Kubernetes API clone A faithful core object model only. NO full apimachinery surface, NO API aggregation.
CRD / operator framework (Phase 12) A single Custom-resource hook only; full CRDs deferred → PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §23").
Full RBAC matrix (Phase 12) Authn/authz hooks at the apiserver; a minimal Role/RoleBinding/ClusterRole lands in Phase 12 (§23), not a complete matrix.
Production CNI/CSI ecosystem A reference NetworkPlugin (CNI-like) + hostPath/emptyDir/projected volumes; CSI is a stubbed interface only.
Cloud-provider integration No cloud LB / node autoprovision / cloud volumes. NodePort + reference CNI only.
Service mesh / Ingress controller L4 Service dataplane only (ClusterIP, NodePort). No L7 routing, no sidecar mesh.
Distributed etcd orc-store is single-node; HA is a deferred Raft replication hook cross-linking 42 Raft Consensus (multi-apiserver-over-Raft, Phase 13 §24).
Multi-tenant security product Namespaces + minimal RBAC + ResourceQuota only; not a hardened tenancy plane.

Deferred features are gated behind a typed PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §X.Y") marker so they fail loudly, not silently:

Deferred feature Gate
CRDs / operators PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §23")
Full RBAC matrix PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §23")
StatefulSet / DaemonSet / Job / CronJob PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §16")
Horizontal Pod Autoscaler (HPA) PhaseDeferred → see 269 Autoscaler
CSI volume ecosystem PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §22")
Ingress / L7 PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §21")
Raft-backed HA store PhaseDeferred("FINAL_BLUEPRINT §24") → cross-link 42 Raft Consensus

1.4 Deployment model

Mode Binaries Use
All-in-one dev single dev binary embeds orc-apiserver + orc-store + orc-scheduler + orc-controller + orc-kubelet in one process laptop iteration; the Phase 1 smoke test target
Distributed orc-apiserver (+ embedded orc-store, single-node) · orc-scheduler · orc-controller as control-plane processes; one orc-kubelet per machine realistic multi-node clusters
Client orctl (CLI) from anywhere over REST + WATCH 
control plane host:  orc-apiserver(+orc-store) · orc-scheduler · orc-controller
node host (×N):      orc-kubelet → orc-runtime (CRI) · orc-net (CNI + ServiceProxy + DNS)
operator laptop:     orctl ──REST+WATCH(resourceVersion)──▶ orc-apiserver

1.5 Target users

  • Platform engineers learning orchestration internals — informer/watch/reconcile, MVCC + optimistic concurrency, filter/score scheduling, node-agent + OCI runtime — by reading and extending a working core.
  • SREs wanting a legible, debuggable control plane: single durable store, single-writer status fields, level-triggered convergence (no hidden edge state), explicit failure modes.
  • Not: end users running production prod workloads, multi-tenant SaaS operators.

1.6 Phase 1 deliverable — the nine orc-* crates

A Cargo workspace (edition 2021, MSRV 1.78) with nine members + CI, ending in the round-trip smoke test (POST a Pod to orc-apiserver → persist in orc-store → GET it back, object + resourceVersion intact):

  1. orc-api — resource object model + apimachinery: TypeMeta, ObjectMeta, the typed kinds, LabelSelector + label/field selector matching, JSON codec, resourceVersion semantics, ListOptions/WatchEvent, OwnerReference, Condition. (std)
  2. orc-store — MVCC KV store: WAL-backed keyspace, monotonic Revision, range/get/put/delete, Txn (CAS), gap-free ordered Watch, Lease (TTL), compaction; Raft hook deferred. Owns Store, Revision, KeyValue, WatchEvent, Lease, Txn. (std + tokio)
  3. orc-apiserver (binary) — REST handlers, generic Registry over orc-store, validation + Admission, WATCH endpoint (resourceVersion + bookmarks), optimistic concurrency (409 on RV conflict), namespaces, authn/authz hooks. (tokio)
  4. orc-controller (binary) — reconciliation framework + built-in controllers (ReplicaSet, Deployment, Node-lifecycle, EndpointSlice, Namespace, GarbageCollector). Owns Informer, SharedIndexInformer, Lister, WorkQueue, Reconciler, RateLimiter.
  5. orc-scheduler (binary) — scheduling framework: FilterPlugin + ScorePlugin + Bind, SchedulingQueue, optional preemption. Owns SchedulingContext, FilterPlugin, ScorePlugin, Binding.
  6. orc-kubelet (binary) — Pod lifecycle SM (PodWorker), status reporting, Node registration + Lease heartbeat, probes, volume/config injection. Owns PodWorker, PodSyncResult, ProbeManager, VolumeManager, NodeStatusReporter.
  7. orc-runtime — ContainerRuntime trait + CRI-like shim: OCI image pull/unpack, runc-like exec (namespaces, cgroups v2, mounts, pivot_root), sandbox creation, restart/backoff. Owns ContainerRuntime, OciSpec, Sandbox, ImageStore, CgroupHandle. (Linux-real; stub elsewhere)
  8. orc-net — Service networking: kube-proxy-like ServiceProxy (ClusterIP VIP → EndpointSlice), cluster DNS, CNI-like NetworkPlugin. Owns ServiceProxy, NetworkPlugin, DnsServer.
  9. orctl (binary) — CLI + typed client library; talks to orc-apiserver over REST/watch.

1.7 What later phases unlock

Phases Capability unlocked
1–3 (§12–14) Durable MVCC store; full object model; apiserver CRUD + WATCH + optimistic concurrency — a usable declarative API.
4–5 (§15–16) Informer framework + ReplicaSet/GC/Node-lifecycle — desired state is now enforced.
6–7 (§17–18) Scheduler binds Pods; kubelet reports status + heartbeats — Pods reach Running.
8 (§19) Real OCI containers run under namespaces + cgroups v2 — the system actually executes workloads.
9–10 (§20–21) Rolling-update Deployments; Service VIP dataplane + DNS — production-shaped workloads + service discovery.
11–12 (§22–23) ConfigMaps/Secrets/volumes; ServiceAccounts + minimal RBAC + ResourceQuota — config + security baseline.
13–14 (§24–25) Leader election, drain/eviction + PodDisruptionBudget, Raft-backed HA hook; conformance/chaos/fuzz/bench — the "Done" gate.

1.8 Timeline (estimate)

Scope Duration
Phase 1 (workspace + nine crate shells + smoke test) 6–14 weeks
Full blueprint (Phases 1–14) many months of focused work

1.9 Recommended language — Rust 2021, MSRV 1.78, Tokio

Requirement Why Rust + Tokio
Syscall-heavy node hot path Memory safety on the kubelet/runtime path (namespaces, cgroups v2, mounts, pivot_root) — a use-after-free or torn write here is a node-down incident; no GC pauses on the tail-latency path.
Thousands of concurrent watch streams + reconcile loops Tokio async fan-out; fearless concurrency without data races; the apiserver, watch fan-out, and controller workqueues are all async actors.
Resource phases + watch events Sum types + exhaustive match for PodPhase, WatchEvent, Reconcile, PodSyncResult — illegal states unrepresentable; the compiler enforces handling every variant.
Typed store + object API Strong typing on Store, Object, resourceVersion↔Revision mapping; a torn write or RV bug is a split-brain risk.

Rejected: Go (Kubernetes' language; GC pauses hurt tail latency under watch fan-out, nil-interface + data-race classes are exactly what a control plane must avoid, weaker sum types for the object/phase model); C/C++ (memory-unsafe on the untrusted-object + syscall hot path); Python (no static guarantees, GIL). Optional references for the Rust path: the kube-rs client + k3s architecture.

1.10 Required prerequisites

  • Distributed systems + consensus basics — quorum, log replication, split-brain (for the §12 Raft hook → 42).
  • Watch / informer pattern — Reflector → DeltaFIFO → SharedIndexInformer → WorkQueue → Reconciler; level-triggered reconciliation.
  • MVCC + optimistic concurrency — revisions, compare-and-set, gap-free watch, compaction.
  • Linux isolation primitives — namespaces (pid/net/mnt/ipc/uts/user), cgroups v2, OCI runtime + image specs, pivot_root.
  • Async Rust / Tokio — tasks, select!, channels, cancellation, backpressure.
  • Scheduling theory — predicates/priorities, resource fit, spread/affinity, bin-packing trade-offs.

2. Why Build This

2.1 The thesis in one line

A container orchestrator is the canonical instance of the dominant distributed-systems architecture of the 2010s–2020s: a fleet of independent control loops reconciling declared intent against observed reality over a single consistent, watchable store. Building one from scratch — API + MVCC store + informers + controllers + scheduler + node agent + runtime — forces mastery of every layer that pattern rests on. Owning it permanently raises distributed-systems judgement; you stop reading "eventually consistent" as a hedge and start reasoning about convergence, idempotency, and write-skew with precision.

2.2 Historical + architectural arc

Era Unit of compute Failure model Coordination
~2000 one server, hand-managed pet dies → page shell scripts, runbooks
~2004 Google Borg the cluster task dies → reschedule central master + declarative job spec
2014→ Kubernetes (Borg's open lineage) the cluster, declared any component dies → loop re-converges apiserver + etcd + control loops
2015+ everything-as-control-loop desired/observed state of anything reconcile to spec the same pattern, generalized (operators, GitOps, IaC)
  • Borg made the cluster the unit of compute — you declare jobs, not place processes. Kubernetes open-sourced that lineage and, critically, the architecture: not "a scheduler" but "a consistent store + N controllers watching it."
  • The durable lesson is the pattern, not the product: "control loop over a consistent store" now underlies operators, GitOps (Argo/Flux), IaC engines (267), service meshes, autoscalers (269), and progressive-delivery systems (274). Learn it once here, recognize it everywhere.

2.3 Unique systems knowledge this teaches

Concept What you internalize Where in this project
Declarative, level-triggered reconciliation act on current spec vs observed state, never on individual events; missed event is harmless because next resync re-reads full state Reconciler::reconcile, all of orc-controller
Idempotent reconcilers reconcile may run any number of times with the same result; convergence ≠ event-handling ReplicaSet/Deployment controllers
Informer / watch / workqueue pattern Reflector → DeltaFIFO → SharedIndexInformer → rate-limited WorkQueue → reconcile-from-cache (not from event) orc-controller framework (Phase 4)
MVCC + monotonic Revision every write gets a global logical timestamp; reads/watches anchor to a revision orc-store (Phase 1)
Optimistic concurrency / CAS no locks; update carries the resourceVersion it read; store Txn compare-and-set; mismatch → 409, re-read + retry orc-apiserver write path
Gap-free, ordered watch streams from rev R, every change with rev > R in strict order, no gaps, no dups, + bookmarks; compacted R → 410 Gone, relist orc-store watch + orc-apiserver WATCH
Scheduling framework Filter (feasibility) → Score (0..100, weighted) → exclusive Bind orc-scheduler (Phase 6)
Linux container isolation primitives namespaces, cgroups v2 limits, mounts, pivot_root, OCI image unpack orc-runtime (Phase 8)
Single-writer-per-field status only the node's kubelet writes pod.status; scheduler/controllers write spec → eliminates status races by construction orc-kubelet (Phase 7)
Lease-based liveness kubelet renews a Node Lease (TTL); missed lease → NotReady → eviction; failure detection as data, not RPC orc-store Lease + Node-lifecycle controller
Ownership + cascading GC OwnerReferences + finalizers drive cascading delete and adoption/orphan GarbageCollector (Phase 5)

2.4 Why these are hard-won lessons, not trivia

  • Level- vs edge-triggered is the single most misunderstood idea in the field. Most engineers reach for "on event X, do Y." This project forbids it: edge-triggered logic loses correctness on a dropped/duplicated event; level-triggered logic cannot, because it re-derives the world. Building it makes the distinction muscle memory.
  • Optimistic concurrency teaches you to reason about write-skew and lost updates without mutexes — the only approach that scales to thousands of concurrent writers against one store.
  • Gap-free watch is deceptively deep: the entire correctness of every informer cache in the cluster rests on the store never dropping or reordering a change between two revisions. Implementing it (and the 410-Gone relist fallback) teaches why caches are safe to trust.
  • Single-writer-per-field is a design discipline, not a feature: it shows how to make a multi-writer system race-free by partitioning which actor owns which field rather than by locking.

2.5 Engineering muscles built

  • Designing idempotent APIs and reconcilers; thinking in fixed points, not transitions.
  • Reasoning about eventual consistency + convergence: proving a loop terminates at the desired state and that concurrent loops compose.
  • OS-level isolation: namespaces/cgroups/mounts on the syscall-heavy hot path — where a memory bug is a node-down incident (the Rust rationale).
  • Async actor concurrency at scale: thousands of watch streams + reconcile loops fanning out from one store (Tokio), with bounded memory and tail latency.
  • Failure-as-data: modeling liveness, drain, and eviction as state transitions over leases rather than as imperative RPCs.
  • Schema + versioning discipline: apiVersion, ObjectMeta, spec/status separation, resourceVersion semantics.

2.6 Portfolio cross-links

Project Relationship to 268
42 — Raft Consensus the store's HA backbone: orc-store's deferred replication hook (Phase 13) makes the single-node MVCC store a multi-node consistent log; HA control plane = multiple orc-apiservers over a Raft-backed store
267 — IaC Engine the declarative cousin: same desired-vs-observed reconciliation, applied to cloud infra instead of containers; validates that the pattern generalizes
269 — Autoscaler sits on top: watches metrics + Deployments, writes spec.replicas — a controller that consumes 268's API
273 — OCI Image Builder produces the OCI images orc-runtime pulls/unpacks; closes the build→ship→run loop
274 — Rollout / Canary progressive delivery layered on the Deployment controller (Phase 9); same control-loop pattern, richer strategy
275 — Chaos Engineering exercises the resilience claims: kill nodes/pods/apiserver, partition the store (Phase 14 chaos suite)
304 — SWIM Membership an alternative/complement to lease-based node health: gossip failure detection feeding the Node-lifecycle controller
container-runtime / namespaces project the isolation primitives orc-runtime depends on (Phase 8)
etcd-like store project the MVCC + watch + lease lineage orc-store is modeled on (Phase 1)

2.7 Non-goals (so the value is honest)

This is the orchestration core, not a Kubernetes clone. Explicitly out: full CRD/operator framework (a Custom-resource hook only), full RBAC matrix initially, a production CNI/CSI ecosystem (reference CNI + hostPath/emptyDir only), cloud-provider integration, a service mesh / Ingress (Service L4 dataplane only), a distributed etcd (single-node store + Raft hook to 42), and a multi-tenant security product. The learning value is in the core loop, deliberately not diluted by surface-area cloning.

2.8 Calibration value

The payoff is transferable judgement. After building 268 you can read any modern infra system — a mesh control plane, a GitOps engine, an autoscaler, a cloud API — and immediately locate its store, its watch mechanism, its reconcilers, its consistency model, and its failure detector. You will know which guarantees are load-bearing (gap-free watch, CAS, single-writer status) and which are conveniences. That ability to see the skeleton under any distributed control system is the permanent return on this project.

2.9 What Mastery Looks Like

After completing this project, each competency below is demonstrable — not read-about:

  • Phase 1 (orc-store): You can design and implement a from-scratch MVCC key-value store with a monotonic global clock, explain exactly why gap-free watch correctness requires serialized Revision assignment, and walk through a WAL crash-recovery scenario without notes.
  • Phase 3 (orc-apiserver): You can implement optimistic concurrency using compare-and-set with no distributed locks, explain why last-write-wins silently corrupts concurrent controller updates, and trace a 409 Conflict through the request pipeline to the retry path.
  • Phase 4 (orc-controller): You can build a level-triggered reconciliation framework from first principles — Reflector, DeltaFIFO, SharedIndexInformer, WorkQueue, RateLimiter — and explain why a single dropped watch event is harmless while a single missed lock in an edge-triggered system is not.
  • Phase 6 (orc-scheduler): You can implement a pluggable filter/score scheduling framework, reason about the performance ceiling at O(nodes × plugins), and apply the percentageOfNodesToScore optimization without sacrificing correctness of the exclusive Bind.
  • Phase 8 (orc-runtime): You can write the exact Linux syscall sequence (clone flags, cgroup enrollment, mount propagation, pivot_root preconditions) to start a container in an isolated namespace+cgroup set, and debug any one of the six common failure modes (EINVAL from pivot_root, EBUSY from cgroup internal-process rule, user-ns gid_map ordering) without reference documentation.
  • Phase 13 (leader election + Raft hook): You can implement lease-based leader election using only the store's own CAS primitives, explain why optimistic concurrency makes split-brain survivable even when election fails, and design the HA seam between single-node and Raft-replicated stores without changing the controller or scheduler contract.
  • Phase 14 (conformance + chaos): You can write property tests that machine-check the gap-freedom, idempotence, and exclusive-binding invariants across thousands of randomized operation sequences, and instrument a chaos harness that verifies eventual convergence rather than relying on timing.

3. Architectural Overview

The orchestrator is nine orc-* crates compiling to five binaries (orc-apiserver, orc-scheduler, orc-controller, orc-kubelet, orctl) plus library crates (orc-api, orc-store, orc-runtime, orc-net). All coordination is indirect: components never RPC each other; they read/write objects in orc-store through orc-apiserver and converge via watch. The store is the only synchronization point.

              orctl / clients  ── REST + WATCH (resourceVersion) ──┐
                                                                   v
    ┌──────────────────────────── CONTROL PLANE ────────────────────────────┐
    |   orc-apiserver : REST · Registry · Admission · Watch · optimistic CC  |
    |        |  read/watch + CAS write (resourceVersion <-> store Revision)  |
    |        v                                                               |
    |   orc-store : MVCC KV · Revision · Watch · Lease · Txn · compaction     |
    |        ^                          ^                       ^             |
    |        | watch+write              | watch+bind            | watch+write |
    |   orc-controller             orc-scheduler           (status writers)   |
    |   (Reflector->DeltaFIFO->Informer->WorkQueue->Reconciler:               |
    |    ReplicaSet·Deployment·Node·EndpointSlice·Namespace·GC)               |
    └────────────────────────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────┘
                          watch bound Pods | (Pod.spec.node_name == self)
                                           v
    ┌──────────────────────── NODE (one per machine) ────────────────────────┐
    |  orc-kubelet : PodWorker · status report · Node Lease heartbeat · probes|
    |        |  CRI                          |  CNI                            |
    |        v                               v                                 |
    |  orc-runtime : OCI image · runc-like   orc-net : ServiceProxy (VIP->EP) ·|
    |  exec (namespaces·cgroups v2·mounts)   NetworkPlugin (pod net) · DNS     |
    └─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
    TRUST BOUNDARY: clients + node agents are untrusted to the apiserver (authn/authz);
    container images + workloads are untrusted to the node (isolation). CONCURRENCY BOUNDARY:
    every component is an independent async actor reconciling via watch; the store serializes
    writes by Revision. PERSISTENCE BOUNDARY: orc-store is the single durable source of truth.

3.1 Boundaries (the three lines that govern everything)

Boundary Where Rule Enforced by
TRUST clients/nodes ↔ apiserver every request authn'd + authz'd; no client is implicitly privileged apiserver authn chain (cert/token) + RBAC (Phase 12)
TRUST images/workloads ↔ node container code is hostile; isolate via namespaces + cgroups v2 + pivot_root orc-runtime (Phase 8)
CONCURRENCY every component each is an independent async actor; no shared mutable state across components; coordinate only via watch + CAS store Revision serializes all writes (Phase 1)
PERSISTENCE orc-store single durable source of truth; component caches are derived + disposable, rebuilt by relist WAL + Revision (Phase 1)

Implication: any component may crash/restart at any time and recover by relisting from the store at its last-known resourceVersion — no component holds authoritative unpersisted state. Caches are soft; the store is hard.

3.2 orc-apiserver (control-plane front door)

Responsibilities: the only writer to orc-store; exposes REST CRUD + list + WATCH over versioned resource kinds via a generic Registry; runs validation + Admission; enforces namespaces; maps object resourceVersion ↔ store Revision. In: REST/WATCH from orctl, controllers, scheduler, kubelets. Out: JSON objects, watch streams, Revision-stamped writes into the store. Invariant: optimistic concurrency — every update carries the resourceVersion it read; the apiserver issues a store Txn compare-and-set; mismatch → 409 Conflict; no locks, conflicts resolved by client re-read + retry. Stateless and horizontally scalable (all state in the store). Built Phase 3 (§14); authn/authz/admission deepened Phase 12 (§23); multi-apiserver HA Phase 13 (§24).

3.3 orc-store (MVCC KV — single durable truth)

Responsibilities: a WAL-backed keyspace with a global monotonic Revision; range/put/delete, Txn (compare-and-set), gap-free ordered Watch from a revision, Lease (TTL keys for node heartbeats), and compact. In: Op/Compare/Txn calls from the apiserver; lease keepalives from kubelets (via apiserver). Out: KeyValue ranges, WatchEvent streams (Put/Delete/Bookmark), granted Revisions. Invariant: the store serializes all writes by Revision — every mutation bumps a single global counter, and a Watch from R streams every change with Revision > R strictly in order, with no gaps and no duplicates (compacted R → 410 Gone, forcing relist). This gap-freedom is the foundation of every downstream cache. Single-node now; Raft replication is a deferred hook cross-linking project 42. Built Phase 1 (§12).

3.4 orc-controller (controller-manager + informer pipeline)

Responsibilities: the reconciliation framework Reflector → DeltaFIFO → SharedIndexInformer → WorkQueue → Reconciler plus built-in controllers (ReplicaSet, Deployment, Node-lifecycle, EndpointSlice, Namespace, GarbageCollector). The Reflector list+watches a resource into a DeltaFIFO; the SharedIndexInformer applies deltas to a thread-safe indexed cache (the Lister) and fans changes to handlers that enqueue object keys into a rate-limited, de-duplicated WorkQueue; the Reconciler dequeues a key and reconciles from the cache, not from the event. One informer per type is shared across controllers. In: watch streams + reads from the apiserver. Out: CAS writes (spec/child objects) back through the apiserver. Invariant: reconcile is level-triggered + idempotent — it reads current desired (spec) + observed (cache status) and drives toward desired; a missed event is harmless because the next resync/watch re-reads full state; edge-triggered logic is forbidden. Ownership/GC honors OwnerReference + finalizers (cascading delete). Framework Phase 4 (§15); core controllers Phase 5 (§16); Deployment Phase 9 (§20); EndpointSlice Phase 10 (§21); leader election Phase 13 (§24).

3.5 orc-scheduler (filter + score + bind)

Responsibilities: watch unscheduled Pods (spec.node_name == None) via a SchedulingQueue; for each, run every FilterPlugin (resource fit, nodeSelector, taints/tolerations, affinity/anti-affinity) to get feasible Nodes, then each ScorePlugin (least/most-requested, spread; 0..100, weighted-summed), pick the max-scoring Node, and Bind. In: Pod + Node watch streams from the apiserver. Out: a Binding — a single CAS write of pod.spec.node_name. Invariant: binding is exclusive — a Pod binds exactly once; the write is a compare-and-set on the unbound state, so a duplicate bind loses on resourceVersion and is rejected 409. Stateless beyond an in-memory node cache; on restart it relists and re-derives the queue. Built Phase 6 (§17); preemption optional.

3.6 orc-kubelet (per-Node agent)

Responsibilities: register the Node + renew its Lease heartbeat; watch Pods bound to this Node (spec.node_name == self); run the PodWorker lifecycle state machine (PodSyncResult: Synced/Backoff/Terminated); report pod.status; run liveness/readiness/startup probes (ProbeManager); inject volumes/config (VolumeManager). In: bound-Pod watch from the apiserver; container state from orc-runtime; network from orc-net. Out: pod.status writes + Node status/Lease updates through the apiserver; CRI calls to orc-runtime; CNI calls to orc-net. Invariant: the kubelet is the sole writer of pod.status for its own Pods (scheduler/controllers write spec only) — single-writer-per-field eliminates status races. Node liveness is lease-based: a missed lease → Node-lifecycle controller marks the Node NotReady and evicts Pods after grace. Built Phase 7 (§18).

3.7 orc-runtime (CRI-like container runtime)

Responsibilities: the ContainerRuntime trait — OCI image pull_image/unpack into an ImageStore, pod sandbox creation (run_sandbox), runc-like container exec (create_container/start_container/stop_container) using Linux namespaces, cgroups v2 limits (CgroupHandle), mounts, and pivot_root; container status; restart policy + crash backoff. In: CRI calls from the kubelet (PodSandboxConfig, ContainerConfig). Out: SandboxId/ContainerId/ImageId, ContainerStatus. Invariant: workload isolation — every container runs in its own namespace + cgroup set with a pivoted root, and resource limits are hard-enforced by the kernel; the container is untrusted to the host. Linux-real; a stub runtime on other OSes for dev. Built Phase 8 (§19).

3.8 orc-net (Service networking + pod network)

Responsibilities: the ServiceProxy dataplane programming Service ClusterIP VIP → EndpointSlice healthy backends (ClusterIP/NodePort, via iptables/eBPF-like or userspace proxy); cluster DnsServer; the CNI-like NetworkPlugin (setup_pod_network/teardown_pod_network) assigning each Pod a PodIp. In: Service + EndpointSlice watch streams; sandbox handles from the kubelet. Out: programmed VIP→backend rules; a PodIp per sandbox; DNS records. Invariant: VIP → endpoint indirection — clients address a stable Service VIP; the proxy load-balances only to healthy EndpointSlice backends, which the EndpointSlice controller keeps in sync with Pod readiness (level-triggered). Built Phase 10 (§21).

3.9 orc-api (object model + apimachinery)

Responsibilities: the shared resource vocabulary every component depends on — TypeMeta/ObjectMeta, the Object trait, the typed kinds (Pod, ReplicaSet, Deployment, Service, Node, Namespace, ConfigMap, Secret, Event, EndpointSlice), LabelSelector + label/field selector matching, the JSON codec, resourceVersion semantics, ListOptions/WatchEvent, OwnerReference, Condition. In/Out: pure library — serializes/deserializes objects and evaluates selectors; holds no state. Invariant: spec vs status separation + a stable wire contract — spec is desired (written by clients/controllers), status is observed (written by the owning component); the JSON codec round-trips losslessly so a round-tripped object equals the persisted one. Built Phase 2 (§13); shells in Phase 1 (§12).

3.10 orctl (CLI + typed client)

Responsibilities: the operator interface + typed client library — apply, get, describe, delete, scale, rollout, logs, exec, watch; talks to orc-apiserver over REST/WATCH. In: user commands + YAML manifests. Out: REST writes (declarative apply) + read/watch displays. Invariant: declarative apply — the client submits desired spec; it never imperatively mutates cluster internals, so the same manifest applied twice converges to the same state. Built incrementally from Phase 1 (§12) onward as resources land.

3.11 End-to-end flow (a Deployment to running containers)

Step Actor Action Store effect
1 orctl apply POST Deployment put → Deployment@Rev, status Pending
2 Deployment ctrl observe Deployment, create ReplicaSet (ownerRef) put ReplicaSet
3 ReplicaSet ctrl observe RS, N replicas missing → create N Pods (ownerRef) put Pods, spec.node_name=None
4 orc-scheduler watch unscheduled Pod → Filter+Score+Bind CAS put spec.node_name=node-X
5 orc-kubelet (X) watch bound Pod → PodWorker → pull image, run sandbox, start containers CAS put pod.status Running
6 orc-net Pod Ready → EndpointSlice updated → ServiceProxy programs VIP→Pod put EndpointSlice

Every arrow is a watch + CAS through the apiserver; no component calls another directly. Failure at any step is recovered by the next reconcile reading current state — the whole pipeline is a chain of independent, idempotent, level-triggered loops sharing one durable, Revision-ordered store.

4. System Constraints

4.1 Quantified Targets

Constraint Phase 1 Target Design Ceiling (Raft HA, deferred) Rationale / Mechanism
Cluster scale — nodes 100 1,000 Node Lease fan-out + watch cache bound the apiserver heap
Cluster scale — pods 5,000 50,000 scheduler queue + kubelet PodWorker count per node
Cluster scale — total objects 30,000 ~300,000 all kinds in orc-store keyspace; bounds apiserver cache
apiserver read throughput > 5,000 req/s scales w/ replicas served from watch cache; store range only on cache-miss/relist
apiserver write throughput > 500 req/s quorum-bound every write = store Txn CAS; serialized by global Revision
watch fan-out > 2,000 concurrent watchers > 10,000 one broadcast per Revision; per-watcher bounded ring buffer
WATCH propagation latency < 100 ms p99 (commit→watcher) < 150 ms p99 store Watch ordered, gap-free; no per-event store re-read
store write latency WAL fsync p99 < 10 ms quorum p99 < 30 ms single-node WAL append+fsync; Revision global-serialized
scheduling bind rate > 100 pods/s > 100 pods/s one Filter+Score+Bind cycle per pod; Bind = single CAS write
per-pod schedule decision p99 < 50 ms @ 100 nodes p99 < 200 ms @ 1k nodes feasible-node filter is O(nodes); cache-resident Node list
reconcile latency act < 1 s of watch event < 1 s watch→WorkQueue→Reconciler; full resync every 10 min
kubelet pod startup sandbox+container start < 5 s < 5 s excludes image pull; namespaces+cgroups+pivot_root setup
Node Lease renew every 10 s; NotReady after 40 s missed same lease_keepalive; 4× grace before NotReady
pod eviction grace (post-NotReady) 5 min default tunable Node-lifecycle controller evicts after grace
apiserver memory < 2 GB @ 30k objects bounded by replica watch cache + index; ~64 KB/object budget
kubelet memory < 256 MB < 256 MB PodWorker state + status cache; no full object store
store cache memory bounded (compaction-driven) bounded compaction reclaims superseded Revisions
RTO (apiserver/controller crash) re-sync from store < 30 s < 30 s stateless components; Reflector relist+rewatch on start
network RTT intra-cluster < 1 ms assumed < 1 ms datacenter LAN; not a WAN/edge target

4.2 Hard Invariants (always hold)

  • resourceVersion monotonic — every object's resource_version == store Revision at last write; strictly increasing per keyspace; never reused, never decremented. Foundation of optimistic concurrency + watch ordering.
  • Global write serialization — orc-store assigns Revision under a single logical clock; concurrent writes totally ordered; no two writes share a Revision.
  • Watch gap-freedom — a Watch from R delivers every change with Revision > R, in Revision order, no gaps, no dups; compacted R → 410 Gone, client relists. (No correctness path tolerates a dropped event.)
  • Single-writer-per-field — kubelet is sole writer of pod.status; scheduler/controllers write spec only; scheduler is sole writer of spec.node_name. Prevents status/bind races without locks.
  • Bind exclusivity — a Pod binds exactly once (spec.node_name set via CAS; second bind → 409).
  • Reconcile idempotence — reconcile reads current desired+observed and is safe to run N times; never relies on a specific event sequence (level-triggered).

4.3 Concurrency & Consistency Model

  • Consistency: orc-store is linearizable for reads/writes within a single Revision timeline; apiserver reads served from a watch cache are bounded-stale (≤ WATCH propagation latency). Clients needing strong reads pass resourceVersion=0-bypass → direct store range.
  • No distributed locks — coordination is via CAS on resourceVersion + leader election (one active controller/scheduler instance per kind; standbys hot-idle).
  • Independent async actors — each binary is a separate Tokio process; the only shared state is orc-store; all cross-component coordination flows through watch+CAS.
  • Backpressure — slow watchers get a bounded ring buffer; overflow → close stream with 410 (client relists) rather than unbounded apiserver heap growth.

4.4 Environment & Platform Constraints

Constraint Phase 1 Decision Note
Store topology single-node orc-store no quorum latency; Raft HA cross-link 42 deferred (Phase 13)
Node OS for runtime Linux-only (real) namespaces + cgroups v2 + pivot_root; stub runtime elsewhere
cgroups v2 unified required v1 unsupported; fail kubelet startup if v2 absent
Toolchain Rust edition 2021, MSRV 1.78, Tokio memory-safe syscall hot path; async watch fan-out
Network model flat pod network, RTT < 1 ms no WAN/cross-region; reference CNI only
Clock bounded skew assumed; leases use local TTL countdown no NTP dependency for correctness; skew tolerance via 4× grace

4.5 Explicit Non-Constraints (out of scope this phase)

  • No multi-region / WAN latency budget; no cloud-provider load balancer SLA.
  • No CRD/operator throughput target (Custom resource hook only — Phase 1 non-goal).
  • No full RBAC matrix performance; no service-mesh / Ingress dataplane targets.
  • No CSI plugin ecosystem SLA (hostPath/emptyDir + stub CSI only).
  • No quorum-latency budget until the Raft HA store lands (then store-write p99 shifts to the quorum column above).

4.6 Degradation Contract (constraint-violation behavior)

Trigger System response Constraint preserved
Watch buffer overflow close stream → 410 Gone gap-freedom (client relists, never silent gap)
Stale resourceVersion on write 409 Conflict optimistic concurrency (caller re-reads + retries)
Compacted Revision watched 410 Gone bounded store memory + gap-freedom
Node Lease lapse > 40 s Node → NotReady, evict after grace liveness; no pods stranded on dead node
apiserver/controller crash restart, relist+rewatch < 30 s RTO no state lost (store is sole durable truth)
Store WAL fsync stall writes block (no ack) durability over availability — never ack an unpersisted write

5. Required Reading

Curated, scoped to the build. Each item: where it maps onto the canonical spine, what to extract, what to ignore. Read order: spec sections gate phases; books/papers gate design decisions; reference impls gate API shape (read for contract, not for line-by-line porting — Go idioms do not transfer to Rust sum types + the typed Store trait).
