  • pivot_root requires new root be a mountpoint on a separate mount — root: bind-mount rootfs onto itself first; debug: EINVAL from pivot_root, check /proc/self/mountinfo.
  • cgroups v2 "no internal processes": can't put procs in a cgroup with child cgroups — root: enroll at leaf <ctr> only; debug: EBUSY writing cgroup.procs.
  • User-ns uid_map must be written by parent before child execs, and setgroups=deny before gid_map — root: ordering; debug: EPERM on map write.
  • Mount propagation leaks host mounts into container (or vice versa) — root: forgot to make / rec-private (MS_PRIVATE|MS_REC); debug: stray mounts in mountinfo.
  • Zombie PID 1: entrypoint not reaping -> defunct procs fill pid-ns — root: no init reaper; mitigation: optional tini-like reaper or document.
  • Layer tar with absolute symlinks / .. path traversal escapes rootfs — root: untrusted image; must sanitize paths + refuse .. during unpack.

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Layer unpack: stream gzip->tar, no full-file buffering; parallelize independent layers; target 200MB image unpack <3s on SSD.
  • Overlay mount is O(1); avoid per-container layer copy (share lowerdirs read-only across pods).
  • Container start (cached image): namespace+cgroup+pivot+exec target <150ms; cold pull dominated by network.
  • cgroup writes are cheap sysfs writes; batch limit writes before enroll. Image dedup: content-addressed blobs shared, no duplicate disk.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero-layer / scratch image (empty rootfs) -> overlay with only upper; entrypoint must be static binary.
  • Image config.User numeric vs name -> resolve against /etc/passwd in rootfs, fallback numeric.
  • Multi-arch manifest list -> select by host os/arch; none matching -> RuntimeError::NoMatchingPlatform.
  • Container exits before status poll -> cache exit code from reaper, not live waitpid.
  • Restart while stopping (SIGTERM in-flight) -> idempotent: stop wins, no double-start. Disk full mid-unpack -> rollback partial rootfs, error.

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Registry 5xx / network drop mid-blob digest mismatch on close / timeout retry blob (resumable via Range), exp backoff
cgroup limit write rejected (kernel lacks controller) ENOENT/EACCES on cpu.max check cgroup.controllers enabled; surface to kubelet event
pivot_root / mount fails after partial setup syscall errno unwind: umount in reverse, rmdir cgroup, free netns; no leak
OOM kill of container memory.events oom_kill++ mark exit 137 OOMKilled, restart per policy
Sandbox netns gone but containers alive setns ESRCH tear down pod, kubelet recreates sandbox

8.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: runtime_pull_seconds, runtime_unpack_bytes, container_start_seconds, oom_kills_total, restarts_total{container}, backoff gauge.
  • Logs: structured per containerId/sandboxId; layer digests at pull; cgroup path + applied limits at create.
  • Inspect: orctl exec/logs route via runtime; /sys/fs/cgroup/orc.slice/... is the source of truth for live limits.
  • GC: prune unreferenced image blobs + dead sandboxes on kubelet GC tick; umount2(MNT_DETACH) orphaned overlays on restart-recovery scan.
  • Security: run rootless via user-ns where possible; drop ambient caps; seccomp profile hook (deferred to Phase 12 admission).

20. Phase 9 — Deployment controller: rolling updates, rollback, history

9.1 Objectives

  • Deployment controller owns N ReplicaSets (one per pod-template revision); LOCKED level-triggered, idempotent reconcile from cache, never event payload.
  • Strategies: RollingUpdate{maxSurge,maxUnavailable} (scale-new-up / scale-old-down honoring readiness) + Recreate (terminate-all-then-create).
  • Track rollout via status (replicas/updated/ready/available/unavailable) + Conditions Progressing/Available; progressDeadlineSeconds → ReasonProgressDeadlineExceeded.
  • Revision history: stable template-hash → ReplicaSet identity; revision annotation; revisionHistoryLimit GC; pause/resume; rollback to prior revision.
  • NON-GOAL: blue/green or canary analysis (RollingUpdate + Recreate only); no autoscaling (HPA out of scope).

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-15 (level-triggered reconcile), ADR-16 (OwnerReferences between Deployment→ReplicaSet→Pod), ADR-13 (shared informers on three types), ADR-10 (optimistic-CC: Txn CAS on rollout status); INV-4 (convergent rolling update), INV-7 (no orphan RSes); P-1 (level-triggered), P-5 (idempotent rollout step); §5.1 Kubernetes in Action ch.9 (rolling update, rollback, history), Programming Kubernetes ch.6 (controller); §5.3 pkg/controller/deployment (rollout strategy, RS selection, pause/resume).

Module/file (crate orc-controller) Content
deployment/mod.rs DeploymentReconciler impl Reconciler; shared informers on Deployment+ReplicaSet+Pod
deployment/hash.rs pod_template_hash(&PodTemplateSpec) -> u32 — FNV-1a over canonical-JSON of template (excl. hash label); collision-avoidance suffix
deployment/rolling.rs reconcile_rolling: compute new_rs, old_rss, scaling deltas under maxSurge/maxUnavailable
deployment/recreate.rs reconcile_recreate: scale old→0, wait terminated, scale new→replicas
deployment/rollback.rs resolve target revision RS, copy its template into Deployment.spec.template
deployment/status.rs aggregate RS statuses → Deployment.status + Conditions
deployment/history.rs revision annotations, revisionHistoryLimit prune
  • Hash → RS identity: RS name = <deploy>-<hash>; label pod-template-hash=<hash> added to RS selector + template + Pod labels → prevents cross-RS adoption.
  • maxSurge/maxUnavailable resolve: percent → ceil(maxSurge), floor(maxUnavailable); invariant surge≥0 ∧ maxUnavail≤replicas ∧ not both 0.
  • Rolling step math: maxTotal = replicas + maxSurge; scale new up to min(desired, maxTotal − currentTotal); scale old down to max(0, currentReady − (replicas − maxUnavailable)); bound by readiness each pass; emit RequeueAfter(resync) until converged.
  • Writes: only Deployment.status + child RS spec.replicas (via apiserver CAS, retry 409). Deployment owns RS via OwnerReference{controller:true, block_owner_deletion:true}; RS owns Pods.
#Deployment with rolling strategy + recorded revision
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata: { name: web, annotations: { "deployment.orc/revision": "4" } }
spec:
  replicas: 10
  strategy: { type: RollingUpdate, rollingUpdate: { maxSurge: "25%", maxUnavailable: 1 } }
  revisionHistoryLimit: 10
  paused: false
  selector: { matchLabels: { app: web } }
  template: { metadata: { labels: { app: web } }, spec: { containers: [...] } }
status:
  observedGeneration: 7
  replicas: 10  updatedReplicas: 10  readyReplicas: 10  availableReplicas: 10  unavailableReplicas: 0
  conditions:
    - { type: Progressing, status: "True",  reason: NewReplicaSetAvailable }
    - { type: Available,   status: "True",  reason: MinimumReplicasAvailable }

9.3 Deliverable

  • orc-controller Deployment controller live; orctl apply of a new template → controlled rolling update; orctl rollout status/history/undo, rollout pause/resume.
  • Verifiable: editing spec.template triggers RollingUpdate with zero windows where availableReplicas < replicas − maxUnavailable; orctl rollout undo restores prior RS; Recreate fully terminates old before new.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Unavailability bound observed unavailable ≤ maxUnavailable at every reconcile sample (1s poll)
Surge bound total Pods ≤ replicas + maxSurge throughout
Rollout convergence (10→10 replicas, ready≈2s) Progressing=True/NewReplicaSetAvailable within ≤ 30s
Rollback undo to rev R restores that template hash, no new RS created (reuses existing)
Idempotency re-reconcile of converged Deployment → Reconcile::Done, zero writes
History RS count ≤ revisionHistoryLimit + active; old scaled-to-0 RS retained for rollback

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
rolling_progress.yaml step-by-step RS replica counts trace; invariants surge/unavail hold each step
recreate.yaml old RS→0 (all Pods Terminated) before new RS scales >0
rollback.yaml undo selects RS with revision annotation = target; template restored byte-equal
maxunavail_respected.yaml golden trace: availableReplicas never < replicas−maxUnavailable
paused.yaml template change while paused:true → no RS scaling, Progressing unchanged
template_hash_stable.json same template (reordered map keys) → identical hash
Malformed: both_zero.yaml maxSurge=0 ∧ maxUnavailable=0 → admission/validation reject
Malformed: bad_percent.yaml maxSurge="150x" → 422 invalid

9.6 Pitfalls

  • Hash instability → new RS churns every reconcile. Root cause: non-canonical JSON (map order, defaulted fields) in hash input. Debug: log hash + canonical bytes; diff across reconciles.
  • Adoption across revisions → old RS adopts new Pods. Root cause: selector lacks pod-template-hash. Fix: hash in selector; debug via ownerReferences + label audit.
  • Surge/unavail rounding → momentary outage. Root cause: percent→both rounding down to 0 unavailable + 0 surge stalls; ceil(surge)/floor(unavail) + the not-both-0 rule.
  • observedGeneration lag → rollout status hangs. Root cause: status written before observedGeneration=metadata.generation set; gate completion on observedGeneration match.
  • Scale-down too fast → counts readiness incorrectly. Root cause: using replicas not availableReplicas of old RS; always gate on Pod readiness, not spec.
  • Rollback creates new revision → infinite revision growth. Root cause: rollback edits template to a value with a new hash; must reuse the target RS's exact template.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • Reconcile from informer cache (no list-per-reconcile); WorkQueue dedup collapses burst RS/Pod events → ≤1 reconcile per Deployment per debounce.
  • 1000 Deployments × 3 RS each: full resync sweep < 5s; per-reconcile p99 < 5ms (cache hit).
  • Template hash cached on RS label → no rehash unless template changes; rolling step is O(#RS) not O(#Pods).
  • Bounded RequeueAfter (resync 30s) avoids hot-loop while a rollout waits on readiness.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • replicas:0 Deployment → all RS to 0, Available trivially true. Scale during rollout → re-spread surge/unavail proportionally.
  • maxUnavailable=replicas (full churn allowed) vs maxSurge=0 → in-place style. Single replica + maxUnavailable=0 → must surge (else deadlock).
  • Identical template re-apply → no-op, generation unchanged, no new RS. Rapid successive template edits → only newest RS active, intermediates scaled to 0.
  • Orphan RS (manual create matching selector) → adopt only if controller-ref empty + hash matches. Rollback to current revision → no-op.

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Response
New Pods crash-loop (never ready) no progress before progressDeadlineSeconds Condition Progressing=False/ProgressDeadlineExceeded; rollout halts, old RS retained
apiserver 409 on RS scale CAS conflict on resourceVersion re-read RS from cache, recompute, requeue (no over-scale)
Controller crash mid-rollout restart, relist via Reflector resume from observed RS counts (level-triggered, idempotent) — no double scale
Stuck (paused) rollout paused:true no scaling; resume re-enters rolling math from current counts

9.10 Operational Notes

  • orctl rollout status deploy/web (watch Conditions), rollout history, rollout undo --to-revision=N, rollout pause|resume, scale.
  • Events emitted: ScalingReplicaSet, NewReplicaSetCreated, DeploymentRollbackTo. Metrics: deployment_reconcile_duration, rollout_in_progress, rollout_failed_total.
  • Tuning: progressDeadlineSeconds (default 600), revisionHistoryLimit (default 10), controller resync 30s, WorkQueue rate-limit (exponential 5ms→1000s).
  • Debug: compare metadata.generation vs status.observedGeneration; inspect per-RS pod-template-hash + revision annotation to trace history.

21. Phase 10 — orc-net: Services, EndpointSlices, kube-proxy dataplane, DNS, CNI

10.1 Objectives

  • Service abstraction: stable ClusterIP VIP + ports + spec.selector; types ClusterIP, NodePort; headless (clusterIP: None) -> A-records per endpoint, no VIP.
  • EndpointSlice controller (in orc-controller, owns orc-net domain): watch Pods matching each Service selector + Node readiness; maintain EndpointSlice objects of ready backend IPs only.
  • ServiceProxy dataplane (kube-proxy-like): program VIP+port -> healthy endpoint LB rules; userspace proxy first, iptables/eBPF backend deferred.
  • DnsServer: resolve <svc>.<ns>.svc.cluster.local -> ClusterIP (A); SRV for ports; PTR optional; backed by EndpointSlice watch.
  • NetworkPlugin (CNI-like): assign Pod IP + wire pod netns on sandbox setup; teardown on delete; reference bridge+IPAM plugin.
  • Non-goals: no Ingress/L7, no service mesh, no real cloud LB (LoadBalancer type stubbed), single reference CNI only.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-24 (NetworkPlugin CNI-like trait), ADR-25 (userspace ServiceProxy first), ADR-15 (level-triggered EndpointSlice reconcile); P-1 (level-triggered), P-2 (store as source of truth for EndpointSlices); §5.2 kube-proxy dataplane writeups (VIP→endpoint selection, NodePort DNAT), DNS RFC 1035 + k8s DNS spec; §5.3 pkg/proxy (VIP→EndpointSlice sync), §5.4 CNI v1.0, DNS RFC 1035.

Module / file Adds
orc-api/src/service.rs Service{spec.selector,ports:Vec<ServicePort>,cluster_ip,type:ServiceType}, EndpointSlice{endpoints:Vec<Endpoint>,ports}, Endpoint{addresses,conditions:{ready,serving,terminating},target_ref}
orc-controller/src/endpointslice.rs EndpointSliceReconciler: Reconciler — informers on Pod+Service+Node; selector match -> group ready Pod IPs into slices ≤100 endpoints each; ownerRef Service; readiness from PodStatus.conditions[Ready]
orc-net/src/proxy.rs ServiceProxy::sync(services,slices); userspace: listener per (ClusterIP,port) + per-NodePort; pick_backend = round-robin / least-conn over ready endpoints; session affinity (ClientIP, default 10800s) via src-IP hash table
orc-net/src/vip.rs ClusterIP allocator (CIDR 10.96.0.0/16); dummy-iface VIP bind; NodePort range 30000-32767 allocator
orc-net/src/dns.rs DnsServer UDP/TCP :53; zone cluster.local; A=<svc>.<ns>.svc, SRV=_port._proto.<svc>.<ns>.svc, headless -> per-endpoint A; informer-driven cache; NXDOMAIN on unknown
orc-net/src/cni.rs NetworkPlugin impl: alloc IP from node PodCIDR, create veth pair, move one end to pod netns, assign IP+route+default gw=bridge, teardown reverses + frees IP
  • ClusterIP allocation: apiserver admission picks free IP from CIDR on create; persists in spec.cluster_ip; freed on delete (bitmap in store key /ranges/serviceip).
  • VIP -> backend: sync is level-triggered from Service+EndpointSlice watch; rebuild rule table on each delta (idempotent), never edge-patch.
  • Affinity table: HashMap<SrcIp,(Backend,Instant)>; evict on TTL or backend-removal.
  • Headless: skip VIP alloc; DNS returns all ready endpoint IPs.

10.3 Deliverable

orc-net linked into orc-kubelet (CNI + node ServiceProxy) and a standalone DNS process; EndpointSliceReconciler in orc-controller. Demo: apply Deployment+Service; curl http://<ClusterIP>:<port> round-robins across ready pods; nslookup <svc>.<ns>.svc.cluster.local -> ClusterIP; scaling pods updates EndpointSlice + LB set within reconcile latency.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • 1 Service / 5 ready pods: 1000 VIP requests distribute 200±40 per backend (round-robin within ±20%).
  • Pod -> NotReady (readiness probe fail): removed from EndpointSlice ≤2s; receives 0 new connections; in-flight drains.
  • Scale 5->10 pods: EndpointSlice reflects 10 ready endpoints ≤1s after all Running+Ready; LB set updated same.
  • DNS: A query for <svc>.<ns>.svc.cluster.local returns ClusterIP <5ms p99; NXDOMAIN for unknown; headless returns N endpoint IPs.
  • CNI: pod gets unique IP in node PodCIDR; ping pod->pod cross-node works; teardown frees IP (reusable, no leak over 1000 churn cycles).

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Expect
svc_clusterip.yaml VIP allocated from 10.96.0.0/16, idempotent re-apply keeps IP
endpointslice_churn.jsonl pod add/del/ready-flip stream -> golden slice sequence (ready-only, ≤100/slice)
dns_queries.txt A/SRV/headless/NXDOMAIN -> golden response packets
proxy_distribution.json 10k req over 5 backends -> per-backend counts within ±20%
cni_lifecycle.json setup->IP assigned in CIDR; teardown->IP freed
Malformed selector matching 0 pods -> empty slice (not absent); duplicate NodePort -> 409; ClusterIP exhaustion -> 422; pod IP collision -> CNI error + retry

10.6 Pitfalls

  • Including not-ready endpoints in LB set -> traffic to pods still starting -> connection refused. Root: reading Pod.phase=Running not conditions[Ready]. Debug: diff EndpointSlice vs PodStatus.conditions.
  • Stale affinity entry after backend removal -> packets to dead pod. Root: affinity table not pruned on endpoint delete. Debug: dump affinity map vs current backends.
  • VIP not freed on Service delete -> CIDR exhaustion. Root: missing finalizer / dealloc. Debug: bitmap occupancy vs live Services.
  • DNS cache lag -> resolves to deleted Service VIP. Root: serving from stale informer cache after compaction/relist. Debug: compare DNS zone vs apiserver Service list.
  • Slice churn thrash (>100 endpoints flapping across slices) -> excessive writes. Root: unstable endpoint->slice assignment. Debug: track slice mod_rev rate; use stable hashing of pod UID.
  • veth/netns leak on sandbox crash -> IP exhaustion + dangling ifaces. Root: teardown not called on abnormal sandbox exit. Debug: ip netns list vs running sandboxes.

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Userspace proxy adds ~1 hop copy: target <0.5ms added p99 latency, ≥50k conn/s/node; iptables/eBPF backend (deferred) for >100k.
  • ServiceProxy::sync O(services × endpoints); incremental rule diff, not full rebuild, when slice count >1k; rebuild ≤10ms for 1k services.
  • DNS: in-memory cache, ≥50k qps/instance, p99 <5ms; negative-cache NXDOMAIN 5s.
  • EndpointSlice reconcile: batch + ≤100 endpoints/slice caps write size; debounce 200ms on rapid pod churn.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • Service with 0 ready endpoints -> VIP listener returns RST/refused (not hang); empty (not absent) EndpointSlice.
  • All endpoints terminating -> serve terminating only if no ready (graceful), else drop.
  • ClusterIP reuse race: deleted Service IP not reissued until store delete committed.
  • NodePort collision across Services -> 409 at allocation.
  • Pod IP reused before old conntrack expires -> conntrack flush on endpoint delete.
  • Dual-stack: IPv4-only Phase 1; IPv6 endpoints filtered out, logged.

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
DnsServer crash health probe / no :53 response restart; pods retry resolver; cache rebuilt from watch
Proxy desync (rules ≠ slices) periodic reconcile compares table vs slices full resync rebuild on drift
EndpointSlice controller lag endpoint age vs pod ready time metric resync interval 30s catches missed watch
CNI IPAM exhaustion alloc returns NoIP expand PodCIDR / GC leaked IPs; pod sandbox stays Pending
VIP bitmap corruption alloc/free mismatch on startup scan rebuild bitmap from live Service list

10.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: orcnet_proxy_backends, orcnet_lb_requests_total{service}, orcnet_dns_qps, orcnet_dns_nxdomain_total, orcnet_endpointslice_sync_seconds, orcnet_ipam_allocated.
  • Tunables: ClusterIP CIDR, NodePort range, affinity TTL, DNS TTL, slice max-size (100), reconcile debounce.
  • Debug: orctl get endpointslices -l kubernetes.io/service-name=<svc>; orctl describe service <svc> shows VIP+backends; dig against DnsServer.
  • Single-writer: EndpointSlice owned by EndpointSliceReconciler only; ServiceProxy is read-only consumer.
  • Cross-link: NetworkPlugin invoked by orc-kubelet sandbox setup (Phase 7/8); DnsServer ClusterIP injected into pod /etc/resolv.conf via orc-runtime.

22. Phase 11 — Config and storage: ConfigMaps, Secrets, volumes, CSI hook

11.1 Objectives

  • Inject ConfigMap/Secret into Pods two ways: env (valueFrom.configMapKeyRef/secretKeyRef, envFrom) and projected volume files.
  • VolumeManager in orc-kubelet drives volume lifecycle keyed to PodWorker: emptyDir, hostPath, projected (configmap/secret/serviceAccountToken/downwardAPI). NON-GOAL: real CSI ecosystem.
  • Live update: ConfigMap/Secret mutation propagates to mounted projected files within bounded TTL (atomic symlink swap). Env injection is one-shot at container start (no live update — requires restart).
  • CSIPlugin trait (stub) for external volumes; Secret base64 decode + at-rest + log-redaction guarantees.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-26 (Secrets: base64 + tmpfs projection + envelope-encryption seam), ADR-15 (level-triggered VolumeManager reconcile on ConfigMap/Secret watch); P-2 (store as source of truth for ConfigMap/Secret), P-6 (VolumeManager sole writer of volume mounts); §5.4 CSI v1.x (VolumeManager stub: NodePublish/NodeUnpublish), §5.4 OCI Runtime Spec (volume mount in config.json).

  • orc-api (orc-api/src/volume.rs): enum VolumeSource { EmptyDir{medium,size_limit}, HostPath{path,type_}, ConfigMap{name,items,optional}, Secret{secret_name,items,optional}, Projected{sources:Vec<ProjectedSource>}, Csi{driver,volume_handle,read_only,attributes} }; Volume{name, source}; VolumeMount{name,mount_path,sub_path,read_only}; enum ProjectedSource { ConfigMap, Secret, ServiceAccountToken{audience,expiration_seconds,path}, DownwardApi{items} }. Secret{ meta, type_:String, data:BTreeMap<String,Vec<u8>> } (raw bytes; base64 only at JSON codec edge). ConfigMap{ meta, data:BTreeMap<String,String>, binary_data:BTreeMap<String,Vec<u8>> }.
  • orc-kubelet volume.rs: VolumeManager{ pod_volumes: HashMap<PodUid,Vec<MountedVolume>>, informers: {cm,secret}, base_dir }. Methods async fn mount_volumes(pod)->Result<Vec<Mounted>>, async fn unmount_volumes(pod_uid), async fn reconcile_projected(pod_uid). Layout: {base_dir}/pods/{uid}/volumes/{plugin}/{name}/.
  • Projected write algorithm (atomic, k8s-style timestamped dirs): write payload to ..{rev}/, fsync, swap ..data symlink atomically (renameat2 RENAME_EXCHANGE/rename tmp-symlink), unlink old rev dir. Per-key files symlink key -> ..data/key. Guarantees readers never see partial set.
  • Update propagation: VolumeManager watches ConfigMap/Secret via SharedIndexInformer (cache keyed by namespace/name); on delta, enqueue affected pod UIDs (reverse index cm/secret -> pods), reconcile_projected rewrites ..data. Bounded by --config-sync-period (default 60s) + informer watch latency.
  • Env injection (in orc-runtime ContainerConfig build, fed by kubelet): resolve refs from informer cache → OciSpec.process.env. optional=false + missing key → Pod stays Pending, Event CreateContainerConfigError.
  • CSI stub (orc-kubelet/src/csi.rs): trait CsiPlugin { async fn node_stage(VolumeId, StagePath)->Result<()>; async fn node_publish(VolumeId,target,ro)->Result<()>; async fn node_unpublish(target)->Result<()>; }; stub returns Unimplemented but registers/validates Csi source against a driver registry.
  • emptyDir: tmpfs mount if medium=Memory (counts against container memory cgroup) else dir under pod base; deleted on pod teardown. hostPath: bind-mount host path; type check (DirectoryOrCreate,File,Socket); admission gate (security risk).

11.3 Deliverable

  • orc-kubelet VolumeManager mounting all listed volume types; ConfigMap/Secret env + projected-file injection; live projected update; CSI stub registry; Secret redaction in logs/events/orctl describe.
  • e2e: Pod consumes a ConfigMap + Secret as env and files, mounts an emptyDir shared between two containers → all assertions green.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Metric Target
Projected mount→file-ready latency < 200 ms p99 after sandbox ready
ConfigMap/Secret update → file visible ≤ config-sync-period+2s (default ≤62s)
..data swap atomicity reader never observes partial/empty key set (10k concurrent reads, 0 torn)
Secret redaction 0 secret bytes in logs/events/describe/store-debug-dump
emptyDir teardown 0 orphan dirs/tmpfs mounts after pod GC (verified via /proc/mounts)
Env missing required key Pod Pending, Event CreateContainerConfigError, container not started

11.5 Conformance Fixtures

  • fixtures/vol/configmap_env_and_file.yaml → env LOG_LEVEL=info + file /etc/cfg/log_level identical; exit 0.
  • fixtures/vol/secret_projected.yaml → /etc/sec/token mode 0400, base64 round-trips to raw bytes; describe shows <redacted>.
  • fixtures/vol/emptydir_shared.yaml → writer container writes, reader reads same bytes via shared emptyDir.
  • fixtures/vol/projected_update.yaml → mutate CM mid-run, assert file changes within TTL; ..data inode changes, key symlink stable.
  • Malformed: secret_missing_key_required.yaml (optional=false) → Pending+Event; hostpath_escaping_subpath.yaml (subPath: ../../) → rejected by admission; csi_unknown_driver.yaml → Csi validation error.

11.6 Pitfalls

  • subPath .. traversal → mount escapes pod dir → arbitrary host write. Root cause: naive path join. Fix: canonicalize + assert prefix==volume root; reject ... Debug: realpath mount target.
  • Non-atomic projected write → reader sees half-updated keyset → app crash. Root cause: per-file in-place rewrite. Fix: timestamped dir + single symlink swap. Debug: ls -la ..data inode churn.
  • Secret leaking into Events/logs → exposure in orctl get events. Root cause: error msg interpolates value. Fix: Secret wrapper type with redacting Debug/Display; never log data. Debug: grep store dump.
  • tmpfs emptyDir not counted in cgroup → memory medium escapes limit → node OOM. Root cause: tmpfs on shared mount. Fix: mount in pod cgroup memory accounting. Debug: memory.current vs tmpfs usage.
  • Stale informer cache → projected file lags spec by full resync. Root cause: missed watch + long resync. Fix: reverse-index enqueue on every delta + bounded sync period. Debug: compare file rev vs CM resourceVersion.
  • Orphan mounts on crash → kubelet restart leaks bind mounts. Root cause: no mount reconstruction. Fix: rebuild VolumeManager state from /proc/mounts + pod cache on startup. Debug: mount | grep pods.

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Projected reconcile O(affected pods), not O(all pods): reverse index cm/secret name → pod UIDs; avoid full sweep.
  • Coalesce rapid CM updates (debounce ≤1s) → 1 file rewrite per burst, not per delta.
  • Shared SharedIndexInformer for CM+Secret across all PodWorkers (1 watch/type/node, not per-pod) → bound watch fan-out.
  • ..data swap is rename(2) (atomic, no copy of unchanged keys); fsync only changed payload dir.

11.8 Edge Cases

  • optional=true + missing CM/Secret → empty mount, Pod runs. optional=false → block.
  • Key with subPath → single file mount, no live update (k8s semantics: subPath projected is frozen).
  • items projection (key→relative path) with overlapping paths → last-wins; collision across projected sources → validation error.
  • Immutable Secret/ConfigMap (immutable:true) → reject updates at admission; skip informer-driven reconcile.
  • ServiceAccountToken projected: rotate before expiration_seconds expiry (refresh at 80% TTL); audience-bound.
  • Zero-byte / binary-data keys; key names with . .. reserved (rejected); mode bits honored (default 0644).

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Mount syscall fails (ENOSPC/EACCES) mount_volumes err Pod Pending, Event FailedMount, backoff retry
Symlink swap interrupted (crash mid-swap) startup reconstruct both ..data + tmp valid; pick newest rev dir, gc others
Secret deleted while mounted informer delete delta keep last-known file (don't blank), Event Warning; do not crash pod
CSI driver unregistered publish call node_publish err → FailedMount; never block other volumes
Disk full on projected write fsync EIO/ENOSPC abort swap, keep old ..data, Event, retry on next sync

11.10 Operational Notes

  • Flags: --config-sync-period=60s, --volume-base-dir=/var/lib/orc-kubelet, --enable-hostpath=false (gated), --csi-driver-dir.
  • Metrics: volume_manager_mount_seconds, projected_reconcile_total{result}, config_sync_lag_seconds, secret_redaction_violations_total (alert >0).
  • Security: Secrets 0400, tmpfs-backed (never to disk by default); at-rest encryption is orc-apiserver concern (Phase 12 EncryptionConfig); kubelet never logs Secret.data.
  • Debug: orctl describe pod shows mounts + redacted secrets; find {base}/pods/{uid}/volumes to inspect layout; mount | grep {uid} for leaks.

23. Phase 12 — Security: ServiceAccounts, RBAC, admission, quotas, authn

12.1 Objectives

  • Authenticate every apiserver request: client TLS cert (CN→user, O→groups) OR bearer/ServiceAccount JWT → Identity{user, groups, sa?}; anonymous → system:anonymous.
  • ServiceAccounts (orc-api) + projected, audience-scoped, time-bound token volumes mounted by orc-kubelet VolumeManager.
  • Minimal RBAC authorizer: Role/RoleBinding (namespaced) + ClusterRole/ClusterRoleBinding → (verb,resource,namespace,name) → Allow/Deny; default deny.
  • Admission chain: mutating then validating, built-ins (NamespaceLifecycle, LimitRange defaulting, ResourceQuota enforce) + external webhooks; tamper-evident audit log.
  • NON-GOAL: full K8s RBAC matrix, aggregated/escalation rules, OIDC, encryption-at-rest KMS (XOR-seal stub only, cross-link Phase 11).

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-26 (Secrets never logged; envelope-encryption provider seam), ADR-10 (RBAC admission as another optimistic-CC guard layer), ADR-28 (typed auth errors); INV-3 (resourceVersion CAS still enforced post-auth); P-9 (fail toward safety — deny-by-default), P-11 (node isolates untrusted workloads — ServiceAccount token scope); §5.4 RFC 5280/JWT (client-cert authn chain, SA JWT validation); §5.2 Kubernetes API Conventions (conditions on authn failures), §5.3 pkg/apiserver (Authenticator/Authorizer middleware chain).

  • orc-apiserver/src/authn/ — mod.rs (Authenticator trait authenticate(&Request)->Result<Identity,Unauthenticated>), x509.rs (verify chain vs client-CA bundle), token.rs (HS256/RS256 JWT verify, iss/aud/exp/sa.uid claims), union.rs (first-success chain). 401 on all-fail.
  • orc-apiserver/src/authz/rbac.rs — Authorizer::authorize(&Identity,&Attributes)->Decision; informer-backed cache of Role/ClusterRole + bindings; match by user/group/system:serviceaccount:<ns>:<name>; rule = {verbs,api_groups,resources,resource_names} w/ * wildcard.
  • orc-api: ServiceAccount, Role, RoleBinding, ClusterRole, ClusterRoleBinding, ResourceQuota, LimitRange kinds; TokenRequest subresource (spec audiences/expirationSeconds → status token+expiry).
  • orc-apiserver/src/admission/ — Admission trait admit(&mut AdmissionReview)->Result<(),Reject>; chain ordered mutate→validate; quota.rs (atomic Txn: used+request ≤ hard per ns), limitrange.rs (default/clamp container requests/limits), nslifecycle.rs (reject writes into Terminating ns), webhook.rs (HTTPS POST AdmissionReview, failurePolicy: Fail|Ignore, timeout 10s).
  • Request pipeline order: authn → authz → mutating admission → schema validation → validating admission → Registry write (orc-store Txn). Audit emitted at each stage to orc-apiserver/src/audit.rs (append-only JSONL, stage+verb+user+decision+RV).
  • Controllers (orc-controller): ServiceAccount controller (auto-create default SA per namespace + token Secret); token rotation re-projects before 80% TTL.

12.3 Deliverable

orc-apiserver enforcing authn+authz+admission+quota+audit; orctl --token/--client-cert flags; ServiceAccount controller in orc-controller. Runnable: 401 for no creds, 403 for out-of-RBAC verb, 403/422 for quota breach, mutating webhook injects a label; audit JSONL written.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Anonymous write rejected HTTP 401
SA outside RBAC (verb/resource/ns) HTTP 403, reason names missing rule
Pod create exceeding ResourceQuota HTTP 403, used unchanged (atomic)
LimitRange defaulting container w/o requests gets ns default
Webhook reject (failurePolicy: Fail) request denied; Ignore + timeout → admitted
authn+authz overhead < 1 ms p50 (cache hit), < 5 ms p99
Audit completeness 1 line per request, ordered by RV, no gaps

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
authn_x509_valid.pem / authn_token_valid.jwt Identity{user,groups}; expired/aud-mismatch JWT → 401
rbac_role_pod_reader.yaml + rolebinding.yaml get pods Allow; delete pods Deny(403)
rbac_clusterrole_admin.yaml cross-namespace Allow
quota_hard_cpu_2.yaml 3rd pod (cpu sum > 2) → 403; quota.used exact
limitrange_defaults.yaml golden mutated PodSpec
webhook_mutating_addlabel.json response patch applies label
Malformed: unsigned JWT, alg:none, binding→missing role, quota negative, webhook 500 401 / 401 / authz-deny / validation 422 / failurePolicy-governed

12.6 Pitfalls

  • alg:none / key-confusion JWT — root: accepting unsigned or HS-signed-with-RS-pubkey tokens. Pin alg per issuer; reject none. Debug: decode header, assert alg allowlist.
  • TOCTOU quota race — root: read-used then write non-atomically → two pods both pass. Fix: enforce inside the same orc-store Txn that writes the pod (CAS on quota RV). Debug: concurrent-create test, assert used ≤ hard.
  • Admission ordering bug — mutate after validate → validated stale object; or quota counts a pod the webhook later rejects. Order mutate→validate; deduct quota only on commit. Debug: replay AdmissionReview log.
  • RBAC cache staleness — informer lag grants/revokes late. Bound by resync; deny on cache-miss for cluster-scoped. Debug: compare decision vs live store RV.
  • Webhook deadlock — webhook served by a Pod gated by the same admission → cluster can't start. Exempt kube-system/bootstrap ns + failurePolicy: Ignore on self. Debug: admission latency spike on startup.
  • Token in audit/logs — leaking bearer token. Redact Authorization + token claims before audit write.

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • RBAC decision: in-memory rule index keyed by (group,resource); O(rules-for-subject) ~µs; target < 1 ms p50.
  • JWT verify: cache RS256 pubkey-parse + LRU verified-token cache (TTL ≤ token exp) to skip re-verify; HS256 ~10 µs.
  • Admission webhook is the tail-latency risk: parallel-call independent webhooks, 10 s timeout, circuit-break on repeated failure; quota Txn adds one extra CAS per write.
  • Audit: buffered async append, fsync batched (≤ 100 ms) — never block request on disk.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • ServiceAccount deleted while token valid → bound-token sa.uid mismatch → 401 (uid binding defeats stale tokens).
  • ResourceQuota added to a namespace already over hard → no eviction; blocks new only (level-triggered).
  • RoleBinding references nonexistent Role → treated as no rules (deny), not error.
  • ClusterRoleBinding + namespaced resource → applies in all namespaces.
  • Quota on a resource the object doesn't consume → no-op; object with no requests + quota on requests.cpu → reject (requests required).
  • Webhook returns both allow + patch → patch applied only if allowed.

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Response
Client-CA bundle missing/rotated x509 verify fail spike reload bundle (SIGHUP); fail-closed 401
Webhook endpoint down timeout/conn-refused apply failurePolicy; emit Event; circuit-break
Quota counter drift vs actual pods reconcile recompute vs cache ResourceQuota controller recomputes used from live list each resync
Audit disk full write ENOSPC fail-closed (reject writes) if audit.required, else drop+alert
RBAC authorizer panic request 500 default-deny fallback, never fail-open

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Bootstrap: static superuser cert (system:masters group → implicit allow) seeds the first RBAC objects; rotate out post-bootstrap.
  • Flags: --client-ca, --service-account-key, --service-account-issuer, --audit-path, --enable-admission=Quota,LimitRange,NamespaceLifecycle,Webhook, --authorization-mode=RBAC.
  • Token rotation: kubelet re-requests projected tokens at 80% TTL; clock skew tolerance ±60 s on exp.
  • Metrics: authn_failures_total{reason}, authz_denied_total{verb,resource}, admission_latency_seconds{webhook}, quota_rejections_total{ns}, audit_events_total.
  • Secrets at rest: SA token Secrets sealed via Phase-11 stub; treat audit log as sensitive (token-redacted, 0600).

24. Phase 13 — Resilience and HA: leader election, drain/eviction, PDBs, Raft-backed store

13.1 Objectives

  • Single-active control-plane components — exactly one orc-controller + one orc-scheduler active; standbys hot-idle, fail over within lease TTL on crash/partition.
  • Leader election via orc-store Lease + CAS Txn (no external coordinator) → reuses MVCC/lease primitives, no split-active.
  • Graceful Node drain + eviction honoring PodDisruptionBudget — never evict below budget; taint-based eviction with tolerationSeconds.
  • Multi-orc-apiserver HA: wire orc-store raft_hook → project-42 Raft, replicate keyspace across odd quorum (3/5), tolerate ⌊(N-1)/2⌋ losses; split-brain impossible (quorum write).
  • End artifact: kill active leader → standby acquires within TTL; drain respects PDB; store/apiserver node loss survived under chaos.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-27 (lease-based leader election via store CAS), ADR-06 (Raft replication hook seam), ADR-05 (Store trait: ReplicationHook seam); INV-1 (Revision monotonic — survives HA handoff), INV-3 (CAS guards leader acquisition); P-2 (store single source of truth even in HA), P-9 (fail-stop on leader loss); §5.1 Distributed Systems 4e (lease TTL ↔ false-positive eviction, split-brain), Raft paper (log replication, leader election); §5.3 etcd/lease (Lessor keepalive, TTL expiry cascade).

  • orc-controller/src/leaderelection.rs: LeaderElector{ lease_key, identity, ttl, renew_deadline, retry_period }; async fn run(&self, on_acquire, on_lose). Acquire = store Txn: Compare(lease_holder, absent|expired) → Op::Put(holder=id, renew_rev) else observe. Hold = lease_keepalive every ttl/3; lose if renew fails before renew_deadline.
  • LeaderLease object stored at /registry/leases/<component>: { holder_identity, lease_duration, acquire_rev, renew_time, leader_transitions } (mirrors coordination.k8s.io/Lease). RV-CAS guards concurrent acquire.
  • Component lifecycle: run() blocks reconcilers/scheduler until on_acquire fires; on_lose → cancel all WorkQueue/informers, std::process::exit(1) (fail-stop, restart re-contends). Informers may warm cache while standby (read-only); no writes off-leader.
  • orc-api: add PodDisruptionBudget{ spec.selector, spec.min_available|max_unavailable, status.disruptions_allowed, status.current_healthy, status.expected_pods, status.observed_generation }; add Eviction subresource (POST /pods/<n>/eviction).
  • orc-apiserver/src/eviction.rs: eviction handler → compute live disruptions_allowed from matching PDB cache; if >0 decrement (CAS Txn on PDB status) + delete Pod; else 429 TooManyRequests w/ retry-after.
  • orc-controller: PdbController recomputes status.current_healthy/disruptions_allowed from informer cache on Pod/PDB deltas. Node-lifecycle extended: drain = cordon (spec.unschedulable=true) + iterate Pods calling Eviction API (not raw delete) → PDB-respecting.
  • Taint eviction: Node-lifecycle adds node.kubernetes.io/not-ready:NoExecute on lost lease; Pod w/o matching toleration evicted after toleration_seconds (timer in informer cache); tolerating Pod stays.
  • orc-store/src/raft.rs: raft_hook trait ReplicationLog{ async fn propose(&self, entries: &[LogEntry]) -> Result<Revision>; async fn apply_committed(&self) -> WatchStream }. Writes (put/delete/txn) → propose to Raft, commit on quorum, then bump local Revision from committed index. range/watch served from local committed state. Backend selectable: single-node (default) | raft (cross-link 42).

13.3 Deliverable

orc-controller/orc-scheduler run N-replica with leader election; orctl drain <node> + Eviction subresource + PdbController; taint-based eviction w/ tolerationSeconds; orc-store --backend=raft --peers=... 3-node replicated store. Chaos harness: chaos kill-leader, chaos drain, chaos partition-store.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
Leader failover on kill standby active ≤ ttl (default 15s); ≤1 active at all times
Two leaders observed (split-active) 0 across 10k contention rounds
Drain below PDB 0 evictions that violate min_available
Eviction when disruptions_allowed==0 429 + retry-after, Pod survives
Taint eviction timing tolerating Pod stays; non-tolerating evicted at tolerationSeconds ±1s
Raft store: lose 1 of 3 nodes reads+writes continue; lose 2 of 3 → writes block (no split-brain)
Quorum write latency (3-node, LAN) p99 ≤ 12ms (vs ≤3ms single-node)

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

  • leaderelection_acquire.jsonl — two contenders, one acquires; expect single holder, leader_transitions increments on failover.
  • pdb_block_eviction.yaml — PDB min_available=2, 2 healthy Pods → eviction request expects 429.
  • pdb_allow_eviction.yaml — min_available=2, 3 healthy → 200, disruptions_allowed 1→0.
  • taint_toleration.yaml — Pod with tolerationSeconds=300 on NoExecute taint → not evicted before 300s.
  • raft_quorum_loss.jsonl — 3-node, kill 2 → expect write Unavailable, no committed divergence.
  • Malformed: PDB with both min_available+max_unavailable → 422; eviction of already-deleted Pod → 404 idempotent; Raft propose past compacted index → 410.

13.6 Pitfalls

  • Clock-skew leader election → use store Revision/server time for renew deadlines, NOT wall clock on contenders; skew >TTL else dual-leader. Debug: log acquire_rev + renew_time divergence.
  • Off-leader writes after lease loss → in-flight Txn commits after standby acquired → conflicting writes. Mitigation: fence each write with Compare(lease_holder==self); on_lose must cancel before exit. Debug: trace write holder vs current.
  • PDB staleness → disruptions_allowed from lagging informer cache over-permits eviction. Root cause: read from cache not store; do CAS Txn on PDB status at eviction time. Debug: replay PDB RV at eviction.
  • Drain raw-delete bypasses PDB → must call Eviction subresource, never delete pod. Debug: audit-log delete vs eviction path.
  • Raft read-stale → serving range from non-leader follower returns stale Revision; require leader-lease read or read-index. Debug: compare follower committed index vs leader.
  • Cordon-without-drain → new Pods stop scheduling but running Pods linger; ensure cordon+evict are one orctl op.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Leader renew: ttl/3 keepalive → ~3 store ops/component/15s; negligible. Failover latency bounded by renew_deadline (default 10s) < ttl.
  • Raft quorum write: every put/txn = 1 RTT to majority → p99 ≤ 12ms (3-node LAN); batch proposals (≤64 entries / 5ms) to amortize fsync + RTT.
  • PDB recompute O(pods matching selector) per delta → index Pods by PDB selector in informer; cap recompute fan-out.
  • Drain throughput: serial eviction respects PDB pacing; parallelize across distinct PDBs, serialize within one.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • All replicas crash mid-hold → lease expires after ttl; first restart re-acquires; gap ≤ ttl.
  • PDB selector matches 0 Pods → disruptions_allowed undefined → treat as block (429) until expected_pods known.
  • Overlapping PDBs on one Pod → eviction must satisfy ALL; pick most-restrictive; if any blocks → 429.
  • Pod already terminating during drain → skip eviction, count as disrupted.
  • Raft even-split (2/2 after partition) → both sides lack majority → writes block both sides (correct, no split-brain).
  • Taint added then removed within tolerationSeconds → cancel eviction timer.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
Active leader OOM/crash renew stops, lease expires standby acquires ≤ ttl; fail-stop restart re-contends
Network partition isolates leader renew Txn fails (no quorum) leader on_lose self-fences+exits; majority side elects
Raft loses quorum (≥⌊N/2⌋+1 down) propose returns Unavailable apiserver returns 503; reads from committed state OK; resume on quorum heal
PDB controller lag disruptions_allowed stale eviction CAS Txn on store rejects stale; client retries
Split-brain attempt two holders' CAS both target same key store Revision serializes; loser gets 409, exits

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Tunables: --leader-elect=true, --lease-duration=15s, --renew-deadline=10s, --retry-period=2s; --backend=raft --peers=h1,h2,h3.
  • Metrics: leader_is_active{component}, leader_transitions_total, eviction_blocked_total{pdb}, raft_proposal_latency_seconds, raft_quorum_size, node_drain_duration_seconds.
  • Run odd quorum (3 or 5); never even. Place store peers in distinct failure domains; co-locate apiserver+store-peer per HA node.
  • Drain runbook: orctl drain <node> --grace-period=30s cordons then PDB-paced evicts; abort leaves node cordoned (re-run or orctl uncordon).
  • Alert: leader_transitions_total spike → flapping (TTL too low / store latency); raft_quorum_size < majority → page.

25. Phase 14 — Conformance, e2e, chaos, fuzzing, and benchmarks

14.1 Objectives

  • Stand up the full validation surface gating "Done": multi-node e2e, chaos, fuzz, property, scale benchmarks.
  • e2e: spin a real cluster (1 control plane + N kubelets via docker-compose) and run workload scenarios end-to-end.
  • chaos: kill/partition every component; assert eventual convergence + zero double-run / zero split-brain.
  • fuzz: JSON codec never panics; watch protocol stays ordered + gap-free under random compaction; selector matcher total.
  • property: encode section-7 invariants (reconcile convergence/idempotency, watch gap-freedom, optimistic-CC safety, binding exclusivity, GC reachability) as machine-checked properties.
  • scale: benchmark apiserver req/s, watch fan-out, scheduling throughput @100 nodes, reconcile + store latency, with CI regression gates.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Anchors: ADR-31 (deterministic-time test harness), ADR-32 (cargo-fuzz on codec + watch decoder), ADR-33 (proptest for invariants + in-proc e2e harness), ADR-34 (CI: fuzz-smoke + e2e on Linux); INV-1…INV-7 (all invariants machine-checked here); §5.2 "Level Triggering" (idempotence property), Omega (conflict/retry property); §5.1 DDIA ch.8/9 (fault model for chaos scenarios).

  • New workspace crate orc-e2e (dev-only, not published): harness/cluster.rs (compose lifecycle), scenarios/*.rs, chaos/faults.rs, bench/ (Criterion), fuzz/ (cargo-fuzz targets).
  • Cluster::up(nodes: usize) -> Cluster → docker compose up with orc-apiserver + orc-store + orc-controller + orc-scheduler + N×orc-kubelet; Cluster::orctl(args) -> Output; Cluster::down() tears down + collects logs.
  • Scenarios drive via orctl apply/get/scale/rollout/logs; poll orc-apiserver watch for terminal conditions, not fixed sleeps (wait_until(pred, timeout)).
  • Chaos FaultInjector trait: kill(component), pause(component) (SIGSTOP), partition_store(duration), expire_lease(node), inject_image_pull_err(rate), inject_rpc_err(rate) (toxiproxy-style proxy in front of apiserver REST + store).
  • Fuzz targets (libFuzzer via cargo-fuzz): fuzz_codec (arbitrary bytes → orc_api JSON decode, must Result, never panic), fuzz_watch (random put/delete/compact op stream → assert WatchStream ordering+gap-freedom), fuzz_selector (arbitrary LabelSelector + labels → matches() total, no panic/overflow).
  • Property tests (proptest): generators for Pod/ReplicaSet/Node + op sequences; oracle re-derives expected convergent state; differential check vs live controller cache.
  • Bench harness: Criterion groups emit target/criterion/*/estimates.json; a bench-gate binary diffs vs committed benchmarks/baseline.json, fails if regression > threshold.

14.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test -p orc-e2e --features compose runs all e2e scenarios green against a live N-node compose cluster.
  • make chaos (chaos suite), make fuzz-ci (time-boxed fuzz), make bench (+ gate) all wired into CI; benchmarks/REPORT.md published.

14.4 Acceptance Criteria

Gate Target
e2e scenarios 100% pass: deploy, scale, rolling-update (zero-downtime), service traffic, node-failure reschedule
chaos convergence every fault → cluster reconverges to desired within ≤ 60 s after fault clears
no double-run leader-election + binding exclusivity: 0 duplicate Pods, 0 double-bind across all chaos runs
fuzz 0 crashes / panics / OOM over CI budget (≥ 5 min/target, ≥ 10⁷ execs)
property 0 falsifying cases over ≥ 10⁴ proptest iterations/property
scale ≥ 1000 apiserver req/s; watch fan-out ≥ 1000 streams; schedule ≥ 100 Pods/s @100 nodes; reconcile p99 < 200 ms

14.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Expected
e2e/deploy_scale_rollout.yaml Deployment → 3 Pods Running; scale 3→10 converges; rolling-update keeps ≥ maxUnavailable healthy
chaos/kill_apiserver.toml apiserver killed mid-reconcile → controllers retry on restart, no resource corruption
chaos/partition_store.toml store partition → writes 503; on heal, watch resumes gap-free or 410→relist
fuzz/corpus/codec/*.json seed valid + truncated/over-deep/dup-key/NaN objects → decode returns Err, never panic
props/watch_gapfree.rs random op+compact stream → emitted Revisions strictly increasing, no gaps below compaction floor
Malformed bad_compose_missing_kubelet.yaml → harness fails fast w/ clear error; bench_baseline_corrupt.json → gate errors, not silent pass

14.6 Pitfalls

  • Flaky e2e from fixed sleeps → root cause: async convergence timing. Fix: wait_until polling on watch/status, never sleep; debug via collected per-component logs + final cluster dump.
  • Chaos false "double-run" → actually a stale informer cache reading pre-fault Revision. Debug: log resourceVersion at reconcile entry; confirm cache re-list after 410.
  • Fuzz finds JSON recursion limit stack overflow on deeply-nested objects → enforce max decode depth in orc-api codec, not a fuzz allowlist.
  • Watch property fails only after compaction → compaction floor not reflected in start-rev validation; assert 410 path is exercised, not silently dropped.
  • Docker-compose port races (apiserver not ready) → Cluster::up must health-poll /readyz before returning, else first scenario flakes.
  • Bench gate false regressions from noisy CI runners → use Criterion's noise threshold + median-of-N; pin CPU governor / isolate runner.

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • Bench isolation: run scale benches on dedicated runner, taskset-pinned, perf governor; report p50/p99/p999 not mean.
  • Watch fan-out bench: 1000 concurrent watchers on one type → measure apiserver memory + per-event broadcast latency; target < 50 ms p99 propagation.
  • Scheduling bench: 1000 unscheduled Pods @100 nodes → throughput + per-Pod filter+score latency; guard against O(pods×nodes×plugins) blowup.
  • Store bench: WAL fsync batching under concurrent CAS Txns; report write p99 + watch catch-up lag after compaction.

14.8 Edge Cases

  • Node failure mid-rolling-update: eviction + reschedule must not violate maxUnavailable; assert.
  • All kubelets partitioned simultaneously → all Nodes NotReady; on heal, no mass-reschedule thrash (respect grace + backoff).
  • apiserver restart with in-flight WATCH at compacted Revision → client gets 410, relists, no missed delta.
  • Two leaders briefly during election flap → binding exclusivity still holds (CAS on node_name rejects second bind).
  • Empty cluster (0 kubelets) → Pods stay Pending, scheduler emits FailedScheduling Event, no crash.

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Recovery
e2e scenario hang wait_until timeout dump cluster state + logs; fail with diff (desired vs observed)
chaos non-convergence post-heal poll exceeds 60 s mark fault non-recoverable; bisect which component stuck
fuzz crash libFuzzer artifact minimize → add to corpus → file regression test → fix codec/protocol
property falsification proptest shrunk case persist seed in proptest-regressions/; reproduce deterministically
bench regression gate diff > threshold block merge; attach flamegraph; identify hot path

14.10 Operational Notes

  • CI matrix: linux-real (full orc-runtime, namespaces/cgroups v2) gates everything; macOS/CI uses stub runtime → e2e runtime scenarios skipped (annotated), not silently passing.
  • Fuzz corpora committed under fuzz/corpus/; nightly long-fuzz job (≥ 1 h/target) separate from PR budget; new crashes auto-file issues.
  • benchmarks/baseline.json updated only via reviewed PR with justification; REPORT.md regenerated each release tag.
  • "Done" gate = all of {e2e green, chaos converged, 0 fuzz crashes, properties pass, scale within budget}; only then may a future phase append the — Rust ✅ tag. Cross-link 42 (Raft HA) chaos (multi-apiserver partition) lands when 42 integrates.

Threat Model

Scope & Method

STRIDE per trust boundary; each threat row: vector → mitigation → detection/telemetry → recovery. Trust boundaries (section 3, restated as adversary edges):

TB Boundary Untrusted side Crown asset behind it
TB-1 client/orctl → orc-apiserver external clients, RBAC subjects full object model + write authority
TB-2 orc-kubelet → control plane node agents (may be compromised) other nodes' objects, all Secrets
TB-3 container/workload → node kernel image + process running in Pod host kernel, other Pods, kubelet creds
TB-4 anyone → orc-store direct store socket / disk all Secrets, all spec/status — total compromise
TB-5 image registry → orc-runtime supply chain code executed as root-in-sandbox

Assumptions: store disk + WAL are on a host an attacker may image-copy; clients reach apiserver over a routable network; node-to-node is not implicitly trusted. Out of scope (NON-GOALS): hardware side-channels, hypervisor escape below the kernel, multi-tenant guarantees beyond namespace+RBAC+quota.

T1 — Unauthorized API access (TB-1, Spoofing/Elevation)
Vector unauthenticated/over-privileged REST/WATCH calls; impersonation; token replay
Mitigation TLS 1.3 mandatory on the apiserver listener; authn chain = client-cert (CN→user, O→groups) or bearer/ServiceAccount token (Phase 12); authz = RBAC Role/RoleBinding/ClusterRole evaluated per (verb, resource, namespace, name); deny-by-default; --anonymous-auth=false
Detection/telemetry structured audit log (one event per request: user, groups, verb, resource, RV, decision, sourceIP, responseCode); counters apiserver_authn_failures_total, apiserver_authz_denied_total{verb,resource}; alert on denial-rate spike or auth from new sourceIP
Recovery rotate/revoke compromised cert or token; rotate the SA token-signing key (invalidates all SA tokens → forces re-auth); tighten RBAC binding; the audit log is the forensic timeline

Invariant: no write path bypasses authn+authz — Registry handlers are reachable only after the auth middleware sets the request UserInfo.

T2 — Workload escaping container isolation (TB-3, Elevation)

Vector: malicious process in a Pod abuses a kernel/runc/namespace/cgroup bug to escape the sandbox → host root → kubelet creds + other Pods.

Defense-in-depth (orc-runtime OciSpec, all default-on):

Control Default Effect
dropped capabilities drop ALL, add back only requested no CAP_SYS_ADMIN, no mknod, etc.
seccomp profile RuntimeDefault (deny dangerous syscalls) shrinks kernel attack surface
user namespaces container UID 0 → unprivileged host UID host-root escape lands as nobody
read-only rootfs opt-in per container, recommended no tamper of image layers at runtime
no_new_privs set blocks setuid privilege gain
cgroups v2 limits enforced (cpu/mem/pids) escape can't fork-bomb the node
pivot_root + masked /proc always hides host paths

Privileged/hostPath/hostNetwork/hostPID Pods are gated behind admission policy (Phase 12), denied for non-system namespaces.

  • Detection/telemetry: seccomp-kill audit events; unexpected syscall-denied counts per Pod; runtime RuntimeError on spec-violating create; node-level eBPF anomaly hook (deferred).
  • Recovery: kubelet kills+restarts per RestartPolicy/backoff; Node-lifecycle cordon+drain the suspect node; quarantine image digest.
  • Deferred hardening (note, NON-GOAL for Phase 1): stronger isolation via a gVisor/Kata-style sandboxed runtime is a pluggable ContainerRuntime impl, not built initially — runc-like + seccomp + userns is the baseline.

T3 — Compromised node/kubelet forging objects or reading foreign Secrets (TB-2, Elevation/Info-disclosure)

Threat: a node's kubelet credential is the most-exposed control-plane credential (sits on every untrusted machine). A stolen kubelet token must NOT grant cluster-wide read/write.

Control Rule
Node authorizer a kubelet identity (system:node:<nodeName>, group system:nodes) may only GET/WATCH objects scheduled to <nodeName> and the Secrets/ConfigMaps referenced by those Pods
NodeRestriction admission a kubelet may only modify its own Node object + the pod.status of Pods bound to it; cannot set pod.spec.node_name (no self-scheduling), cannot edit other Nodes, cannot escalate its own labels into restricted ranges
single-writer kubelet is sole writer of pod.status for its Pods (KEY-ALGO) — forging another node's status is rejected by the authorizer scope
  • Detection/telemetry: audit events where a system:node subject touches an out-of-scope object (denied → alert immediately, this is always anomalous); noderestriction_denied_total{nodeName}.
  • Recovery: revoke the node's cert/token, cordon+drain+delete the Node object (cascades Pod eviction); the Node lease expiry already marks it NotReady (T-node-liveness).

T4 — Secret exfiltration (TB-2/TB-4, Information disclosure)

Layer Mitigation
at rest Secret values encrypted before write to orc-store (AES-256-GCM, per-object DEK wrapped by a KEK; envelope encryption) — see at-rest analysis below
in transit TLS on apiserver + store sockets; Secrets only sent to a node whose Pod references them (Node authorizer scope)
authz RBAC on secrets resource is deny-by-default; get/list/watch secrets is a high-privilege grant, separated from general read
logging redaction: Secret.data and Authorization/token headers never logged; audit log records that a Secret was accessed, never its value; structured logger has a Secret-typed field that prints <redacted>
projected tokens/secrets mounted as tmpfs projected volumes (RAM-backed, never on node disk)
  • Detection: alert on any list secrets across all namespaces by a non-system subject; count Secret reads per subject.
  • Recovery: rotate the leaked Secret's value (bump RV → all consuming Pods re-projected on next sync), rotate the KEK (triggers re-encrypt of all Secrets at next compaction-driven rewrite).

T5 — Resource-exhaustion DoS via the API (TB-1, DoS)

Sub-vector Mitigation Cap (target)
huge object body request body size limit ≤ 1.5 MiB / object (etcd-parity)
list bomb (unpaginated all-namespaces list) mandatory pagination over the limit ListOptions.limit ≤ 500, continue-token required beyond
watch storm (N clients open WATCH) per-apiserver concurrent-watch cap + per-user cap global ≤ 50k watches; per-user ≤ 1k
write flood per-client rate limit (token bucket) at the auth layer e.g. 50 QPS / 100 burst per subject
object-count explosion ResourceQuota per namespace (pods/secrets/cpu/mem) per-namespace, admission-enforced
expensive label/field selectors server-side selector eval bounded; reject regex-style abuse —
  • Telemetry: apiserver_request_duration_seconds (p99 target read < 50 ms, write < 100 ms under load), apiserver_inflight_requests, apiserver_watch_count, quota-rejection counters.
  • Recovery: shed load via priority+fairness queues (system/leader-election traffic prioritized over bulk list); reject with 429 + Retry-After; watch caps return 429 so clients back off rather than the apiserver OOMing.

T6 — Malicious image / supply chain (TB-5, Tampering/Elevation)

Control Mechanism
digest pinning resolve image:tag → image@sha256:… at admission; record digest in pod.status; ImageStore verifies layer digests on unpack (content-addressed)
signature verification admission/runtime checks a registry/cosign-style signature against a configured trust root before pull (Phase 12 hook)
registry allowlist admission denies images outside the allowed registry prefixes
pull-time integrity OCI manifest + layer digests checked; mismatch → RuntimeError, image rejected
  • Detection: audit events for image admitted by digest; counter image_verify_failures_total{registry}; alert on unsigned/unknown-registry pull attempt.
  • Recovery: add digest to a denylist; the level-triggered ReplicaSet/Deployment controllers re-create Pods which now fail admission → workload halts safely rather than running a bad image.

T7 — Admission / webhook bypass or poisoned mutating webhook (TB-1, Tampering)

Threats: (a) a request reaching the Registry without passing the admission chain; (b) a mutating admission webhook itself compromised → injects sidecars/escalated specs; (c) webhook unavailable → fail-open lets bad objects through.

  • Mitigation: admission is an in-line, non-bypassable stage of the write path (Admission::admit runs inside the Registry before persist — no handler writes to orc-store without it); webhook endpoints over TLS with pinned CA; fail-closed for security-relevant webhooks (failurePolicy: Fail → request rejected if webhook down) while a tightly-scoped allowlist of non-critical webhooks may fail-open; mutating webhooks run before validating webhooks, and a final built-in validation re-checks invariants the webhook could have violated.
  • Detection: audit records the ordered list of admission plugins/webhooks applied + the resulting object diff; admission_webhook_latency, admission_webhook_fail_total{policy}.
  • Recovery: disable the poisoned webhook config (it is itself an RBAC-protected object); reconcile re-evaluates existing objects on next resync.

T8 — Node liveness spoofing / lease abuse (TB-2, DoS/Tampering)

Vector: an attacker holds a Node Lease to mask a dead node, or floods lease keepalives. Mitigation: lease keepalive authorized by the Node authorizer (only the node's own lease); a missed lease TTL → Node-lifecycle controller marks NotReady then evicts (KEY-ALGO). Telemetry: node_lease_renew_failures_total, lease-age gauge. Recovery: eviction + reschedule of the dead node's Pods onto healthy nodes.

T9 — Store compromise = total cluster compromise (TB-4, the crown jewels)

orc-store holds every object including all Secrets, all spec, the SA-signing material's consumers. Direct store access == cluster-admin + plaintext secrets. This is the single highest-severity boundary.

Control Mechanism
network access control store socket bound to localhost / mTLS-only; only orc-apiserver may dial it — controllers/scheduler/kubelet NEVER touch the store directly, all go through the apiserver (architecture invariant)
at-rest encryption Secrets (min.) encrypted via envelope encryption; option to encrypt full keyspace; KEK held outside the data disk
WAL/disk integrity WAL checksummed per record (torn-write detection, see Failure Modes & Recovery); periodic snapshot integrity check
audit every store-mutating Txn traceable back to an apiserver audit event by Revision ↔ resourceVersion
  • Detection: any store connection from a non-apiserver identity → immediate alert; checksum mismatch on WAL replay → halt + page.
  • Recovery: restore from an encrypted snapshot at a known-good Revision; rotate KEK + all Secrets (assume plaintext disclosure if the data disk leaked); rotate all credentials persisted as objects.

T10 — Operator mistakes (TB-1, human, highest real-world frequency)

Mistake Guardrail
apply to wrong cluster orctl shows current context/server + requires --context match; destructive verbs print target server
delete namespace (cascades everything) finalizers on Namespace block deletion until child GC completes; orctl delete on a namespace requires --confirm + typed name
accidental mass delete orctl delete of >N objects or a whole collection requires explicit --all + confirmation prompt
stale-spec overwrite optimistic concurrency: apply carries resourceVersion → 409 on conflict, not silent clobber

Detection: audit log of delete verbs with object counts. Recovery: finalizers + GC ordering give a window to abort; restore deleted critical objects from a store snapshot.

Privacy / Secret-at-Rest Analysis

  • Data classes in the store: Secrets + SA tokens (highest), ConfigMaps (often config incl. semi-sensitive), object spec/status + labels/annotations (may leak topology/intent), audit log (records who-did-what — sensitive itself).
  • At-rest model: the data disk + WAL are treated as potentially attacker-readable (stolen disk image). Therefore Secret data is never written in plaintext; DEK-per-object, KEK envelope, KEK outside the disk. ConfigMaps default plaintext (documented: do not put secrets in ConfigMaps).
  • In-memory exposure: decrypted Secrets live transiently in apiserver memory + the consuming node's tmpfs projected volume; never in node persistent disk, never in logs (T4 redaction).
  • Blast radius if disk leaks (no encryption): every Secret + token → full cluster + downstream-system compromise → mandatory full credential rotation. With encryption + external KEK: disk leak yields ciphertext only; blast radius reduced to "rotate KEK if KEK also leaked."
  • Telemetry & retention: audit log shipped off-node, append-only, retained ≥ 90 d for forensics; PII/Secret values never enter it.

Threat → Phase Traceability

Threat Primarily addressed in
T1 authn/authz/audit Phase 12 (§23), apiserver auth chain (Phase 3 §14 hooks)
T2 isolation Phase 8 (§19) runtime; admission gates Phase 12 (§23)
T3 Node authorizer/NodeRestriction Phase 12 (§23) + kubelet scope Phase 7 (§18)
T4 secret exfil Phase 11 (§22) + Phase 12 (§23)
T5 API DoS Phase 3 (§14) limits + Phase 12 (§23) ResourceQuota/LimitRange
T6 supply chain Phase 8 (§19) ImageStore + Phase 12 (§23) admission
T7 admission/webhook Phase 3 (§14) Admission + Phase 12 (§23) webhooks
T8 lease abuse Phase 5 (§16) Node-lifecycle
T9 store compromise Phase 1 (§12) store + Phase 11/12 (§22/§23) encryption
T10 operator error Phase 12 (§23) confirmation; finalizers Phase 2/5 (§13/§16)

Failure Modes & Recovery

Failure taxonomy for the orchestrator. Severity scale: S1 total control-plane outage / data loss / split-brain; S2 workload-impacting; S3 degraded / self-healing. Each class: symptoms → root cause → detection → automatic recovery → manual recovery → operator actions → forensic artifacts → long-term mitigation. Highest severity: store loss (S1), split-brain (S1), reconcile non-convergence (S1/S2) — covered last with full trace/debug methodology.

Failure class summary

# Class Sev Blast radius Auto-recovers? RTO target
F1 orc-apiserver down S2 reads/writes blocked, workloads keep running yes (restart) < 30 s
F2 orc-store corruption / quorum loss S1 entire control plane partial (Raft) minutes–hours
F3 stuck / hot-looping Reconciler S2/S3 one resource kind yes (backoff) self
F4 leader-election split-brain S1 double-acting controllers yes (invariants) seconds
F5 Node NotReady S2 one node's pods yes (reschedule) grace+sched
F6 Pod stuck Pending S3 one workload no (needs capacity) manual
F7 Pod stuck Terminating S3 one object no (finalizer) manual
F8 watch lag / 410 storm S2/S3 controller caches yes (relist) self
F9 kubelet / runtime failure S2/S3 one node / pod yes (backoff) self
F10 clock skew (lease/cert) S2 leases, TLS, tokens no manual
F11 reconcile non-convergence S1/S2 drifted resources NO (bug) manual+fix

F1 — orc-apiserver down (S2)

  • Symptoms: orctl → connection refused / 503; controllers + scheduler log watch disconnects; kubelet status writes fail; running pods keep running (node agents are level-triggered, cache-backed).
  • Root cause: panic, OOM, port exhaustion, TLS cert expiry, store backend unreachable, deploy crash-loop.
  • Detection: /healthz, /readyz probe fail; client 503/ECONNREFUSED rate; watch-disconnect count spike across all components.
  • Automatic recovery: restart (supervisor/systemd) → re-attach to orc-store (state intact — store is sole truth); clients reconnect with exponential backoff + jitter; controllers/kubelet relist from last resourceVersion then re-watch (idempotent).
  • Manual recovery: if crash-loop on a poison object → start with admission disabled / --read-only to inspect; roll back binary; rotate expired cert.
  • Operator actions: check store reachability first (apiserver-down is often store-down in disguise); verify no quorum loss (→ F2).
  • Forensic artifacts: apiserver stderr + panic backtrace; last N request audit log; readyz history; goroutine/task dump.
  • Long-term mitigation: ≥3 apiserver replicas behind LB (stateless — all read/write through store); cert auto-rotation alarm at T-30d; admission timeout caps; request concurrency limiter (max-inflight read/write) to prevent OOM.

F2 — orc-store corruption / quorum loss (S1, gravest)

  • Symptoms: every write 500/timeout; watches stall; apiserver readyz red; total control-plane freeze (workloads continue but no reconciliation, no scheduling, no status updates).
  • Root cause: torn WAL write / fsync gap on power loss; disk full; bit-rot in keyspace pages; Raft quorum loss (majority members down/partitioned → no leader, no commits); compaction bug truncating live revisions.
  • Detection: WAL CRC mismatch on replay; Revision monotonicity check fails; Raft "no leader" / commit-index stall; range read returns checksum error.
  • Automatic recovery: WAL replay reconstructs keyspace to last fsync'd Revision on restart; with Raft backend (cross-link 42) a follower with the longest log is elected, lagging members re-sync via snapshot; single-node has no auto-recovery beyond WAL replay.
  • Manual recovery: restore from latest periodic snapshot + WAL tail (orctl store restore --snapshot <rev>); rebuild quorum by removing dead members + adding fresh ones (never add a member with stale/conflicting log → split log); if keyspace unrecoverable → restore snapshot, accept loss back to snapshot Revision, controllers re-derive observed state by relisting actual (nodes report pod status; ReplicaSet re-counts).
  • Operator actions: NEVER force-write into a possibly-partitioned Raft cluster; quiesce writers (stop controllers/scheduler) before restore to avoid clobbering restored revisions.
  • Forensic artifacts: WAL segments + CRC report; Raft log dump (term, index, commit) per member; snapshot manifest; compact history (last compacted Revision must be < oldest live watch start_rev).
  • Long-term mitigation: fsync on WAL append before ack; per-page CRC; off-cluster snapshot every 5 min + WAL archival (point-in-time restore); Raft with ≥3 (ideally 5) members across failure domains; compaction guard — refuse to compact below min(active watch start_rev); periodic corruption scrub.

F3 — stuck / hot-looping Reconciler (S2/S3)

  • Symptoms: WorkQueue depth grows unbounded or a key requeues thousands/sec; reconcile error rate high; CPU pegged on one controller; object never reaches steady state.
  • Root cause: reconcile that never returns Reconcile::Done (always Requeue); transient error treated as terminal; missing idempotency (creates duplicate children each pass); two controllers fighting over one field (→ also F4/F11); panic caught + re-enqueued at full rate.
  • Detection: workqueue_depth{kind}, workqueue_requeue_rate, reconcile_errors_total, reconcile_duration p99; same key dequeued > N times in window.
  • Automatic recovery: RateLimiter exponential backoff (when() grows per failed key) caps hot-loop CPU; forget() on success resets; max-retry → drop + Event so it stops thrashing.
  • Manual recovery: identify hot key from metrics → inspect object + ownerRefs; fix the spec or remove the offending object; deploy controller fix.
  • Operator actions: temporarily pause the controller (leader-election lease release) to break the loop without restarting the whole control plane.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-key reconcile trace (see methodology); WorkQueue add/get/forget log; Events on the object; controller flame graph.
  • Long-term mitigation: enforce Done/RequeueAfter discipline in code review; bounded retry → terminal Event; property test: reconcile is idempotent (running twice ≡ once) and converges (Phase 14).

F4 — leader-election split-brain (S1)

  • Symptoms: two orc-controller (or scheduler) instances both active; double-created children (2× pods per ReplicaSet); conflicting writes; 409 churn.
  • Root cause: leader lease not renewed in time (GC pause-free in Rust, but clock skew / store stall) yet old leader still acting → two believe they hold the lease; network partition between old leader and store.
  • Detection: leader-lease holder flaps; > 1 instance emitting "I am leader"; duplicate-child Events; 409 conflict rate spike.
  • Automatic recovery — the safety net is the invariants, not the election: even with two actors, optimistic-concurrency CAS + exclusive binding prevent corruption — only one CAS on a given resourceVersion wins; the loser gets 409, re-reads, finds the work done, no-ops. Scheduler binding writes pod.spec.node_name exactly once via CAS Txn → a Pod binds to exactly one Node even under two schedulers. Lease-fencing: a controller must re-verify lease ownership before each write batch and abort on loss.
  • Manual recovery: kill the stale instance; if duplicate children created, GC reconciles ReplicaSet back to N (excess pods deleted by ownerRef + replica count).
  • Operator actions: verify lease TTL > 2× max store write latency; confirm clocks (→ F10).
  • Forensic artifacts: lease object mod history (holder, renewTime, Revision); 409 audit trail; duplicate-child Events.
  • Long-term mitigation: lease renew interval ≤ TTL/3; mandatory lease-fenced writes (carry expected lease Revision in the CAS); idempotent creation keyed by deterministic child name + ownerRef so a duplicate create is a 409 no-op, not a second child.

F5 — Node NotReady (S2)

  • Symptoms: Node status.conditions[Ready]=False; its pods get evicted + rescheduled; brief workload disruption.
  • Root cause: kubelet crash / node reboot / network partition (node healthy but unreachable); Lease keepalive missed.
  • Detection: Node Lease TTL expiry (no lease_keepalive within TTL) → Node-lifecycle controller flips Ready=False.
  • Automatic recovery: after grace period, controller taints node.kubernetes.io/unreachable:NoExecute → pods evicted → rescheduled onto healthy nodes (respecting PodDisruptionBudget, Phase 13).
  • Classic hazard — false-positive eviction from partition: node + pods are actually fine but unreachable → mass eviction + reschedule = double-running workloads + churn.
  • Manual recovery: restore node connectivity; kubelet re-registers + renews lease; stale pods on the returned node are reconciled/killed (they no longer match desired).
  • Operator actions: tune eviction grace vs partition frequency; cap eviction rate (--node-eviction-rate) so a network blip doesn't evict the whole cluster.
  • Forensic artifacts: Lease renewTime history; Node condition transition timestamps + Revision; eviction Events.
  • Long-term mitigation: rate-limited + zone-aware eviction — if a large fraction of nodes go NotReady at once, assume partition not node death → stop evicting (secondary-mode); kubelet-side self-fencing (stop pods if it can't reach apiserver) to bound double-running.

F6 — Pod stuck Pending (Unschedulable, S3)

  • Symptoms: PodPhase=Pending indefinitely; condition PodScheduled=False, reason=Unschedulable.
  • Root cause: no node passes all FilterPlugins — insufficient allocatable CPU/mem, nodeSelector/affinity unmatched, untolerated taints, no node in required topology.
  • Detection: scheduler records Unschedulable Event + per-predicate failure counts ("3 Insufficient cpu, 2 node(s) didn't match selector"); SchedulingQueue backoff/unschedulable subqueue depth.
  • Automatic recovery: pod stays in unschedulable queue; retried when cluster state changes (node added, pod freed, taint removed) — level-triggered, no manual poke needed once capacity appears.
  • Manual recovery: add nodes / scale; relax nodeSelector/affinity; remove blocking taint; lower resource requests.
  • Forensic artifacts: scheduler predicate-failure breakdown per node; Pod Events; node allocatable snapshot.
  • Long-term mitigation: cluster-autoscaler hook (out of scope, note as cross-link); preemption (Phase 6 optional) to evict lower-priority pods.

F7 — Pod stuck Terminating (S3)

  • Symptoms: deletionTimestamp set but object never disappears; phase shows Terminating.
  • Root cause: a finalizer never removed (controller down / buggy / external dependency unmet); kubelet can't kill container (stuck runtime, uninterruptible D-state process); volume detach hang.
  • Detection: object age since deletionTimestamp > threshold while finalizers non-empty; GC blocked on blockOwnerDeletion.
  • Automatic recovery: none — finalizers are an intentional block.
  • Manual recovery: fix/restart the finalizer's owning controller; as last resort force-remove the finalizer (orctl patch ... finalizers=[]) — DANGER: skips cleanup, may leak resources (volumes, cloud LBs); document before doing.
  • Operator actions: identify which finalizer is stuck + which controller owns it before forcing.
  • Forensic artifacts: object finalizers + ownerReferences; owning-controller reconcile log; runtime stop-container error.
  • Long-term mitigation: finalizer timeout + alerting; idempotent finalizer cleanup; kubelet force-kill (SIGKILL) after grace + cgroup freeze to escape D-state.

F8 — watch lag / 410 Gone storm (S2/S3)

  • Symptoms: controllers reconcile on stale cache; 410 Gone (Expired) returned to watchers → mass simultaneous relist → apiserver + store load spike (thundering herd).
  • Root cause: watcher's start_rev fell below the last compacted Revision (slow consumer, long disconnect, aggressive compaction); apiserver watch buffer overflow drops a slow client.
  • Detection: watch_lag_seconds (now − last delivered Revision); 410_total rate; relist QPS spike; store revision − slowest-watch revision gap.
  • Automatic recovery: on 410 the client relists (LIST at current rev) then re-watches from that rev — gap-free invariant preserved; staggered/jittered relist backoff to avoid herd.
  • Manual recovery: raise watch buffer; slow compaction cadence; scale apiserver read replicas.
  • Forensic artifacts: per-watch lag histogram; compaction Revision timeline vs watch start_revs; dropped-watch log.
  • Long-term mitigation: compaction guard refuses to compact below min(active watch start_rev) (also F2 mitigation); periodic Bookmark events keep idle watchers' resourceVersion advanced so they don't fall behind compaction window; bounded per-watch send buffer + backpressure.

F9 — kubelet / runtime failures (S2/S3)

Sub-failure Symptom Detection Auto-recovery Mitigation
Image pull fail container Waiting:ImagePullBackOff pull_image err; backoff timer exponential backoff retry registry mirror, pre-pull, creds check
CrashLoopBackOff container restart count climbs exit≠0 within window RestartPolicy + crash backoff (cap 5 min) readiness gate, fix image/cmd
cgroup OOM-kill container killed, Reason=OOMKilled cgroup v2 memory.events oom_kill++ restart per policy raise mem limit, fix leak, LimitRange
Sandbox/CNI fail Pod stuck ContainerCreating setup_pod_network err retry sandbox create CNI plugin health, IPAM exhaustion alarm
Runtime hang status() times out CRI call deadline kubelet force-kill + recreate runtime watchdog, separate sandbox per pod
  • Status authority: only the node's kubelet writes pod.status (single-writer rule) → no status race even during recovery.
  • Forensic artifacts: container last-state (exit code, OOM flag, finishedAt); kubelet PodWorker sync log; runtime + image-store logs; cgroup memory.events.

F10 — clock-skew lease/cert failure (S2)
