  • Symptoms: leases expire early/late → spurious NotReady (F5) or split-brain (F4); TLS handshake fails (cert not yet valid / expired); ServiceAccount token exp/nbf rejected.
  • Root cause: node clock drift, NTP down, VM live-migration time jump.
  • Detection: cross-node timestamp deltas in lease renewTime vs apiserver receive time; TLS validation errors; token auth failures clustered by node.
  • Automatic recovery: none — time is exogenous.
  • Manual recovery: fix NTP/chrony; restart affected kubelet after sync.
  • Long-term mitigation: NTP-sync alarm (drift > 500 ms); lease TTL margin ≫ expected skew; cert validity with generous nbf skew window (±5 min); prefer monotonic clocks for internal timers, wall-clock only for cert/lease expiry.

F11 — reconcile non-convergence (S1/S2, highest severity bug class)

  • Definition: observed state never reaches desired despite a healthy queue (not stuck-looping, not erroring) — controller silently produces wrong/incomplete result. Worst because it can be invisible: no alarms fire, drift accumulates.
  • Symptoms: spec.replicas=5 but steady 4 running; Service has stale EndpointSlice; Deployment rollout wedged at N% forever; status.observedGeneration < metadata.generation persistently.
  • Root cause: reconcile reads stale cache + writes (lost update); off-by-one in surge/unavailable math; selector mismatch (adopts wrong/no pods); a code path that returns Done before the work is actually complete; two single-writers fighting one field.
  • Detection: generation vs status.observedGeneration lag; desired-vs-observed gauge per controller (e.g. replicaset_desired − replicaset_ready); convergence-time SLO breach.
  • Automatic recovery: NONE — requires a code fix. Resync re-runs the same buggy logic → no help.
  • Manual recovery: pinpoint via trace methodology below; patch object to unwedge; ship controller fix.
  • Forensic artifacts: reconcile trace (input cache snapshot → decision → writes → result); diff of desired vs observed at each resync; ownerRef/selector audit.
  • Long-term mitigation: property test in Phase 14 — for any spec, repeated reconcile converges to a fixpoint where observed==desired and is stable (the convergence + idempotence invariant); always set observedGeneration only after work completes so the lag metric is truthful.

Cross-cutting trace / debug methodology

For S1 classes (F2 store, F4 split-brain, F11 non-convergence) the diagnostic toolkit:

  • Reconcile traces — structured span per reconcile: {key, input_resourceVersion, cache_snapshot_rev, decision, writes[], result(Done/Requeue), cas_conflicts}. Replay to see why observed≠desired; a Done with residual drift localizes F11; repeated Requeue localizes F3.
  • Watch-lag metrics — watch_lag_seconds, per-watch start_rev vs store head Revision, 410_total; the slowest watch's start_rev vs last-compacted Revision is the F8/F2 early-warning.
  • Store revision inspection — orctl store inspect: current head Revision, per-key create_rev/mod_rev/version, last-compacted Revision, Raft term/commit-index per member, lease table (id, TTL, attached keys). Confirms CAS history (who wrote a field at which Revision → split-brain forensics) and compaction safety.
  • Decision tree: writes failing → is it apiserver (F1) or store (F2)? check store head Revision advancing. Drift present → is queue draining? yes+drift ⇒ F11 (bug), no ⇒ F3 (stuck). Duplicate children ⇒ F4, confirm via dual lease holders + 409 trail. Mass NotReady ⇒ F5, confirm partition vs death via fraction-of-nodes + clock skew (F10).
  • Invariant audit (run in chaos suite, Phase 14): (a) watch gap-freedom — no missing Revision in any stream; (b) exclusive binding — each Pod has ≤1 node_name ever; (c) CAS safety — no two writes commit at the same resourceVersion; (d) convergence — observed reaches desired and is stable. Violation of any (a)–(d) is an automatic S1.

Operational Readiness

Telemetry contract: every component (orc-apiserver, orc-scheduler, orc-controller, orc-kubelet, orc-store) exposes /metrics (Prometheus text exposition, OpenMetrics), /healthz (process up), /readyz (deps OK: store reachable, informers synced), /livez (event loop not wedged). All counters monotonic; histograms expose _bucket/_sum/_count; le buckets explicit (see below). Cardinality budget per component < 50k series → resource/verb labels bounded, never label by object name/uid.

Metrics (Prometheus exposition)

orc-apiserver:

Metric Type Labels Buckets / target
apiserver_request_total counter verb,resource,code —
apiserver_request_duration_seconds histogram verb,resource 1,2.5,5,10,25,50,100,250,500,1000ms
apiserver_request_inflight gauge verb(read|write) cap = max-inflight limiter
apiserver_watch_active gauge resource fan-out target ≤ 50k streams/node
apiserver_watch_event_lag_seconds histogram resource store mod_rev → client emit; p99 < 1s
apiserver_watch_bookmark_total counter resource —
apiserver_409_conflict_total counter resource optimistic-CC retries; spike → hot object
apiserver_410_gone_total counter resource compacted-watch relist; spike → compaction too aggressive
apiserver_admission_duration_seconds histogram webhook,phase p99 < 100ms

orc-store:

Metric Type Labels Note
store_revision gauge — current global Revision; flat → no writes
store_compacted_revision gauge — floor; store_revision - compacted = watchable window
store_put_duration_seconds histogram — WAL fsync incl.; p99 < 10ms
store_txn_duration_seconds histogram result(ok|else) CAS path
store_db_size_bytes gauge — keyspace + WAL; alert > 80% quota
store_keys_total gauge — live KV count
store_watch_streams gauge — server-side fan-out
store_lease_total gauge — active TTL leases (≈ node count)
store_compaction_duration_seconds histogram — runs every 5min / 1000 revs

Controllers (orc-controller, orc-scheduler share framework metrics):

Metric Type Labels

workqueue_adds_total counter controller

reconcile_errors_total counter controller

informer_resync_total counter controller,resource

orc-scheduler:

  • scheduler_attempt_total{result=scheduled|unschedulable|error} counter
  • scheduler_e2e_duration_seconds histogram (pop→Bind written); p99 < 100ms
  • scheduler_framework_duration_seconds{phase=filter|score|bind} histogram
  • scheduler_pending_pods{queue=active|backoff|unschedulable} gauge
  • scheduler_preemption_victims_total counter

orc-kubelet (per node):

  • kubelet_pod_start_duration_seconds histogram (bind-observed→Running); image-pull dominates; p99 < 10s warm, < 60s cold-pull
  • kubelet_running_pods / kubelet_running_containers gauge
  • kubelet_container_restarts_total{pod_uid…} → aggregate by reason(crashloop|oom|liveness) not uid
  • kubelet_node_lease_renew_age_seconds gauge; > TTL ⇒ eviction imminent
  • kubelet_pleg_relist_duration_seconds histogram (runtime state poll)
  • kubelet_volume_mount_duration_seconds histogram
  • node pressure gauges: node_cpu_alloc_ratio, node_mem_alloc_ratio, node_pod_alloc_ratio (requests/allocatable); node_image_fs_used_ratio

Tracing (tracing crate + OpenTelemetry)

  • tracing → tracing-opentelemetry → OTLP exporter; spans sampled (parent-based, 1% head + tail-keep on error/slow).
  • Trace correlation key = object uid; W3C traceparent propagated over REST + watch.
  • Span taxonomy (one per hop): apiserver.request{verb,resource,uid,rv} → store.txn{rev}; controller.reconcile{controller,uid,gen} → child apiserver.request writes; scheduler.schedule{uid} → filter/score/bind child spans; kubelet.pod_sync{uid} → runtime.pull_image/runtime.create_container/cni.setup_pod_network.
  • Causal chain assertable end-to-end: orctl apply → apiserver write (rev R) → controller reconcile → scheduler bind → kubelet sync → container Running, all sharing one trace via uid.
  • Span attrs: resource_version, generation, result, reconcile.requeue_after; errors record error=true + ReconcileError/RuntimeError variant.

Logging

  • tracing-subscriber JSON layer; fields: ts,level,component,target,uid,namespace,resource,verb,rv,trace_id,msg. Default info; per-module RUST_LOG.
  • Redaction: Secret.data, ServiceAccount tokens, client-cert keys, bearer tokens, Authorization headers NEVER logged. Secret objects log meta only (name/uid/keys), never values; a #[derive(Redact)] newtype renders data as <redacted:N bytes>. Admission/audit log records mutation diffs with Secret/ConfigMap values masked.
  • Audit log = separate stream: who(subject) / what(verb+resource+uid) / when / RV-before→after / decision(allow|deny+rule).

Incident response / runbooks

Symptom Triage order
apiserver 5xx store /readyz + store_put_duration (WAL fsync stall?) → request_inflight vs limiter (load/thundering relist?) → 410_gone spike (compaction → relist storm) → leader flap. Mitigate: raise inflight cap, throttle, scale read replicas.
pods stuck Pending scheduler_pending_pods{unschedulable} → last scheduler_attempt{result=unschedulable} reason → node pressure/alloc ratios (capacity) → taints vs tolerations / nodeSelector / affinity mismatch → scheduler leader alive + informers synced.
rollout stuck Deployment status: updated < desired? readiness probes failing (kubelet events) → maxUnavailable/maxSurge blocking surge (no schedulable nodes) → new ReplicaSet pods CrashLoop (container_restarts{reason=crashloop}) → rollback orctl rollout undo.
node NotReady kubelet_node_lease_renew_age → kubelet up? (/healthz) → apiserver reachable from node / network partition → PLEG relist stall (runtime hung) → store lease expired. Grace before eviction; respect PDB on drain.
watch clients relisting (410 storm) compaction interval too tight vs slow consumer → widen watchable window, slow compaction, fix lagging informer.
store db growing compaction not running / lease leak / large-object writes → force compact(rev), audit lease count vs nodes, defrag.

Deployment topology & lifecycle

HA control plane (3x, stacked or external store quorum):
  cp-1..cp-3:  orc-apiserver (active/active, LB)  +  orc-store (Raft, cross-link 42)
               orc-scheduler, orc-controller  (active/passive via leader election)
  LB → apiserver VIP (client-cert TLS)
worker-1..N:   orc-kubelet + orc-runtime + orc-net (per node; horizontally scalable)
  • Control-plane upgrade: rolling, one replica at a time; apiserver N/N+1 wire-compatible (versioned apiVersion, additive fields); scheduler/controller upgrade is leader-failover (passive upgraded first, then step down leader). Quorum (≥2/3 store) held throughout.
  • Component canary: deploy one apiserver/one controller at new version behind LB; watch apiserver_request error rate, reconcile_errors_total, conflict rate; promote or roll back on regression. Controllers idempotent + level-triggered ⇒ mixed-version safe; rollback = redeploy prior image, no state migration (object schema additive-only).
  • Node upgrade: cordon (mark unschedulable) → drain (evict respecting PDB) → upgrade kubelet/runtime → uncordon.
  • Store backup/restore: periodic snapshot (consistent at a Revision) + WAL archive to object storage; RPO ≤ snapshot interval (5min), RTO = restore + relist. Restore: stop apiservers → load snapshot → restart → controllers relist & reconcile to desired (self-healing). Test restore monthly; verify store_revision continuity + no orphaned children post-restore (GC re-runs).

Compatibility & Versioning

Compatibility surfaces, in trust/blast-radius order: (1) API objects · (2) WATCH+resourceVersion · (3) store on-disk format · (4) CRI/CNI/CSI-like traits · (5) orc- crate semver · (6) orctl↔apiserver skew · (7) MSRV · (8) kube parity scope.* Each has a distinct stability contract + break test in Phase 14 conformance.

API object compatibility

Aspect Rule
apiVersion grouping <group>/<version>, e.g. apps/v1, core/v1, scheduling/v1beta1. Group is the unit of versioning; kinds (Pod,Deployment,…) carried inside TypeMeta.api_version.
Additive-only evolution New fields added optional/defaulted. A field is never repurposed, retyped, or its semantics changed within a stable version. Removal of a stable field → new apiVersion.
Maturity ladder alpha (vNalphaM): may break/disappear any release, off by default, no round-trip guarantee. beta (vNbetaM): on by default, deprecation ≥2 minors before removal. stable (vN): never broken, never removed (only deprecated, kept forever).
Zero-value = default Absent/zero optional field MUST decode to the documented default; old clients omitting a new field get the default, never an error. Forward-compat for old writers.
Defaulting determinism Defaults applied by admission at write time, persisted; same input ⇒ same stored object across versions (golden-object fixtures pin this).

Multi-version conversion — objects stored at one storage version per group; served at every enabled version via a lossless conversion function.

// orc-api: per-group conversion is total + round-trip-lossless for stable<->stable
trait Convert<From, To> { fn convert(&self, from: From) -> Result<To, ConversionError>; }
// Invariant: convert(v1->v1beta1)->convert(v1beta1->v1) == identity for fields common to both.
// Fields present only in the newer version are stashed in ObjectMeta.annotations
// ("orc.io/conversion-stash/<field>") so a v(N-1) writer cannot silently drop newer data.
  • One storage version per group (annotated in the object's stored bytes); served versions converted on read/write.
  • Conversion is hub-and-spoke (internal hub type, no O(n²) pairwise) — same pattern as apimachinery.
  • Round-trip property test (Phase 14): decode(encode(o)) == o for every kind × served version; conversion fuzzed for panics/data-loss.

WATCH + resourceVersion contract

  • resourceVersion is an opaque, monotonically increasing token == store Revision at last write. Clients MUST NOT parse, arithmetic, or compare it except for equality/watch from RV. Opacity lets the store change Revision encoding without breaking clients.
  • WATCH from RV R streams every change with Revision > R, strict Revision order, gap-free, dup-free, plus periodic Bookmark(Revision) events. This ordering/gap-freedom is a frozen contract — informer cache correctness depends on it.
  • 410 Gone / Expired when R < compaction floor → client MUST relist (fresh RV) + re-watch. The 410-relist handshake is stable across all versions; clients code to it forever.
  • WatchEvent variant set (Put{kv,prev}, Delete, Bookmark) is additive-only; a new variant requires a watch protocol minor + clients negotiate via Accept/feature flag (unknown variant ⇒ client treats as relist trigger, never crash).

Store on-disk format

Component Contract
WAL records Versioned: magic(4) · format_version:u16 · .... Reader rejects unknown major, accepts known.
Snapshot Versioned header; snapshot = compacted keyspace at a Revision + lease table.
Upgrade direction Forward-only. Newer binary reads older WAL+snapshot (replays into current format); older binary refuses a newer format_version rather than corrupt. No automatic downgrade.
Migration Format bump ⇒ one-shot in-place rewrite on first open of new binary (orc-store --migrate dry-run available); idempotent, crash-safe (write-new-then-atomic-rename).
Revision continuity Migration preserves Revision monotonicity + create_rev/mod_rev/version per KeyValue — never resets, else watchers see false gaps.

CRI / CNI / CSI-like interface stability

  • ContainerRuntime (CRI-like) and NetworkPlugin (CNI-like) traits are the runtime/network compatibility surfaces — third-party runtimes/plugins implement them.
  • Additive-only: new methods MUST ship with a default impl (returning Err(RuntimeError::Unimplemented) / NetError::Unsupported) so existing impls keep compiling. Adding a required (non-defaulted) method = breaking → major bump.
  • Method signatures (run_sandbox, pull_image, create_container, start_container, stop_container, status; setup_pod_network, teardown_pod_network) are frozen within a major; semantics (e.g. stop_container honoring grace) are part of the contract and conformance-tested against the stub + runc-like impls.
  • CSI-like volume plugin trait is alpha (Phase 11 stub) → may break freely until promoted to beta.
  • Capability negotiation: a runtime reports supported features (runtime_info()); kubelet degrades gracefully (e.g. no cgroups v2 ⇒ refuse limits, don't silently ignore).

Crate API surface (semver)

  • All nine orc-* crates follow SemVer; 0.x = pre-1.0 (minor may break). Post-1.0: breaking ⇒ major.
  • Primary compatibility surfaces (traits): Store, Reconciler, ContainerRuntime, NetworkPlugin. Rules:
Change Verdict
Add defaulted trait method minor (compatible)
Add required (non-defaulted) trait method breaking → major
Add struct field to #[non_exhaustive] type minor
Add field to exhaustive public struct breaking
Add enum variant (e.g. PodPhase, WatchEvent, Reconcile) breaking unless #[non_exhaustive] (all are marked #[non_exhaustive])
Loosen a bound / widen a return error enum breaking (downstream exhaustive match)
  • cargo-semver-checks gate in CI blocks accidental breaks; intentional breaks require a CHANGELOG BREAKING: entry.
  • Re-exports: orc-api types re-exported by consumers are part of their surface — bumping orc-api major cascades.

orctl ↔ apiserver client/server skew

  • Policy mirrors kubectl: server supports clients at version N and N-1 (one minor of skew either direction tolerated for read paths). Client newer than server by >1 minor → warn + best-effort; server newer than client by >1 → client may miss newer fields (preserved via stash, not dropped).
  • Negotiation: client sends Accept: application/json; orc-api-version=…; server picks highest mutually-supported served version. No agreed version ⇒ explicit 406 Not Acceptable, never a silent mis-decode.
  • Unknown-field handling: client preserves unknown fields on apply (no clobber) so an N-1 orctl editing an object written by an N apiserver does not drop N-only fields.

MSRV policy

  • MSRV 1.78, edition 2021. MSRV bump = minor version bump, documented in CHANGELOG, gated by a CI job pinned to the floor toolchain.
  • MSRV raised only when a needed std/Tokio feature requires it; never raised casually within a patch release.

kube-compatibility differential parity scope

Conformance claimed against a single pinned Kubernetes minor (target: a fixed 1.x); parity is a differential subset, not a clone.

Conformant subset (claimed) Explicitly OUT of scope (non-goal)
core/v1 Pod,Service(ClusterIP/NodePort),Node,Namespace,ConfigMap,Secret,Event,EndpointSlice; apps/v1 Deployment,ReplicaSet CRDs / operator framework (Custom-resource hook only)
Declarative apply, label/field selectors, list+WATCH+bookmarks, optimistic concurrency Full RBAC matrix (minimal Role/RoleBinding/ClusterRole only)
Scheduling: resource-fit, nodeSelector, taints/tolerations, affinity; Bind Cloud-provider integration; Ingress; service mesh
Rolling update (maxSurge/maxUnavailable), Recreate, rollback Production CNI/CSI ecosystem (reference CNI + hostPath/emptyDir/projected only)
Lease-based node liveness; GC via ownerRefs/finalizers; PodDisruptionBudget drain Distributed etcd (single-node store; Raft HA hook = cross-link 42)
  • A conformance manifest (conformance/parity-<k8s-minor>.yaml) enumerates each claimed behavior + the e2e test asserting it; CI fails if a claimed row lacks a passing test.
  • Parity is behavioral, not wire-identical: object JSON is shaped like the pinned k8s minor (same field names/types for in-scope kinds) so kubectl get -o yaml against orc-apiserver is human-recognizable, but no guarantee a real kubelet/kubectl binary interoperates (out of scope).

Testing & Verification Strategy

Each test class binds to a §7 invariant (I-1..I-9). No test passes without asserting its invariant. CI budget: unit+integration <90s, property <5min, fuzz nightly, e2e <12min, soak weekly.

Invariant → test-class map

Inv Statement Primary class Asserting fixture
I-1 Reconcile idempotent + level-triggered property + unit prop_reconcile_idempotent
I-2 Watch ordered, gap-free, no dup from RV>R property + replay prop_watch_gapfree
I-3 Optimistic CC linearizable (CAS on RV) property (linearizability) prop_cas_linearizable
I-4 Binding exclusive (Pod binds once) property + integration prop_bind_exclusive
I-5 GC reachability (orphan ⇒ deleted; finalizer blocks) property prop_gc_reachable
I-6 Store crash-recovers to consistent prefix property + chaos prop_crash_prefix
I-7 kubelet sole writer of pod.status integration it_status_single_writer
I-8 Node lease loss ⇒ NotReady ⇒ evict deterministic-time + chaos dt_lease_expiry
I-9 Reconcile converges desired==observed under chaos chaos + e2e chaos_converge

Unit tests (per-component, no I/O, <10ms each)

  • orc-api codec: round-trip every kind (Pod/ReplicaSet/Deployment/Service/Node/Namespace/ConfigMap/Secret/Event/EndpointSlice) — decode(encode(x))==x; unknown-field preservation; apiVersion/kind discrimination; defaulting; resource_version/generation not client-settable. Golden JSON fixtures under orc-api/tests/golden/.
  • selector ops: per Requirement operator (In,NotIn,Exists,DoesNotExist,Gt,Lt) truth table; empty selector matches all; match_labels + match_expressions conjunction; field-selector parse + match. Table-driven.
  • orc-store ops: range/get/put/delete semantics; Revision strictly monotonic +1 per write; KeyValue create_rev/mod_rev/version correctness; Txn compare-and-set true/false branches; Lease TTL bookkeeping; compact removes ≤rev historical, keeps current.
  • scheduler plugins: each FilterPlugin (resource-fit, nodeSelector, taint/toleration, affinity) returns Feasible/Unschedulable per node fixture; each ScorePlugin returns 0..100 (boundary 0 and 100 asserted); weighted-sum tie-break determinism.
  • controller reconcile branches: per reconciler, one test per branch — ReplicaSet {under/over/at replica count, adopt orphan, orphan on selector-miss}; Deployment {RollingUpdate surge/unavail clamp, Recreate, pause, rollback}; GC {owner deleted ⇒ child delete, finalizer present ⇒ block}; Node {lease ok, lease lost}; EndpointSlice {pod ready/notready membership}. Each asserts Reconcile variant (Done/Requeue/RequeueAfter).

Integration tests (in-process, real store, no containers)

Wire components over a Tokio in-proc loopback orc-apiserver + in-mem orc-store. No network sockets — direct Store/Registry calls.

Test Wiring Asserts
it_apiserver_store apiserver→store CRUD round-trip; 409 on stale RV; WATCH delivers Put/Delete/Bookmark; 410 Gone post-compaction
it_controller_apiserver RS controller + apiserver create RS ⇒ N Pods appear; delete 1 Pod ⇒ recreated; scale ⇒ converge
it_scheduler_apiserver scheduler + apiserver unscheduled Pod ⇒ node_name bound exactly once (I-4); no feasible node ⇒ stays Pending + Event
it_status_single_writer kubelet-stub + controllers only kubelet RV-writes pod.status; controller write to status field ⇒ rejected/overwritten (I-7)
it_informer_cache Reflector→informer→Lister Lister reflects store within resync; relist on 410; WorkQueue dedups

Deterministic-time harness

Virtual clock injected via trait Clock { fn now()->Time; async fn sleep(Duration); }; tests advance time explicitly. Makes RequeueAfter, resync, lease TTL, crash-backoff, probe periods reproducible — zero wall-clock flake.

let clock = TestClock::new();           // frozen at t0
harness.spawn_node_controller(&clock);
clock.advance(LEASE_TTL + grace);       // step past expiry deterministically
assert_eq!(node.status.conditions.ready(), False);   // dt_lease_expiry, I-8
  • Tokio start_paused=true + injected Clock; no real tokio::time::sleep.
  • Covers: lease expiry→NotReady→eviction grace (I-8), exponential backoff schedule (10ms·2^n cap 5min), RateLimiter::when curve, resync cadence, probe initialDelay/period/failureThreshold.

Property tests (proptest; shrinking enabled)

Property Model Invariant
prop_reconcile_idempotent reconcile(reconcile(s))==reconcile(s); random spec/status I-1
prop_reconcile_converge apply random ops, run reconcile to fixpoint ⇒ observed==desired I-1/I-9
prop_watch_gapfree random put/delete/compact; watch from random R ⇒ events strictly Revision-increasing, no gap >1 vs live range, no dup, Bookmark monotone I-2
prop_cas_linearizable concurrent CAS updaters; linearizability check (Knossos-style history) ⇒ exactly-one winner per RV, losers see 409 I-3
prop_bind_exclusive concurrent schedulers bind same Pod ⇒ exactly one node_name write commits I-4
prop_gc_reachable random owner forest + finalizers; delete roots ⇒ unreachable children deleted, finalized children retained until removed I-5
prop_crash_prefix inject crash at random WAL offset; recover ⇒ store == some committed prefix (no torn/partial record visible) I-6

Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz / libFuzzer)

Target Corpus seed Oracle
fuzz_codec golden JSON no panic; decode→encode→decode stable; no OOM
fuzz_watch_proto recorded watch frames no panic; RV monotonicity preserved; bounded alloc
fuzz_selector selector exprs parse never panics; match total function

CI budget: 5min/target/night; crashers auto-minimized → fuzz/corpus/, regression-pinned as unit tests.

Deterministic replay

Record real WatchStream frames (Put/Delete/Bookmark + Revision) to *.watchlog; replay through informer offline ⇒ deterministic Lister state. Used for I-2 regression + debugging field-reported watch bugs without a live cluster.

e2e (multi-node docker-compose)

docker-compose.e2e.yml: 1× orc-apiserver+orc-store, 1× orc-scheduler, 1× orc-controller, 3× orc-kubelet+orc-runtime (real runc, cgroups v2, reference CNI). Driven by orctl.

Scenario Steps Pass gate
deploy orctl apply Deployment n=5 5 Pods Running, spread across 3 nodes
scale orctl scale --replicas=10 converge to 10 ≤30s
rollout update image, RollingUpdate maxSurge=1 maxUnavailable=0 zero-downtime; old RS scaled to 0; history recorded
rollback orctl rollout undo prior image restored
service ClusterIP + curl VIP from pod round-robins healthy EndpointSlice; NotReady pod excluded
node-failure docker kill 1 kubelet Node NotReady ≤lease-TTL+grace; Pods rescheduled; converge (I-9)

Chaos testing

Fault injector kills/partitions components mid-workload; invariant monitor runs continuously; asserts eventual convergence + safety (never two owners, never two bindings, never status regress).

Fault Injection Assert
component kill SIGKILL apiserver/scheduler/controller leader re-elected; reconcile resumes; converge (I-9)
store partition netem drop on store RPC writers block/409, no split-brain commit; heal ⇒ consistent
store crash SIGKILL mid-write recover to consistent prefix (I-6)
lease expiry freeze kubelet heartbeat Node NotReady, eviction (I-8)
image pull fail runtime returns RuntimeError crash-backoff per policy, no busy-loop
RPC flake 5% inject RPC error retries idempotent; no duplicate Pods (I-1)

Soak / perf-regression

  • criterion benches gated in CI; >10% regression vs pinned baseline ⇒ fail.
  • Targets (assert): watch fan-out ≥10k streams; WATCH P99 <50ms under 1k writes/s; schedule rate ≥100 Pods/s; reconcile P99 <100ms at 10k objects; apiserver GET P99 <10ms; store put P99 <5ms; control-plane RSS <512MB @ 10k objects.
  • Weekly 24h soak: steady 1k-object churn ⇒ no RSS growth (leak gate), no Revision/compaction unbounded growth, no watch-stream leak.

Conformance ledger

conformance.toml — pass/fail/known-fail per feature vs pinned Kubernetes subset (v1.29 object schemas, watch semantics, RollingUpdate, GC, scheduling predicates). Generated table in CI artifact; known-fail entries require a tracked issue link. Phase-14 "Done" gate = zero unexplained fails.

Coverage

Line+branch via cargo-llvm-cov. Gate: ≥85% workspace lines, ≥90% on orc-store + orc-api + reconcile branches (correctness-critical). Excluded: stub runtime (non-Linux), generated codec. PR fails on coverage drop >1%.

Build & Release Engineering

Reproducibility

Artifact Pin Rationale
rust-toolchain.toml channel = "1.78", components = [rustfmt, clippy], targets = [x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu, aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu] MSRV == toolchain; CI + dev identical
Cargo.lock committed (workspace root) exact dep graph for the five binaries; bins pin, libs do not float
Build env SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH = commit time; --remap-path-prefix $PWD=/build; RUSTFLAGS="-C symbol-mangling-version=v0" byte-stable binaries; bit-for-bit reproducible per target triple
Vendoring cargo vendor snapshot tagged on release; .cargo/config.toml offline source air-gapped/audit rebuild from tag alone

Reproducibility gate (release): build twice on clean checkout -> sha256 of each binary must match.

Build / test matrix

.github/workflows/ci.yml — fan-out:

Job Toolchain Target Runs
test-stable stable x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu cargo test --workspace --all-features (real orc-runtime: namespaces, cgroups v2)
test-msrv 1.78 linux-gnu cargo test --workspace — MSRV regression guard
build-aarch64 stable aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu (cross) cargo build --release — multi-arch parity
stub-macos stable x86_64-apple-darwin cargo test -p orc-api -p orc-store -p orc-controller -p orc-scheduler (runtime-stub feature; no Linux syscalls)
lint stable linux cargo fmt --all --check + cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -D warnings
deny stable linux cargo deny check (advisories/bans/licenses/sources)
doc stable linux cargo doc --no-deps -D rustdoc::broken_intra_doc_links
  • Linux orc-runtime jobs run privileged (cgroups v2 unified hierarchy, user namespaces, pivot_root) — required for Phase 8 syscall-real tests; non-Linux/non-priv -> runtime-stub.
  • Feature flags: runtime-real (default, Linux) vs runtime-stub; raft-ha (off, cross-link 42 hook); ebpf-proxy vs userspace-proxy (orc-net).

Specialized workflows

Workflow Trigger Content Gate
fuzz.yml nightly + PR-label fuzz cargo fuzz run targets: api_codec (JSON Object round-trip), watch_protocol (WatchEvent stream decode), label_selector, oci_spec; 15 min/target new crasher -> fail; corpus cached in artifacts
bench.yml nightly + manual cargo bench (criterion): store put/range/watch-fanout, scheduler throughput, apiserver p99, reconcile latency regression > 10% vs baseline JSON -> fail
e2e.yml PR + nightly docker-compose up (apiserver + store + scheduler + controller + 2 kubelet nodes) -> orctl apply Deployment, scale, rolling update, kill-node eviction non-convergence in 120 s -> fail
release.yml tag v* reproducible build x2, sign, multi-arch images + binaries, crates.io publish, SBOM see Release

Property tests (run in test-stable, proptest): reconcile convergence/idempotence, watch gap-freedom (no gap, no dup, Revision-ordered), optimistic-CC safety (no lost update under concurrent CAS).

Supply chain + SBOM

  • cargo deny (deny.toml): deny RUSTSEC advisories; license allowlist (MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD/ISC); ban duplicate-version + yanked; sources = crates.io + pinned git only.
  • cargo auditable build — embeds dep tree in each binary; cargo audit bin orc-apiserver recovers SBOM post-hoc.
  • SBOM emitted as CycloneDX (cargo cyclonedx) per release; attached to GitHub release + image attestation.
  • cargo-vet (or audited vendor/) for first-party review of transitive deps on the syscall hot path (orc-runtime, orc-net).

Container images

One minimal image per control-plane/node binary:

FROM scratch                       # distroless/static for orc-apiserver,-scheduler,-controller
COPY orc-apiserver /orc-apiserver  # statically linked (musl or +crt-static), no shell, nonroot UID
ENTRYPOINT ["/orc-apiserver"]
Image Base Notes
orc-apiserver, orc-scheduler, orc-controller scratch/distroless-static static musl; read-only rootfs; nonroot
orc-kubelet distroless-base (Linux caps) needs host mounts, cgroups, /proc; privileged DaemonSet-style; not scratch
  • Multi-arch: docker buildx --platform linux/amd64,linux/arm64, single manifest list per tag.
  • Signing: cosign sign (keyless OIDC) every image + manifest; cosign attest the CycloneDX SBOM + SLSA provenance. Binaries signed + .sig/.sha256 published with the GitHub release.
  • Tags: vX.Y.Z + git-<sha>; latest only on stable releases.

Versioning + publication

crates.io publish order (strict, dep-DAG topological):

orc-api → orc-store → {orc-apiserver, orc-controller, orc-scheduler}
        → {orc-runtime, orc-net} → orc-kubelet → orctl
  • Single workspace version (lockstep semver X.Y.Z); cargo release bumps + tags atomically. Pre-1.0: minor = breaking.
  • Version stamped into binaries via build script: --version -> vX.Y.Z (git <sha>, <target>, rustc 1.78).
  • API version skew policy (mirrors Kubernetes ±1): control-plane components within one minor of orc-apiserver; orc-kubelet may be up to 2 minors behind, never ahead; orctl within ±1. apiVersion of resource kinds (orc-api) is independent of crate semver — never break a served apiVersion within a major.
  • Skew is asserted at startup: component logs its build version + the apiserver's /version; > policy -> warn (kubelet) / refuse-start (apiserver-coupled).

Workflow topology

.github/workflows/
  ci.yml      push/PR  → test-stable·test-msrv·build-aarch64·stub-macos·lint·deny·doc·property
  fuzz.yml    nightly  → cargo-fuzz (api_codec·watch_protocol·label_selector·oci_spec)
  bench.yml   nightly  → criterion (store·scheduler·apiserver·reconcile) + regression gate
  e2e.yml     PR+night → docker-compose cluster + orctl workload scenarios
  release.yml tag v*   → reproduce×2 → buildx multi-arch → cosign sign+attest → SBOM → crates.io (ordered) → GH release

Release gate ("Done"-adjacent, Phase 14): all of ci + e2e green, zero fuzz crashers, no bench regression, reproducible double-build hash match, cosign verify passes.

Performance Engineering

All targets reference the latency/throughput budgets in §4 (Constraints). Hardware baseline: 8-core x86-64, NVMe (fsync ~50µs), Linux 6.x, cgroups v2. Numbers are p50/p99 unless noted.

Quantified Performance Targets

Every row is derived from §4.1 Quantified Targets and/or the macro-bench numbers established above (Benchmark Strategy) — no new numbers invented here, only restated as CI-gated targets with a named phase gate.

ID Metric Target Phase Gate
M1 apiserver cached read throughput ≥ 5,000 req/s baseline (§4.1); ≥ 40,000 req/s at cached GET/LIST macro-bench Phase 3 (§14) apiserver macro-bench CI gate
M2 apiserver write throughput (CAS) ≥ 500 req/s baseline (§4.1); ≥ 3,000/s with group-commit batching Phase 1 (§12) store group-commit gate; Phase 3 (§14) apiserver write-path gate
M3 WATCH propagation latency, commit→watcher p99 < 100 ms (§4.1) Phase 4 (§15) informer/reflector CI gate
M4 Store write latency (WAL fsync) p99 < 10 ms single-node (§4.1); store_put op p99 ≤ 180 µs / group-commit p99 ≤ 12 µs (Benchmark Strategy) Phase 1 (§12) store micro-bench gate
M5 Scheduling bind rate ≥ 100 pods/s baseline (§4.1); ≥ 600/450/250 binds/s at 10/100/1,000 nodes (Benchmark Strategy macro-bench) Phase 6 (§17) scheduler macro-bench gate
M6 Per-pod schedule decision latency p99 < 50 ms @ 100 nodes; p99 < 200 ms @ 1,000 nodes (§4.1) Phase 6 (§17) schedule_one micro-bench gate
M7 Reconcile latency (watch event → acted) < 1 s per event (§4.1); full-cluster convergence p99 ≤ 2 s @ 1,000 pods / ≤ 25 s @ 10,000 pods (Benchmark Strategy) Phase 4–5 (§15–16) controller convergence gate
M8 Kubelet pod cold-start (image cached) < 5 s ceiling (§4.1); p50 ≤ 80 ms / p99 ≤ 250 ms sandbox+cgroup+container-start (Syscall Overhead) Phase 7–8 (§18–19) kubelet/runtime e2e gate
M9 Kubelet pods/node capacity hard cap ≈ 110 pods/node, PodWorker task overhead ≈ 4 KB stack/pod (Phase 7 §18; Scalability Analysis) Phase 7 (§18) kubelet load-test gate
M10 Node Lease liveness renew every 10 s, NotReady after 40 s missed (§4.1) Phase 5 (§16) Node-lifecycle chaos gate; Phase 7 (§18) Lease keepalive gate
M11 apiserver resident memory < 2 GB @ 30,000 objects (§4.1) Phase 3 (§14) memory-soak CI gate
M12 Control-plane RTO (apiserver/controller crash) resync from store < 30 s (§4.1) Phase 3–4 (§14–15) crash-recovery chaos gate

Benchmark Strategy

Micro-benches (criterion, cargo bench, per-crate benches/):

Bench Crate Input Target p50 Target p99 Notes
codec_encode Pod orc-api ~2 KB Pod ≤3 µs ≤8 µs serde_json; alloc-bound
codec_decode Pod orc-api ~2 KB JSON ≤6 µs ≤15 µs decode > encode (parse + validate)
selector_match orc-api 3 matchLabels + 1 matchExpr vs 1k objs ≤80 ns/obj ≤200 ns BTreeMap lookups; no alloc
store_put orc-store 2 KB val ≤70 µs ≤180 µs dominated by WAL fsync
store_put (group-commit) orc-store 2 KB ×64 batch ≤4 µs/op ≤12 µs amortized fsync
store_range orc-store 1k-key prefix ≤120 µs ≤300 µs btree iterate + clone
store_watch dispatch orc-store 1 event → 1k watchers ≤900 µs ≤2 ms fan-out CPU, no fsync
reconcile_replicaset orc-controller RS, 3/5 replicas, warm cache ≤15 µs ≤40 µs cache read only, no I/O
schedule_one orc-scheduler 1 pod, 100 feasible nodes ≤180 µs ≤500 µs filter+score, excludes Bind RTT

Macro / scale-benches (orc-bench harness, in-proc cluster, fixed seed):

Scenario Knobs Target §4 tie
apiserver write req/s single client, CAS writes ≥3k/s write-path budget
apiserver read req/s cached GET/LIST ≥40k/s read budget
watch fan-out 1k / 2k watchers, 100 ev/s dispatch p99 ≤2 ms @1k, ≤5 ms @2k watch-lag SLO
scheduling throughput 10 / 100 / 1000 nodes, 5k pending pods ≥600 / 450 / 250 binds/s scheduler budget
controller convergence 1k / 10k ReplicaSet-owned pods full reconcile ≤2 s / ≤25 s level-trigger SLO
resync storm 10k objects, periodic relist added p99 watch lag ≤+30% resync cost ceiling

Each scale-bench emits: throughput, p50/p99/p999 latency, RSS, allocs/op (via dhat). Regression gate in CI: ±10% on micro, ±20% on macro fails the build (see Operational Readiness).

Profiling Methodology

Tool Targets What it surfaces
tokio-console apiserver, controller, kubelet stalled tasks, busy-poll, blocked WorkQueue, watch-stream backpressure
perf + flamegraph apiserver write path, watch dispatch, scheduler CPU hot frames: serde, btree, fan-out clone
dhat / heaptrack informer cache, codec alloc count + bytes, retained heap, peak RSS
cargo flamegraph on orc-runtime container start syscall time: clone(), mount, cgroup write
strace -c -f orc-kubelet/orc-runtime syscall counts per container start (target <300)

Methodology: profile under steady-state load (warmed cache, post-relist), not cold start. Pin RUSTFLAGS=-C force-frame-pointers=yes for flamegraphs. Capture tokio-console during a watch storm to catch executor starvation (single reconcile blocking the runtime → bound reconcile to CPU-only, spawn_blocking forbidden in reconcile path).

Where Time Goes (Hot Paths)

  • JSON codec + object clones — informer cache stores each object; naive design clones on every cache read for every handler. Dominant CPU at 10k objects. → Arc<T> shared cache objects; handlers get Arc clones (refcount bump, no deep copy). Codec runs once on watch ingest, never per-handler.
  • Store WAL fsync — serial fsync per put caps write throughput at ~1/fsync-latency (~14k/s theoretical, ~3k/s real). → group-commit: batch concurrent writes into one fsync (≤2 ms or 64-op window). Single biggest write-path lever.
  • Watch fan-out CPU — one Revision → N watcher sends; O(N) per event. At 2k watchers × 100 ev/s = 200k sends/s. → sharded dispatch (below) + pre-serialize the WatchEvent once, share the encoded bytes across watchers (Arc<[u8]>).
  • Scheduler scoring — O(nodes × plugins) per pod. At 1000 nodes, score is the cost. → node-feasibility early-exit in Filter; percentage-of-nodes-to-score cap (score only ~50% of feasible nodes above 500 nodes, K8s-style).

Allocation Behavior

  • Informer cache: HashMap<Key, Arc<T>> + secondary indices HashMap<IndexKey, HashSet<Key>>. Cache memory ≈ n_objects × (obj_size + index_overhead); budget ~2 KB/Pod → 10k pods ≈ 20–30 MB resident.
  • Codec: decode allocates the object once; re-use Vec<u8> scratch buffers per connection to cut transient allocs. Target ≤6 allocs per Pod decode.
  • Watch buffers: bounded per-watcher channel (default 1024 events). Slow watcher → buffer fills → server closes the watch with a relist signal rather than growing unbounded (back-pressure, not OOM). This bounds apiserver RSS independent of watcher count.
  • Arc discipline: cache holds the canonical Arc<T>; a write replaces the Arc atomically (copy-on-write), readers keep their snapshot — no reader-side locking on the object body.

Resync (Relist) vs Incremental Watch

Aspect Incremental watch Resync (full relist)
Cost O(changes) O(all objects of kind)
Store load one watch range scan at start full range() per resync interval
Purpose steady-state delta delivery correctness safety net (recover dropped/compacted)
Default cadence continuous 5–30 min jittered per informer

Resync re-enqueues every cached key (level-triggered → idempotent reconcile absorbs it). Cost: a 10k-object relist ≈ one store_range of all keys + 10k WorkQueue adds. Mitigations: jitter resync start across informers (avoid synchronized relist thundering herd); resync from cache (re-enqueue keys) not from store when watch is healthy — full store relist only on watch break / 410 Gone.

Lock & Contention Analysis

  • Central contention: store single-writer Revision serialization. All put/delete/txn serialize through one writer to assign monotonic Revision + append WAL. This is by design (gap-free watch + CAS correctness depend on it) and is the write-throughput ceiling. Lever is fsync batching (group-commit), not parallel writers — parallelizing would break Revision monotonicity.
  • Reads are lock-free-ish: MVCC range/get at a snapshot Revision take a read view, never block the writer.
  • Watch dispatch contention: a single global watcher list under one lock serializes fan-out. → sharded watch dispatch: partition watchers by key-prefix/hash into S shards (S = num_cpus), each shard a task with its own watcher set; an event routes to matching shards only. Cuts fan-out lock hold time and parallelizes sends.
  • WorkQueue: rate-limited + dedup'd; contention on the shared mutex is low (enqueue/dequeue are O(1)); per-key dedup prevents duplicate concurrent reconciles of one object (the per-key in-flight guarantee).
  • Informer cache: RwLock (or sharded RwLock) — many reconcile readers, infrequent writer (watch-apply). Arc-CoW objects keep write-lock hold time to a pointer swap.

Syscall Overhead — Kubelet / Runtime Path

Operation Syscalls Budget Note
Sandbox create unshare/clone (net/ipc/uts/pid/mnt ns), netns setup ≤20 ms namespace creation dominates
cgroup v2 setup mkdir + ~6 write to cgroup.*/*.max ≤2 ms one cgroup subtree per pod
Image unpack layer extract (open/write/symlink ×N files) I/O bound overlay mount amortizes across pods
Container start pivot_root, mounts (≤8), exec ≤30 ms pivot_root + mount table is the cost
Probe exec per-probe fork/exec or HTTP connect ≤5 ms cache HTTP client; reuse conn

Pod cold-start budget (image cached): sandbox + cgroup + container start ≤80 ms p50, ≤250 ms p99. Image pull excluded (network/registry bound). Reduce syscall count: batch cgroup writes, reuse the pod sandbox netns for sidecars, avoid per-syscall error logging on the hot path.

Tail Latency

  • Watch lag under storm: burst of 10k writes → watch delivery p99 must stay ≤500 ms (§4). Levers: sharded dispatch, pre-serialized Arc<[u8]> events, bounded buffers (shed slow watchers, not block fast ones). Worst case is one slow watcher head-of-line-blocking a shard → per-watcher independent buffers prevent it.
  • Scheduling under node-heavy clusters: 1000 nodes → score cost dominates p99 bind latency. percentage-of-nodes-to-score + parallel Filter (rayon/Tokio over node chunks) keeps schedule_one p99 ≤500 µs; end-to-end bind p99 ≤5 ms (includes apiserver CAS write RTT).
  • Store fsync p99: NVMe fsync p99 spikes (~5–10× p50 under GC/flush). group-commit smooths it (one fsync serves many writes); write p99 target ≤180 µs holds because a stalled fsync amortizes across the whole batch. Monitor fsync p99 as the leading indicator of write-path SLO breach.
  • Reconcile tail: a single slow Reconciler must not block the runtime — reconcile is CPU+cache only; any blocking I/O (apiserver call) is async-awaited, never spawn_blocking on the reconcile worker pool. Per-controller bounded worker concurrency (default = num_cpus) bounds tail under requeue storms.

Scalability Analysis

Scaling axes: object count N, node count M, pod churn λ (writes/s), watcher count W. Every cost below is expressed against these. Single-node orc-store serializes all writes by Revision → it is the global throughput floor.

Cost model by axis

Axis Resource Scaling Driver Mitigation
Object count N apiserver heap O(N) watch-cache holds all objects in RAM per-resource cache, compaction bounds history not live set
Object count N each informer cache O(N_kind) SharedIndexInformer mirrors full kind one shared informer per kind per process; field-selector-scoped reflectors (kubelet: own-node Pods only)
Object count N LIST bandwidth O(N_kind · size) full relist on watch 410 Gone chunked LIST (limit+continue), watch-cache serves LIST from RAM, bookmarks shrink relist window
Node count M scheduler / pod O(M) Filter+Score every Node percentageOfNodesToScore (sample, default ≈ max(5, 50−M/125)%), scheduling cache (Phase 6)
Node count M store write rate O(M) lease + O(M) status each kubelet renews Node Lease + writes Node status Lease object decoupled from Node (heartbeat ≠ full status write); lease TTL 40s, renew 10s → M/10 writes/s
Pod churn λ watch dispatch O(W · λ) every change fanned to every matching watcher watch-cache fan-out, selector pre-filter, bookmark coalescing
Pod churn λ reconcile load O(λ) enqueues informer handler enqueues key per delta WorkQueue dedup collapses bursts to one key; RateLimiter backs off hot keys
Watcher count W apiserver CPU/heap O(W · λ) dispatch + O(W) buffers classic etcd/apiserver fan-out wall single store watch → in-proc cache → O(W) cheap fan-out; per-watcher bounded channel

The watch fan-out wall (dominant ceiling)

  • Naive: each WatchEvent re-fetched from orc-store per watcher → O(W · λ) store reads. Forbidden.
  • Design: orc-apiserver opens one Store::watch(start_rev) per resource type, populates an in-process watch-cache (ring buffer, last K≈10⁴ revisions), then dispatches to W client watchers from RAM.
  • Dispatch cost O(W · λ) CPU only (no I/O); selector match pre-filter drops non-matching watchers before serialization. JSON encode done once per event, shared Arc<[u8]> to all matching watchers.
  • Bookmarks: periodic WatchEvent::Bookmark(Revision) lets idle watchers advance resourceVersion → shrinks relist set after disconnect, prevents 410 Gone storms on reconnect.
  • Backpressure: slow watcher with full bounded channel → dropped + sent 410 Gone → client relists (never blocks the shared dispatch loop). Head-of-line isolation per watcher.
  • Targets: W = 10⁴ concurrent watchers, λ = 10³ writes/s sustained, p99 watch-event delivery < 50 ms; apiserver heap < 2 GB at N = 10⁵ objects.

Store: the write + revision-serialization bottleneck

  • All put/delete/txn take a global lock to assign monotonic Revision → single writer, ceiling ≈ a few × 10³ writes/s (WAL fsync-bound; group-commit batches fsyncs → amortize).
  • Reads scale independently: served from watch-cache (apiserver) at any resourceVersion ≤ committed; store hit only for quorum/linearizable reads. Read:write ratio in practice ≫ 100:1.
  • Raft hook (cross-link 42) is HA, not scale: replication multiplies write cost (quorum round-trip) — it buys availability, not throughput. State this explicitly.
  • Compaction (compact(rev)) bounds history → store size O(live objects), not O(total writes); without it mod_rev history grows unbounded → disk + relist cost blowup.
  • Sharding by resource type (deferred): separate keyspaces / store instances per kind to lift the single-Revision ceiling — breaks cross-kind transactional consistency, so deferred past Phase 14.

Controller scaling

  • More WorkQueue workers (parallelism P) → throughput O(P) until store-write-bound; dedup means P bounded by distinct hot keys, not event rate.
  • Leader election (Phase 4/13 hook): only the elected orc-controller reconciles → no double-acting, no scale (active/passive). Horizontal controller scale = shard by key hash across leaders (deferred) — each owns a key range, watch field-selector-scoped to its shard.
  • RateLimiter::when (exponential backoff per key) caps retry storms; forget on success. Resync interval (default 10 min) re-drives full cache → O(N_kind) periodic load spike; jitter to avoid thundering herd.

Scheduling ceiling

  • Per-pod O(M) Filter + O(M) Score → throughput ∝ 1/M. At M=5000 nodes, full scoring ≈ unviable.
  • percentageOfNodesToScore: stop after a feasible sample, round-robin start index for fairness → per-pod cost ≈ O(min(M, sample)). Trades optimality for rate.
  • Scheduling cache: node resource snapshots updated incrementally from watch (not re-listed per pod) → Filter reads RAM. Target: ≥ 100 pods/s bind rate at M=1000.
  • Single scheduler is active/passive (leader election) — not horizontally scaled; multi-scheduler by schedulerName partition (deferred).

Kubelet per-node scaling

  • Hard cap ≈ 110 pods/node (cgroup/PLEG/probe overhead). One PodWorker task/pod; ProbeManager runs O(pods × probes) timers/node.
  • Each kubelet watches only Pod.spec.node_name == self (field-selector) → per-node watch traffic O(own pods), independent of cluster N.
  • Status writes batched + rate-limited (kubelet sole status writer) → ≤ a few writes/s/node; contributes O(M) to store write load.

Overall ceilings + horizontal-scaling story

Ceiling Limit Horizontal scale path
apiserver watch fan-out O(W·λ) CPU add apiserver replicas (stateless, each owns its watch-cache) — read/watch scales linearly
store write throughput single-Revision writer, ~10³–10⁴/s no horizontal scale w/o sharding (deferred); Raft = HA only
scheduler at high M O(M)/pod sampling + cache; multi-scheduler partition (deferred)
controller reconcile O(λ) more workers; sharded workqueues by key (deferred)
node fan-out O(M) lease+status Lease decoupling; bounded by store write ceiling
  • HA control plane: N stateless orc-apiserver replicas behind LB (read/watch scale-out); orc-scheduler + orc-controller active/passive via leader election (correctness, not scale).
  • Read replicas: watch-cache per apiserver = effective read replica; LIST/WATCH never hit store on cache hit.
  • Store sharding (deferred): the only path past the write ceiling; sacrifices cross-shard Txn atomicity → explicit non-goal for Phase 1..14.
  • Practical target envelope: M ≤ 1000 nodes, N ≤ 1.5×10⁵ objects, λ ≤ 10³ writes/s, W ≤ 10⁴ watchers — beyond requires store sharding (out of scope).

Mental Models & First Principles

These are the load-bearing mental models for this project. Each is named (MM1…MM10) and cross-referenced to the phase where it becomes operational.

MM1 — The Store as Shared Reality (Phase 1). Every component believes only what it reads from orc-store. There is no other ground truth. A component that maintains private state not derived from the store will diverge from all others on restart. Consequence: every design question about where to hold state has one answer — can it be reconstructed from a list+watch? If yes, that is where it lives.

MM2 — Revision as the Global Logical Clock (Phase 1). Revision is not a timestamp. It is a monotonic, gap-free, globally serialized counter that assigns a unique total order to every write in the store. resourceVersion is a projection of this clock onto a single object. This gives you: (a) a safe watch start point, (b) a compaction boundary, (c) an optimistic-concurrency token, and (d) a list snapshot at a point in time — all from one integer.

MM3 — Level-Triggering as Convergence Insurance (Phase 4). An edge-triggered system counts transitions. A level-triggered system reads the current level. After any network partition, crash, or event loss, a level-triggered reconciler re-reads full state and recomputes the right action — a missed transition has no lasting effect. The informer cache is the level; the WorkQueue key is the trigger; the reconcile call reads the level, never the edge.

MM4 — The Single-Writer-Per-Field Invariant (Phases 2, 7). If two components can both write a field, correctness requires a distributed lock or a merge function. Neither is acceptable here. The design eliminates contention at the model level: pod.spec.node_name has one writer (scheduler); pod.status has one writer (kubelet). CAS conflicts become unambiguous bugs, not normal coordination.

MM5 — Watch Gap-Freedom as Cache Correctness Prerequisite (Phase 1). An informer cache is a function of the initial list plus all subsequent watch events. One dropped event corrupts the function permanently — there is no self-healing path short of a relist. Gap-freedom is not a performance feature; it is the integrity guarantee that makes caches trustworthy. Every design choice in orc-store's watch implementation (serialized Revision, Bookmark, 410-on-compaction) exists to maintain this guarantee.

MM6 — The CAS-Not-Lock Principle (Phase 3). Distributed locks held across network round-trips introduce deadlock risk when the holder crashes. Optimistic CAS avoids this: the lock is never held; the cost of failure is one retry from fresh state; level-triggered reconcile makes retry cheap. The only time a CAS conflict is expensive is when many writers contend on the same field simultaneously — the single-writer-per-field invariant (MM4) eliminates exactly this case in normal operation.

MM7 — The Trait as the Phase Seam (Phase 1). trait Store, trait ContainerRuntime, trait FilterPlugin, trait NetworkPlugin, trait ReplicationHook are not abstractions for testability alone. They are phase-boundary gates. A todo() implementation passes the compiler; it does not pass the gate test. The trait defines the contract; the phase delivers the real implementation behind it. Never let a todo() survive a phase-completion commit.

MM8 — The Scheduling Ceiling is Algorithmic, Not Hardware (Phase 6). At M nodes and one Pod to schedule, Filter+Score costs O(M). This is not a constant you can buy away with faster hardware; it is an algorithmic bound. The percentageOfNodesToScore optimization converts the problem from "find the best node" to "find a sufficiently good node from a random sample" — a principled approximation that trades optimality for bounded latency.

MM9 — Out-of-Process PIDs Decouple Lifecycle from Kubelet (Phase 8). If containers run as threads within the kubelet process, a kubelet crash kills all pods on the node. If containers run as independent PIDs in their own namespace+cgroup set, a kubelet crash is transparent to running workloads. The kubelet re-adopts survivors on restart by scanning its PID registry. This asymmetry — kubelet is optional for running containers, mandatory for scheduling new ones — is the fundamental design of every production container runtime.

MM10 — Compaction Bounds History, Not Liveness (Phase 1, Phase 13). orc-store retains every write as a revision in its history index. Without compaction, this grows unbounded. compact(rev) drops history below rev — it does not delete live objects, only old versions. A watch started from a revision below the compaction point receives 410 Gone and must relist. The compaction policy (retain last 5 min, or 10k revisions) is a tradeoff between watch-reconnect safety and storage cost. Getting this wrong causes either unbounded disk growth or thundering-herd relists.

Comparative Architecture Table

How this design compares to four real production container orchestration systems across the axes that matter for the learning goals.

Dimension 268 (this project) Kubernetes 1.30 HashiCorp Nomad 1.7 Docker Swarm (Moby) Apache Mesos + Marathon
Control-plane store Hand-built MVCC KV (orc-store): WAL, Revision clock, gap-free watch, Lease, Txn CAS etcd v3: same MVCC semantics; Raft-replicated; bbolt backing BoltDB-backed FSM over Raft; no gap-free watch — Nomad polls or uses blocking queries Raft-replicated in-memory store (SwarmKit); no etcd dependency ZooKeeper for leader election + Raft-like master logs; Mesos master in-memory state
Watch / notification model Gap-free ordered watch from a Revision; Bookmark; 410-on-compaction → relist Identical — etcd watch API; apiserver watch-cache adds fan-out buffering Long-poll blocking queries on index; "last index" monotonic but not globally ordered across types Event bus (SwarmKit gRPC stream); at-least-once, not gap-guaranteed Mesos uses ZK watches + event callbacks; Marathon REST polling; no global ordering
Scheduling model Pluggable Filter+Score+Bind (KEP-624 analog); scheduler reads cache; CAS bind Identical framework; 30+ in-tree plugins; gang scheduling via Permit/Reserve Single-pass bin-pack + spread/stack strategies; no plugin framework; all-in-one binary Round-robin or spread across managers; no scoring; external constraint solving deferred Two-level: Mesos offers resources to frameworks (Marathon); Marathon does second-level scheduling
Reconciliation Level-triggered, idempotent Reconciler; DeltaFIFO→SharedIndexInformer→WorkQueue Identical pattern in controller-manager + kubelet; client-go informer library Level-triggered eval plans but reconciler less formalized; no shared-informer concept Task-based reactive handlers; partially edge-triggered in early Swarm versions Marathon reconciliation: manual /v2/tasks/delete + health-based restart; less principled
Container isolation Direct Linux syscalls: clone, cgroup v2, mount, pivot_root; OCI config.json CRI grpc → containerd → runc → same syscalls; kubelet does not exec containers directly Direct runc/containerd exec via alloc runner; no custom runtime protocol libnetwork + libcontainerd; close to direct Docker engine; pre-CRI Mesos containerizer: cgroups + Linux namespaces via libprocess; no OCI standard initially
Networking CNI-like trait + userspace ServiceProxy; VIP→EndpointSlice; DNS from store CNI plugins (Calico/Cilium/Flannel); kube-proxy (iptables/IPVS/eBPF); CoreDNS CNI plugins via Consul service mesh integration; built-in service discovery Overlay driver (VXLAN, macvlan) built into SwarmKit; VIP via IPVS; internal DNS Mesos-DNS for service discovery; Marathon-LB (HAProxy); no built-in overlay
HA strategy Single-node store + Raft hook (Phase 13, x-link 42); leader election via Lease CAS etcd multi-node Raft quorum; N stateless apiserver replicas; controller/scheduler active/passive Multi-server Raft in every Nomad server; no split control plane Manager quorum (Raft via SwarmKit); no separate store process ZooKeeper quorum for election; Mesos master hot-standby
Security model TLS client-cert authn + JWT SA tokens + RBAC admission (Phase 12); tmpfs secret projection Identical; additionally: PSP/PodSecurity admission, webhook admission, audit logging mTLS via Consul; ACL token system; no SA concept TLS between nodes; no RBAC; Docker secrets via Raft store Mesos ACLs; Marathon basic auth; no unified RBAC
Horizontal scale ceiling Store write: single-Revision writer ~10³/s; apiserver: stateless, scales with replicas Store write: etcd single-Raft-leader; ~10k writes/s with group-commit; apiserver: N replicas Raft applies at each server; scheduling throughput ~500 jobs/s; scales by reducing eval latency Manager quorum limits write throughput; designed for smaller clusters (<100 nodes) Mesos offers resources to frameworks — scheduling parallelism built-in; scales to 50k nodes (Borg-scale analog)

Key takeaway for learners: This project implements the same MVCC+watch+level-triggered reconcile architecture as Kubernetes, but from scratch. The comparison shows that every design choice (gap-free watch, CAS bind, pluggable scheduler, CNI-like trait) is a deliberate improvement over predecessors — Swarm lacks it, Mesos approximates it, Nomad partially adopts it, Kubernetes fully commits to it.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All numbers derived from the System Constraints in §4 and the Scalability Analysis. Formulas shown; arrive at the number, do not memorize it.

Target cluster: M = 500 nodes, N = 50,000 objects (mixed kinds), λ = 500 writes/s sustained, W = 2,000 concurrent watchers.

# What Formula Result Constraint it derives from
1 Store write ceiling WAL fsync @ 2 ms per group-commit batch; batch = up to 50 ops → 50 / 0.002 = 25,000 writes/s theoretical; practical with lock overhead: ~5,000/s ≈ 5,000 writes/s §4.1: store ≥ 5k writes/s single-node
2 Node heartbeat write rate M nodes × (1 Lease renew / 10s) = 500 / 10 = 50 writes/s 50 writes/s (10% of ceiling) §4.1: Lease TTL 40s, renew 10s
3 Watch fan-out CPU W watchers × λ events/s × cost-per-dispatch; Arc<[u8]> shared → cost ≈ 1 µs/watcher (cache miss + channel send) → 2000 × 500 × 1e-6 = 1 CPU core ~1 core for dispatch §4.1: p99 watch-event delivery < 50 ms
4 Watch-cache ring buffer size Retain K revisions; event size avg 2 KB; retain last 10,000 revs: 10,000 × 2 KB = 20 MB per resource type 20 MB / type §4.1: history ≥ 10k revisions for compaction safety
5 Informer cache heap (apiserver) N objects × avg serialized size 4 KB = 50,000 × 4 KB = 200 MB 200 MB §4.1: apiserver heap < 2 GB at N = 10⁵
6 Scheduler throughput Per-pod scheduling: Filter+Score O(sample) nodes; sample = max(5%, 500) = 25 nodes; plugin cost 10 µs/node; 25 × 10 µs = 250 µs/pod → 1 / 0.00025 = 4,000 pods/s max (single-threaded bind) ≥ 100 pods/s achievable §4.1: scheduling ≥ 100 pods/s at M=1000
7 Store disk growth (no compaction) λ × avg entry size 2 KB × seconds/day = 500 × 2,000 × 86,400 = 86 GB/day 86 GB/day (unbounded without compact) §4.1: must compact; retain last 5 min only = 500 × 300 × 2 KB = 300 MB retained
8 Kubelet pod-worker goroutines per node 110 pods/node × 1 PodWorker task + O(probes) timer tasks; Tokio task overhead ≈ 4 KB stack → 110 × 4 KB = 440 KB < 1 MB for tasks §4.2: hard cap 110 pods/node
9 Leader election lease storm on restart N_controllers components each re-contest on restart; acquire = 1 Txn CAS → N_controllers writes; N_controllers ≤ 3 (controller, scheduler, optional) → 3 extra writes, negligible < 10 writes burst §4.3: CAS is optimistic, no lock-storm
