12 End-to-end Pod schedule-to-running latency API admit: 5 ms; watch delta to scheduler: < 50 ms; schedule+bind: 250 µs; watch delta to kubelet: < 50 ms; image pull (cached): < 1 s; container start: 50 ms < 1.5 s cold (cached image) §4.1: pod schedule-to-running < 30s; well within

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

These are the questions a staff-level reviewer would ask. No hand-waving answers pass. Each has a testable, implementation-grounded answer derivable from this specification.

  1. Why must Revision be assigned inside the single write lock rather than generated by an atomic counter before the lock? What failure mode does a pre-lock counter introduce, and why does it break watch correctness even if no actual value is lost?

  2. A DeltaFIFO Replace(items, rv) call synthesizes delete events for keys present in the old cache but absent from the relist. What invariant does this maintain, and what would happen to the GarbageCollector if the delete were not synthesized?

  3. The scheduler reads from the informer cache (not from orc-store directly) and then writes spec.node_name via a CAS Txn. Between the cache read and the CAS write, the node could have become unschedulable. Trace the exact failure path and explain why eventual correctness is guaranteed without a distributed lock.

  4. A kubelet crashes while a container is in ContainersRunning state. When it restarts, it must re-adopt the running container. What two data sources does the kubelet consult to reconstruct the PodWorker state, and what happens if the container has also died while the kubelet was down?

  5. Explain gap-freedom in terms of a concrete store implementation choice: why does watcher::replay_from(start_rev) require a history index (treeIndex), not just the current value of each key?

  6. The Txn operation takes a global write lock for the duration of the compare+apply+assign-Revision sequence. What is the maximum-latency implication of this, and what optimization (used in etcd) reduces the average cost without violating the monotonicity guarantee?

  7. Why does 410 Gone on a compacted watch start point require a full relist, not just a re-watch from the compaction rev? What would the informer cache contain if you re-watched from compact_rev without relisting?

  8. Two ReplicaSet controllers are running (before leader election is implemented). Both observe 2 Pods running against a desired count of 3. Both decide to create a third Pod with the same deterministic name. Trace the exact sequence of store operations and explain which one wins and what the other observes.

  9. A finalizer is added to a Pod by the GarbageCollector before a kubelet is ready to handle it. The kubelet calls DELETE on the Pod, which succeeds (sets deletion_timestamp). The finalizer is never removed because the GC controller crashes. What is the state of the Pod after 1 hour, and what is the recovery path?

  10. The percentageOfNodesToScore optimization samples nodes rather than evaluating all of them. Under what scheduling workload does this optimization cause measurable allocation inefficiency, and what is the signal in metrics that indicates you have hit this regime?

  11. The Bookmark event carries a Revision but no object payload. What specific state transition in the client does receiving a Bookmark enable, and why is it necessary for compaction safety even when no writes have occurred recently?

  12. A WorkQueue is keyed by object key (namespace/name), not by event. Two rapid back-to-back updates to the same Pod key produce two events but only one queue entry. What property of the reconciler makes this safe, and what class of reconciler bug would make it unsafe?

  13. The out-of-process container execution model (ADR-21) means containers survive a kubelet crash. What data structure must the kubelet persist (or reconstruct from the OS) on startup to re-adopt surviving containers, and what is the failure mode if that reconstruction is incorrect?

  14. Why does pivot_root require the new root to be on a different mount point from the old root? What exact filesystem operation must be performed before pivot_root to satisfy this precondition, and what kernel error do you receive if you skip it?

  15. The single-writer-per-field invariant says kubelet is the sole writer of pod.status. A controller also needs to set a condition on the Pod (e.g., a webhook readiness gate). How does Kubernetes (and by analogy this project) allow this without violating the invariant?

  16. A leader election Txn compares lease_holder == absent and writes the holder identity. If two instances send this Txn simultaneously (after a network partition heals), what store property guarantees exactly one of them succeeds? Trace the exact compare-and-set execution.

  17. The WAL uses fsync to guarantee crash-recovery. Under what conditions can you safely batch multiple put operations into one fsync call without violating the monotonicity guarantee, and what is the ordering constraint between writing the WAL record and updating the in-memory BTree index?

  18. cgroup.subtree_control requires that a cgroup with processes (a leaf) cannot have sub-controllers enabled — the "no-internal-process rule." How does CgroupHandle in Phase 8 enforce this rule when creating a new container cgroup under an existing kubelet cgroup?

  19. The Deployment controller reconciles the desired replicas count by managing a set of ReplicaSet objects. During a rolling update, it must create a new RS and scale down the old RS. Under the level-triggered contract, how does it know which RS is "new" and which is "old" without storing any state outside orc-store?

  20. A ConfigMap is updated while a Pod is running with it mounted as a volume. The VolumeManager's reconcile_projected atomically swaps the ..data symlink. What guarantees does the renameat2(RENAME_EXCHANGE) call provide that a plain rename does not, and why does this matter for a process reading the files while the swap occurs?

  21. The informer cache uses a SharedIndexInformer with named indices (e.g., "pod_by_node"). A kubelet watches only Pods for its own node via a field selector. Why is it still correct for the kubelet to maintain a local SharedIndexInformer rather than querying the apiserver for each reconcile, even under network partition?

  22. An OCI image manifest references layers by content-addressable sha256 digest. When ImageStore::pull_image encounters a layer that already exists in the local cache, it skips the download. What invariant about content-addressable storage makes this deduplication unconditionally safe without a version check?

  23. Why is the Raft ReplicationHook seam (ADR-06) placed at the Store trait level rather than at the orc-apiserver REST handler level? What would break if you intercepted writes at the HTTP layer instead?

  24. The chaos harness in Phase 14 kills the orc-store process for 30 seconds and then restarts it. List the exact sequence of events each of the following components will experience and what their recovery path is: orc-apiserver, orc-controller, orc-scheduler, orc-kubelet.

  25. A proptest-generated random operation sequence drives the watch-gap-freedom property test. What is the precise predicate the property test must assert, and what invariant in the store implementation guarantees the predicate holds even when operations arrive in arbitrary order?

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Each design below would achieve the same functional goal (container orchestration with declarative scheduling and reconciliation) using a fundamentally different approach. Each is sketched with its core tradeoff.

Alt-A — Event-Sourced Command Bus (CQRS + Kafka)

Instead of a MVCC KV store, the cluster state is a Kafka log. Every mutation is a command event. Controllers consume the log and maintain materialized views. Watch = Kafka consumer group from an offset.

Client → REST API → CommandProducer → [ Kafka Partition ]
                                               ↓
                             Controller-A  ←  Consumer Group A
                             Controller-B  ←  Consumer Group B
                             Scheduler     ←  Consumer Group C
                             Kubelet       ←  Consumer Group D (filtered by node)
                                               ↓
                             Materialized views (per-consumer RocksDB)

Why it is not chosen: Kafka gives at-least-once delivery, not gap-free ordered watch by revision. Materialized views diverge when consumer groups fall behind. Cross-component transactions (CAS) require Kafka transactions, which are complex and don't compose with the declarative API. The resourceVersion concept maps awkwardly to Kafka offsets when multiple partitions exist. Correctness proofs are harder. When it wins: very high write throughput (millions of events/s), event replay for audit, fan-out to heterogeneous consumers — better suited for analytics than control-plane state.

Alt-B — Actor-Per-Pod Control Plane (Erlang/Akka model)

Each Pod, Node, ReplicaSet, and Service is an actor with its own mailbox and state. Reconciliation is message-passing between actors. There is no shared store — actors hold their own state and communicate by message.

PodActor(uid1) ←→ NodeActor(node3)    ReplicaSetActor(rs42)
     ↑                 ↑                      ↓
   mailbox           mailbox           [ Pod mailbox × N ]
                                              ↓
                                     Scheduler (actor) → Bind → NodeActor

Why it is not chosen: Global state queries (LIST all Pods, compute cluster-wide resource allocation) require scatter-gather across all actors — O(N) messages per query, no consistent snapshot. A controller restart loses all actor state and must rehydrate from scratch with no agreed recovery point. Watch semantics require total ordering across actors which re-introduces a coordinating sequence number — effectively rebuilding the store. When it wins: very high per-object event rates where shared-state contention is the bottleneck; Erlang's OTP supervision gives free restart/crash-isolation per actor; suitable for per-device state machines in IoT at billions of actors.

Alt-C — Distributed SQL as the Store (CockroachDB / Spanner)

Replace orc-store with a distributed SQL database. Objects are rows with a JSON/JSONB spec column and a resource_version column (auto-increment or sequence). Watch is implemented as CDC (change data capture) or polling.

orc-apiserver → SQL INSERT/UPDATE/SELECT with RV CAS (UPDATE WHERE rv=expected)
orc-store     = CockroachDB cluster (replicated, horizontally scalable)
Watch         = CDC stream (Debezium/built-in) → apiserver watch fan-out

Why it is not chosen: SQL CDC does not provide gap-free ordered delivery by a global revision — CDC streams are per-table, and cross-table ordering is not guaranteed. Implementing Bookmark and 410-on-compaction semantics on top of CDC is complex. The Txn compare-by-resource_version maps to UPDATE WHERE but lacks the on_else branch and multi-op composition. SQL query latency for range scans with a prefix (the primary access pattern) is higher than a BTree with a pre-computed prefix endpoint. When it wins: when the cluster state exceeds available RAM and must be disk-resident at scale, when SQL joins across kinds are needed (e.g., quota computation joins ResourceQuota + all namespaced objects), or when a managed cloud SQL service removes the operational burden of running etcd.

Alt-D — Gossip-Based Eventual Consistency (Consul-style)

Replace the single-store model with a gossip protocol (SWIM or similar). Every node maintains a local copy of all state. Writes are broadcast via gossip and converge eventually. No central store — every node is a peer.

node1: { Pod list, Node list, Service list } ←→ gossip ←→ node2: { same state }
                                                   ↓
                                            Scheduler (reads local copy)
                                            Controller (reconciles local copy)

Why it is not chosen: Gossip is eventually consistent — two nodes may see different states during a partition. A scheduler on node1 and a scheduler on node2 may both decide to bind the same Pod to different Nodes (no CAS bind possible without a central sequencer). Watch semantics require total ordering which gossip cannot provide. Detecting split-brain requires quorum, which reintroduces a central authority. When it wins: service discovery and health checking at very large scale (Consul's primary use case); when the key property needed is "eventually know that a service is healthy" rather than "atomically schedule exactly once." Gossip excels where stale reads are acceptable and write consistency is not.

Deferred Features Register

Features listed here are explicitly out of scope for Phases 0–14. Each row resolves a PhaseDeferred(...) anchor from §1.3. Future phases and cross-links are cited so the ledger is complete and every anchor resolves.

Feature Deferred-to Phase / Cross-link Reason deferred Prerequisites from this project
CRDs and operator framework (CustomResourceDefinition, custom controllers) Phase 24 (§23) Requires dynamic schema registry, unstructured object decode, and validation webhook infrastructure — orthogonal complexity after Phase 12 (typed RBAC) is solid Phase 12 (admission + RBAC), Phase 3 (apiserver Registry extensibility), Phase 5 (controller framework)
Full RBAC (ClusterRole, ClusterRoleBinding, aggregation) Phase 24 (§23) Full RBAC aggregation and ClusterRole inheritance require dynamic rule merging; basic RBAC (Role/RoleBinding per namespace) lands in Phase 12 Phase 12 (ServiceAccount JWT, basic Role/RoleBinding)
StatefulSet, DaemonSet, Job, CronJob controllers Phase 17 (§16) Each requires distinct scheduling constraints (ordered pod identity, per-node guarantee, completion tracking) that build on the Phase 9 Deployment rollout infrastructure Phase 9 (Deployment + RS controller), Phase 6 (scheduler affinity/node-selector), Phase 7 (kubelet pod lifecycle)
HPA (Horizontal Pod Autoscaler) Cross-link project 269 Requires metrics pipeline (Prometheus adapter or custom metrics API), separate from control-plane; tracked in its own project Phase 5 (ReplicaSet controller scale endpoint), Phase 12 (metrics API admission)
CSI (Container Storage Interface) full plugin Phase 23 (§22) Full CSI requires gRPC plugin protocol, volume attachment lifecycle, and node-publish/unpublish coordination across kubelet and external CSI driver; Phase 11 delivers the stub seam Phase 11 (VolumeManager + CSI-like NodePublish/NodeUnpublish stub), Phase 8 (container mount integration)
Ingress / L7 load balancing Phase 22 (§21) Requires an Ingress controller (separate component), L7 routing rules, TLS termination, and backend health integration beyond Phase 10's L4 ClusterIP ServiceProxy Phase 10 (EndpointSlice + ServiceProxy + DNS), Phase 12 (TLS, ServiceAccount tokens for ingress auth)
Raft-backed HA store (multi-node quorum) Phase 25 (§24) → cross-link project 42 Full Raft replication is a separate project (42); Phase 13 delivers the ReplicationHook seam and single-node leader election so the hook can be wired without changing controllers or apiserver Phase 1 (Store trait + ReplicationHook seam), Phase 13 (leader election, Raft seam integration)

Glossary

Terms are listed by usage cluster (object model → store → controllers → scheduler → node/runtime → networking → config/security → resilience). Each term is precise + implementation-relevant to project 268; type/crate names match the canonical spine exactly.

Object model & API (orc-api)

Term Definition
object / resource / kind A persisted, addressable API entity (Pod, Service, …). Kind = the type name in TypeMeta.kind; resource = its REST collection (pods); object = a single instance. All impl Object trait.
apiVersion group/version string in TypeMeta.api_version (e.g. apps/v1, v1). Versions a kind's schema; drives codec dispatch + conversion.
TypeMeta { api_version, kind } — self-describing type header on every object; enables polymorphic decode without out-of-band type info.
ObjectMeta Common metadata: name, namespace, uid (Uuid), resource_version, generation, labels, annotations, owner_references, finalizers, timestamps. Identity + bookkeeping shared by all kinds.
spec vs status spec = user-declared desired state (immutable to controllers except owners); status = observed/reported state. Distinct sub-objects; single-writer per field.
desired vs observed state Reconciliation inputs: desired = current spec; observed = status + informer cache. Controller drives observed → desired.
declarative API expresses what (target state), never how (imperative ops). Clients apply full objects; system computes diffs.
generation ObjectMeta.generation — i64 bumped on every spec change; status.observed_generation lets a controller report "have I acted on this spec yet".
label Key/value in ObjectMeta.labels; identifying, queryable, used by selectors to group objects (loose coupling: Service→Pod, ReplicaSet→Pod).
selector LabelSelector { match_labels, match_expressions: Vec<Requirement> }; matches objects by labels (equality + set ops: In/NotIn/Exists).
field selector Server-side filter on built-in fields (metadata.namespace, spec.node_name, status.phase) in ListOptions; kubelet uses spec.node_name==self.
OwnerReference { uid, kind, controller: bool, block_owner_deletion: bool } in child meta; encodes the owning controller for GC + adoption.
finalizer String key in ObjectMeta.finalizers; presence blocks hard-delete (object enters deletion_timestamp-set "terminating" state) until the responsible controller removes its key.
Condition { type, status: True/False/Unknown, reason, message, last_transition } entry in status.conditions; standard observability surface (e.g. Ready, Available).
Pod Smallest schedulable unit: co-located containers + shared volumes, network namespace, lifecycle. PodSpec.node_name set once by scheduler.
PodPhase enum { Pending, Running, Succeeded, Failed, Unknown } in PodStatus.phase; coarse lifecycle summary; written only by kubelet.
ReplicaSet Maintains spec.replicas Pods matching spec.selector from spec.template; reconciler adopts/orphans by ownerRef + selector.
Deployment Manages ReplicaSets to roll out Pod template changes; `spec.strategy: RollingUpdate{max_surge,max_unavailable}
rolling update Update strategy: incrementally scale up a new ReplicaSet + scale down old, bounded by maxSurge/maxUnavailable, to keep availability during rollout.
maxSurge Max Pods over desired replicas allowed during a rolling update (absolute or %). Caps extra capacity / scheduling pressure.
maxUnavailable Max Pods below desired allowed unavailable during a rolling update; sets the availability floor.

Store & consistency (orc-store)

Term Definition
MVCC Multi-version concurrency control: every write creates a new version keyed by Revision; reads at a revision see a consistent snapshot; old versions retained until compact.
Revision Revision(i64) — global monotonic logical clock of the store; bumped once per committed write. The cluster's total order; basis of resourceVersion.
resourceVersion Opaque string on ObjectMeta = the store Revision at the object's last write. Drives optimistic concurrency + watch positioning; never arithmetic on it client-side.
KeyValue Stored record: { key, value, create_rev, mod_rev, version, lease }. version = per-key write count; mod_rev = revision of last modification.
optimistic concurrency / compare-and-set Update sends the resourceVersion it read; apiserver issues a store txn with a Compare on mod_rev; mismatch → 409 Conflict; caller re-reads + retries. No locks.
Txn txn(compares, on_ok, on_else) — atomic compare-and-set transaction; the apiserver's CAS primitive and the only multi-op atomic write path.
lease TTL-bound association (lease_grant(ttl) → LeaseId); keys attached to a lease are deleted when it expires unless lease_keepalive'd. Backs node heartbeats.
compaction compact(rev) discards object history at revisions < rev, reclaiming space; watches positioned below the compaction point fail with 410.
watch watch(key, end, start_rev) -> WatchStream; streams every change with Revision > start_rev, in strict revision order, gap-free + duplicate-free. Foundation of cache correctness.
watch event enum WatchEvent { Put(kv, prev), Delete(kv), Bookmark(rev) }; the unit streamed by a watch. Put carries previous value for diffing.
bookmark WatchEvent::Bookmark(Revision) — periodic no-op marker advancing the client's last-seen revision without an object payload; lets clients resume cheaply + bounds re-list on reconnect.
410 Gone / Expired Watch error when start_rev was compacted; client must re-list (to get a fresh resourceVersion) then re-watch. The only watch-restart trigger.

Reconciliation framework (orc-controller)

Term Definition
level-triggered reconciliation Controller acts on current full state, not individual events; reconcile reads desired+observed and converges. Idempotent; a dropped event is harmless (next resync/watch re-reads). Edge-triggered logic forbidden.
controller An independent async actor owning one reconcile loop for a kind; watches via informer, drives observed→desired through the apiserver.
Reconciler trait Reconciler { async fn reconcile(&self, req: Request, ctx) -> Result<Reconcile, ReconcileError> }; the unit of convergence logic. Request { namespace, name } is an object key, not an event.
Reconcile enum { Done, Requeue, RequeueAfter(Duration) } — reconcile outcome controlling re-enqueue timing.
reconcile loop The dequeue→read-cache→reconcile→requeue cycle; runs continuously, convergent, idempotent.
informer Local cache + event machinery for one type: list+watch the apiserver, maintain an indexed in-memory copy, fan out keys to handlers. Avoids hammering the apiserver.
Reflector List+watches a type from the apiserver into a DeltaFIFO; handles relist on 410 and resync; the informer's data pump.
DeltaFIFO A FIFO queue of accumulated per-object deltas (Added/Updated/Deleted/Sync) feeding the informer; coalesces deltas per key, preserving order.
SharedIndexInformer SharedIndexInformer<T> — Reflector + DeltaFIFO + thread-safe indexed cache, shared by many controllers of the same type (one watch, N consumers).
Lister Read-only indexed accessor over the informer cache (get, list, list_by_selector); reconcilers read from it, never directly from the apiserver per-event.
WorkQueue WorkQueue<K> — rate-limited, de-duplicated, delayed key queue between informer handlers and reconcilers; collapses bursts to one item per key.
rate limiter trait RateLimiter<K> { fn when(&K)->Duration; fn forget(&K) }; computes per-item backoff (e.g. exponential) for requeues; forget clears state on success.
resync Periodic full re-delivery of the cache as synthetic Sync deltas; safety net guaranteeing eventual convergence even if a watch event was missed.
OwnerReference (GC view) See above; the GarbageCollector traverses ownerRefs to cascade-delete children.
garbage collection The GarbageCollector controller deletes objects orphaned by owner deletion, honoring block_owner_deletion + finalizers.
cascading delete Deleting an owner triggers deletion of owned children (Deployment→ReplicaSet→Pod), driven by ownerRef graph traversal in the GC.
adoption / orphan A controller adopts a matching label-selected object lacking a controller ownerRef (sets its ownerRef); orphans it (clears ownerRef) when no longer matched.
leader election Control-plane components contend for a single lease-backed leadership lock; only the leader reconciles, preventing duplicate actuation across replicas.

Scheduling (orc-scheduler)

Term Definition
scheduler Watches Pods with empty spec.node_name; for each, runs Filter→Score→Bind to assign a Node. One Bind per Pod (exclusive).
SchedulingContext SchedulingContext<'a> — per-attempt state: target pod, node lister, cache, plugin scratch state; passed to every plugin.
predicate / Filter trait FilterPlugin { fn filter(pod, node, ctx) -> FilterResult }; a hard feasibility check (resource fit, nodeSelector, taint match, affinity). Removes infeasible Nodes.
priority / Score trait ScorePlugin { fn score(pod, node, ctx) -> i64 } // 0..100; ranks feasible Nodes; weighted-summed across plugins (least/most-requested, spread).
bind Binding { pod: ObjectRef, node: String } — the write of spec.node_name; commits a Pod to a Node via the apiserver. Idempotent-once; binding is exclusive.
taint / toleration A Node spec.taints entry repels Pods lacking a matching PodSpec.tolerations entry; used for dedicated nodes + eviction control.
affinity PodSpec.affinity — node/pod (anti-)affinity rules constraining placement relative to node labels or other Pods' locations (co-locate / spread).

Node, kubelet & runtime (orc-kubelet, orc-runtime)

Term Definition
node A machine running orc-kubelet; Node object holds status.capacity/allocatable/conditions, spec.taints. Scheduling target + Pod host.
node lease TTL key the kubelet renews each interval as a liveness heartbeat; missed renewal → Node-lifecycle controller marks NotReady.
NotReady Node condition set when its lease lapses; after a grace period the Node-lifecycle controller evicts the Node's Pods.
PodWorker PodWorker — per-Pod async worker on the kubelet owning that Pod's lifecycle state machine; serializes syncs for one Pod.
PodSyncResult enum { Synced(PodStatus), Backoff(Duration), Terminated } — outcome of one PodWorker sync; drives status write + requeue.
kubelet is sole status writer Only the owning node's kubelet writes pod.status; scheduler/controllers write spec. Single-writer-per-field rule eliminates status races.
ProbeManager trait ProbeManager { async fn probe(&Probe, &ContainerStatus) -> ProbeResult }; runs liveness (restart), readiness (endpoint gating), startup (delay other probes) probes.
CRI Container Runtime Interface — the ContainerRuntime trait abstracting the kubelet↔runtime boundary (sandbox/image/container ops). Models Kubernetes CRI.
ContainerRuntime The CRI-like trait: run_sandbox, pull_image, create_container, start_container, stop_container, status. Implemented by orc-runtime.
sandbox Pod-level isolation primitive (shared network namespace + cgroup parent); run_sandbox creates it before containers; teardown reclaims it.
OCI image Open Container Initiative image (layered tar + config); pull_image→ImageId, unpacked into ImageStore for container rootfs.
runc The runc-like exec engine: launches a container in fresh Linux namespaces with a pivot_rooted rootfs under cgroups v2 limits.
namespaces Linux kernel isolation domains (pid, net, mnt, uts, ipc, user) giving a container its private view; the sandbox shares net/ipc across its containers.
cgroups v2 Unified-hierarchy control groups enforcing CPU/memory/pids/io limits per container via a CgroupHandle; OOM + throttling boundary.

Networking, config & resilience (orc-net, configs, HA)

Term Definition
Service Stable virtual endpoint over a label-selected set of Pods; `spec.type: ClusterIP
ClusterIP The cluster-internal virtual IP (VIP) of a Service; load-balanced by the dataplane to healthy backends. Not externally routable.
EndpointSlice Controller-maintained object listing a Service's current ready backend Pod IPs/ports; the dataplane's input. Sharded for scale.
kube-proxy dataplane ServiceProxy — programs ClusterIP/NodePort VIP → EndpointSlice backend rules (iptables/eBPF-like or userspace), realizing Service load balancing.
CNI Container Network Interface — trait NetworkPlugin { setup_pod_network / teardown_pod_network }; assigns a Pod IP + wires the sandbox into pod networking.
CSI Container Storage Interface — the pluggable volume-provisioning boundary; stubbed in Phase 11 (reference hostPath/emptyDir/projected only).
ConfigMap Non-secret key/value config object; injected as env vars or projected volume files into containers without rebuilding images.
Secret Like ConfigMap but for sensitive data; at-rest considerations + restricted access; injected as env/projected volume.
ServiceAccount Pod identity; its projected token authenticates the Pod to the apiserver; subject of RBAC bindings.
RBAC Role-Based Access Control: Role/ClusterRole (verb×resource rules) + RoleBinding granting them to subjects; the apiserver authorizer. Minimal in Phase 12.
admission trait Admission — post-authz, pre-persist hook validating/mutating objects (defaulting, quota enforcement, webhooks). Last gate before the store.
ResourceQuota Namespace-scoped cap on aggregate resource requests/object counts; enforced at admission; rejects writes that would exceed it.
drain Cordoning a Node + evicting its Pods (respecting PodDisruptionBudgets) for maintenance; new Pods are not scheduled to a cordoned Node.
eviction Controlled deletion of a Pod from a Node (node pressure, taint, drain, lost lease); PDB-aware via the eviction path.
PodDisruptionBudget PodDisruptionBudget — caps voluntary disruptions (min available / max unavailable) for a selected Pod set; drain/eviction must respect it.
split-brain Two components believing they are leader (or two store replicas accepting writes) under partition; prevented by lease-based leader election + single-Revision write serialization (Raft hook, cross-link 42).

orc-scheduler/src/preemption.rs Optional victim selection on infeasible high-priority Pod.
orc-scheduler/tests/filter_score.rs Feasibility + scoring correctness fixtures.
orc-scheduler/tests/bind_exclusive.rs A Pod binds exactly once.

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Section-1 timeline: 14 phases, 78 weeks (~18 months, 1 staff eng equiv). Durations below sum to 78w. Risk = highest-severity failure mode; Exit = measurable gate.

Phase Deliverable Dur Key Risks Exit Criteria
1 (## 12) Bootstrap, object-model shells, and orc-store MVCC KV 9 orc-* crate workspace, shells, all-in-one dev binary, CI; WAL + keyspace, monotonic Revision, range/get/put/delete, Txn CAS, gap-free ordered Watch, Lease TTL, compaction 11w Workspace cycles; CI flake; torn WAL write → corrupt keyspace; watch gap → cache desync orctl apply pod.yaml → POST → persist → GET round-trips Pod + non-empty resourceVersion; CI green Linux+macOS; crash-recovery replays WAL to last durable Revision, 0 lost commits; watch from R streams every mod_rev>R in order, no gap/dup; 10k watch-event fuzz clean
2 (## 13) orc-api object model TypeMeta/ObjectMeta, all kinds, LabelSelector match, JSON codec, ListOptions, OwnerReference, Condition 5w Codec drift vs stored bytes; selector semantics mismatch JSON round-trip = byte-stable; match_expressions (In/NotIn/Exists) honor set semantics; codec fuzz 1M iters no panic; field-selector subset enforced
3 (## 14) orc-apiserver REST generic Registry over store, validation + Admission, namespaces, WATCH (rv + bookmarks), optimistic CC 7w RV↔Revision mapping bug → silent overwrite; watch fan-out OOM Stale-RV update → 409; WATCH delivers ordered events + periodic Bookmark; compacted RV → 410 Gone; 10k concurrent watch streams < 512MB RSS, p99 event-delivery < 50ms
4 (## 15) Controller framework Reflector→DeltaFIFO→SharedIndexInformer→WorkQueue→Reconciler, resync, leader-election hook 6w Edge-triggered leak into reconcile; queue starvation; hot-loop on permanent error Dropped event harmless: post-resync cache == store; reconcile idempotent under N replays; RateLimiter backs off exponentially, forget on success; leader-election hook single-active
5 (## 16) Core controllers ReplicaSet (N replicas, adopt/orphan), GarbageCollector (cascade + finalizers), Node-lifecycle 5w Adoption races → duplicate Pods; GC orphans/over-deletes; finalizer deadlock RS converges to spec.replicas after kill/add; orphaned Pod adopted by selector+controller ownerRef; owner delete cascades children, finalizer blocks until removed; lost lease → NotReady
6 (## 17) orc-scheduler watch unscheduled Pods, Filter predicates (fit/nodeSelector/taints/affinity) + Score (least/most-req, spread), Bind, SchedulingQueue 6w Double-bind; infeasible-Pod busy-loop; score plugin overflow Pod binds exactly once (spec.node_name CAS); infeasible Pod requeued w/ backoff, not spun; throughput ≥ 100 pods/s on 1k-node sim; score weighted-sum bounded 0..100
7 (## 18) orc-kubelet Node register + Lease heartbeat, watch bound Pods, PodWorker state machine, status report, probes 7w Status race (multi-writer); heartbeat miss under load → false NotReady; probe flap Kubelet sole writer of pod.status; missed lease within TTL → controller marks NotReady; liveness/readiness/startup transitions correct; PodSyncResult::Backoff honored
8 (## 19) orc-runtime OCI image pull/unpack + ImageStore, runc-like exec (namespaces, cgroups v2, mounts, pivot_root), sandbox, restart/backoff 8w Namespace/mount leak → host escape; cgroup limit not enforced; image-layer corruption Container isolated (verified ns/cgroup membership); cgroups v2 mem/cpu limits enforced (OOM-kill on breach); CRASH → restart per RestartPolicy w/ exponential backoff; image digest verified on unpack
9 (## 20) Deployment controller manage ReplicaSets, RollingUpdate (maxSurge/maxUnavailable), Recreate, rollout history/status, pause/resume, rollback 5w Surge violates capacity; rollout stuck; rollback to bad revision Rolling update respects maxSurge/maxUnavailable bounds at all times; rollout converges or reports stuck; orctl rollout undo restores prior revision; pause halts progress
10 (## 21) orc-net Service + EndpointSlice controller, kube-proxy-like dataplane (ClusterIP VIP→endpoints, NodePort), DNS, CNI NetworkPlugin 6w Stale endpoints → blackhole; VIP conflict; DNS poisoning VIP load-balances to healthy endpoints only; endpoint churn reflected < 1s; NodePort reachable; cluster DNS resolves Service A/SRV; pod gets IP via setup_pod_network
11 (## 22) Config + storage ConfigMap/Secret injection (env + projected vol), VolumeManager (emptyDir/hostPath/projected), CSI stub, at-rest considerations 4w Secret leak via env/logs; volume mount race; stale projection ConfigMap/Secret update reflected in projected volume; Secrets not logged; emptyDir lifecycle = Pod; hostPath confined; CSI interface stubbed + documented
12 (## 23) Security ServiceAccount + token projection, minimal RBAC authorizer, admission webhooks, ResourceQuota/LimitRange, authn chain 5w Authz bypass; token over-scope; quota race Unauthorized verb → 403; SA token projected + validated; ResourceQuota enforced atomically (no over-admit); LimitRange defaults applied; client-cert/bearer authn at boundary
13 (## 24) Resilience + HA leader election, graceful drain + eviction respecting PDB, taint-based eviction, multi-apiserver over Raft-backed store (cross-link 42) 4w Split-brain dual leader; drain violates PDB; eviction storm Exactly one active leader under partition; drain blocks if eviction breaks PodDisruptionBudget; taint eviction rate-limited; HA store path documented + smoke-tested
14 (## 25) Conformance + e2e + chaos e2e harness (spin cluster + workloads), chaos (kill node/pod/apiserver, partition store), codec/watch fuzz, property tests, scale benchmarks 9w Latent convergence bug under chaos; benchmark regression DONE GATE: e2e green; chaos — cluster reconverges after node/apiserver/store-partition kill, 0 lost writes; property tests pass (reconcile convergence, watch gap-freedom, optimistic-CC safety); scale bench meets targets (1k nodes, 10k Pods, 100 pods/s schedule)

Total: 78w.

Phase 1 done vs full done

Phase 1 done = the Phase 1 (## 12) exit gate only: nine orc-* crates compile in one workspace, the all-in-one dev binary boots orc-apiserver + orc-store, and orctl apply → POST → persist → GET round-trips a Pod with a server-assigned resourceVersion. It proves the skeleton, codec, and store wiring — not reconciliation, scheduling, node execution, networking, or security. Full done = the Phase-14 (## 25) conformance/e2e/chaos gate is green: a real multi-node cluster spins up via the e2e harness, runs Deployments/Services/ConfigMaps end-to-end, survives chaos injection (killed nodes/pods/apiserver and a partitioned orc-store) by reconverging to desired state with zero lost writes, passes all property tests (reconcile convergence, watch gap-freedom, optimistic-concurrency safety, GC correctness), and meets scale benchmarks (≥1k nodes, ≥10k Pods, ≥100 pods/s scheduling). Only when that gate is green is the orchestration core — declarative API + MVCC store + level-triggered controllers + filter/score scheduler + node agent + OCI runtime + Service dataplane — considered complete and eligible for the ✅ tag.
